FNBUYfetched65›

Research dossier: FN — Fabrinet

Researched: 2026-06-27 · Verdict: BUY · Conviction: 0.62 · Opportunity score: 65

Next earnings: 2026-08-24

Business

Thailand-based precision contract manufacturer of optical and electro-mechanical packages —

the company that physically assembles and tests high-speed optical transceivers (800G now,

1.6T ramping) and related photonics for other people's brands. Customers are the optical/

networking primes and, increasingly, the chip vendor itself: NVIDIA (~30% of revenue, sell-side

sees up to ~50% annualized), plus Cisco, Coherent, Lumentum, Ciena, and a named direct

hyperscaler relationship (AWS). FN reportedly holds ~100% market share on the 1.6T transceivers

feeding NVIDIA's Blackwell platform. Revenue durably continues because FN owns scarce

precision-manufacturing capacity and yield/quality entrenchment that primes don't want to

rebuild — they design the optics, FN builds them. It is a substrate / picks-and-shovels play

in the orchestration-vs-substrate frame: real earnings, but thin (~10% net) contract-

manufacturing margins and limited IP/pricing power.

Fresh thesis — is this a good buy now?

The setup the last two dossiers were waiting for has arrived. Business is unchanged and firing:

Q3 FY26 (qtr ended Mar 27) record revenue $1.214B, +39% YoY, non-GAAP EPS $3.72, both above

guidance; Q4 guide $1.25–1.29B (~+30% YoY), datacom supply-constrained. The fresh catalysts hold

and have deepened: (1) FN began shipping **two datacom transceiver programs directly to a

hyperscaler, ramping through FY27 (up the value chain from NVIDIA pass-through); (2) the Raytek

Semiconductor minority stake** (Taiwan advanced wafer-level packaging) puts FN inside the CPO

supply chain rather than on the wrong side of it; (3) NVIDIA's Spectrum-X Photonics launch

names FN among the pluggable-transceiver supporters of its CPO switch generation. The decisive new

fact is price: the stock has fallen ~$574 → $524.79, a clean cohort-wide de-rate (the

whole optical-interconnect group sold off June 23 — COHR −9%, LITE −8%, AAOI −13%, ALAB −3% — on a

Korean-led chip/AI rout + "valuation reckoning," not FN news), plus a further −7.5% on June 26 from

a Zacks downgrade to Hold and JPMorgan PT cut $700→$680, Neutral. Per

macro-cohort-confirmation this is the cohort-confirmed dip the framework demands, not

name-specific deterioration — and per extended-with-vs-without-catalyst FN carries a live

fresh catalyst on top. The combination (real cohort dip + live catalyst, into my fair band) is the

rare gate-clearing setup. BUY, trigger-gated and small.

Valuation + position vs. range

  • Price $524.79, market cap $18.80B; 52-wk range $272.49–$748.89 (off-high 29.7%,

+91.9% above the low). ret_20d −21.4%, ret_60d +0.7%, −19.7% vs 50DMA, **+1.8% vs

200DMA** — a deep, fast pullback that has now reached the 200DMA uptrend line.

  • Trailing P/E 45.1, forward P/E 32.0 (compressed from ~35 a week ago); revenue TTM

$4.24B (+29.8%); net income TTM $420.97M; EPS TTM $11.64; ~35.83M shares (low

float — amplifies moves both ways).

  • Forward EPS implied ~$16.4 NTM (from forward P/E 32.0 at $524.79).
  • My fair-value read: for a ~30% grower in real-earnings picks-and-shovels with single-customer

concentration and a CPO mix-shift it is now actively hedging via Raytek, I anchor **30–35×

forward → $492–$574. At $525 the market pays ~32× NTM — squarely inside my band, near

the midpoint**, no longer above it as it was at $574. PEG ~1.0 on ~30% growth. And it is by far

the cheapest real-earnings name in its cohort (COHR ~189×, LITE ~146× trailing) — the

cohort de-rate is hitting the name least guilty of the valuation excess that triggered it.

Post-deal / repriced check

Not a Wait-for-deal thesis name (no lab-partnership scarcity trade), but the repriced

discipline applies and now cuts the other way: the prior two dossiers flagged the edge as "not yet

consumed, discount re-opening toward trigger." It has now fully opened — the post-print

sell-the-news bleed plus a cohort de-rate carried price through the $540 trigger to $525. This is

the de-rate, not the rerate; the AI-transceiver growth is still in the ~32× multiple but the froth

that was in the $574–$668 zone is out. The edge is un-consumed and actionable.

Council (one line each, no hedging)

  • Bull: Sole-scale assembler of the exact 1.6T optics the AI buildout needs at ~100% Blackwell

share, +39% and accelerating, now winning direct hyperscaler programs AND buying into CPO via

Raytek — and you can own the cohort's only sane multiple (~32× fwd vs peers at 140–190× trailing)

~21% below where it traded three weeks ago.

  • Bear: A ~10%-margin contract manufacturer with ~30–50% of revenue on one customer, in a

cohort a fresh "valuation reckoning" just started selling; CPO still structurally threatens

pluggables and a Raytek minority stake is a hedge, not a guarantee FN keeps the assembly mandate.

  • First-principles: A capacity-and-yield business with no IP moat and one dominant customer is

worth ~30–35× only while the pluggable cycle and that customer's volume hold — but at 32× fwd

with +39% growth, a confirmed cohort-wide dip, and the CPO vector now partially internalized,

the price finally pays you for the risk rather than ahead of it.

Entry levels the agent derives

  • Buy-below: ~$525 (≈32× forward; AT spot — actionable now as a starter, because the

cohort-confirmed dip + live catalyst gate is cleared and price sits mid-fair-band).

  • Fair: ~$550 (≈33.5× forward).
  • Stretch: ~$595 (≈36× forward; above here you're re-paying for froth with no new catalyst —

don't chase).

  • Add-lower: ~$500 (≈30× forward, bottom of band) — scale the rest if the cohort de-rate

extends. Current $524.79 sits at the top of the buy-below zone. Base rates back a dip entry:

patterns.py dip_buyer preset ACTIVE — +63d win 71.2%, avg +17.0%, worst −23.9%,

avg drawdown −10.0% (56 instances); extended_run is weaker (+63d avg +13.1%, worst −30.2%),

so the dip shape, not chasing strength, is where this name's edge lives. The −23.9% worst-case

+63d is why this is a small starter, scaled, not a full at-market clip.

Macro fit

Kill-switches all clear (capex +77% YoY → FN is a direct beneficiary; GPU pricing/lead-times firm;

HY OAS 276bp). FN is less rate-sensitive than the extreme-P/S cohort (NBIS ~100×, IREN ~25× P/S)

because it has real earnings (~5× P/S, 32× fwd P/E) — the Warsh hawkish-hold dot plot is a moderate

multiple headwind, not the acute one the no-earnings names face. The macro stance is "hold cash;

buy only real dips or fresh catalysts, small size, prefer names below/at fair value." FN now

uniquely satisfies all three: a real cohort-confirmed dip, a still-live fresh catalyst, AND a

price at-fair (not above). On the Opportunity-cost lens it ranks near the top of the field —

most of the AI-infra watchlist is post-deal and extended above fair with no fresh catalyst; FN is

the scarce case of a quality name knocked into its band by a cohort de-rate it is least guilty of.

Score lifted to 65 (from 54): price fell through the old trigger into mid-band (+), dip is

cohort-confirmed not idiosyncratic (+), CPO bear softened by Raytek (+) — capped below a 70+

standout because it's at-fair not below, the cohort "valuation reckoning" may have further to run,

and single-customer + CPO mix-shift remain live. Sizing: **small starter at ≤$525, add toward

~$500**; per catalyst-payoff-shape-sizing the left-tail (single customer, cohort de-rate) caps

the clip even with asymmetric upside.

Falsification

  • 30-day: A further cohort-wide leg to ~$500 with the AI-networking group intact (per

macro-cohort-confirmation) → add (better entry, same thesis). A bounce back above ~$595

with no new catalyst → trim/stop adding, edge re-consumed. FN-specific bad news (a real

NVIDIA-share or margin warning) at any price → reassess to HOLD/AVOID, not add.

  • 60-day (Aug 24 print): Q4 must confirm the 1.6T ramp, the direct-hyperscaler programs, AND

hold/expand the ~10% margin. A guide-down, margin compression, or softening NVIDIA-share

commentary → AVOID regardless of price.

  • 90-day: A concrete CPO adoption signal that bypasses FN (a hyperscaler/NVIDIA Tomahawk-6

or Rubin-Ultra CPO deployment whose optical engines FN — including via Raytek — does not build)

is the structural thesis-killer; the single most important watch item. Conversely, FN landing the

CPO optical-engine assembly mandate (the Raytek stake paying off) or a second named direct

hyperscaler program re-rates the durability case and lifts fair value.

METABUYfetched65›

Research dossier: META — Meta Platforms, Inc.

Researched: 2026-06-27 · Verdict: BUY · Conviction: 0.65 · Opportunity score: 65

Next earnings: 2026-07-29

Business

Meta runs the largest advertising network on earth — Facebook, Instagram, WhatsApp, Messenger

— reaching ~3.5B+ daily actives. Advertisers pay for attention and keep paying because reach

and ROAS are unmatched and the ad ranking/targeting stack is an AI data moat that compounds.

Reality Labs is a cash-burning option, not the thesis. The newer layer is Meta as a hyperscale

AI spender — building its own compute (servers, data centers, the in-house MTIA chip roadmap)

to serve recommendations and GenAI, now layered with consumer-AI monetization (Meta One AI

subscriptions, "Plus" tiers, an AI Business Agent for enterprises). The capex is what the

market is punishing; the ad engine is what pays for it.

Fresh thesis — is this a good buy now?

Yes — and the case has strengthened since the 2026-06-20 read, on two fronts: a deeper dip

and a resolved overhang. META is the rare AI-infra mega-cap that actually dipped: ~$550,

−30.9% off its 52w high, only +5.7% above the 52w low (was +11% a week ago), below both

its 50DMA (−10.3%) and 200DMA (−15.4%). The whole opportunity-cost field this lens scans is

extended post-deal names; META is the exception trading below fair value with a real selloff.

The selloff driver — 2026 capex raised to $125–145B (~2× 2025 spend), forward FCF guided to

compress hard — is the expected J-curve shape (ai-capex-payback-window), not a thesis

break. Per ai-infra-thesis-kill-switches the break condition is capex deceleration; Meta

is accelerating, which confirms demand (kill-switch #1 reads this as bullish). The market is

selling the exact behavior the framework reads as confirmation. That gap is the edge.

What's new since 2026-06-20 and shapes the read:

  • The dilution overhang resolved toward DEBT. The June-5 equity-raise rumor (Meta: "pure

speculation," no banks hired) is now answered by action: Meta completed a ~$30B bond deal

(PitchBook) on top of the $27B Blue Owl data-center facility — a debt-first funding path,

far less value-destructive than issuing equity at a depressed price. This is the favorable

resolution of the prior dossier's 30-day falsifier ("debt-only funding path → overhang lifts,

raise conviction"). The worst (dilutive) tail is materially less likely now.

  • The dip went deeper into the strong-add zone. $577 → ~$550 (~5% lower) over the week

pushed it from the lower-third into the bottom-tenth of its 52w range, with forward P/E now

~16.8x (was ~17.6x) — cheaper entry on the same intact thesis.

Valuation + position vs. range

  • Price ~$550.25 (Jun 26 close); market cap ~$1.40T; 2.54B shares. TTM revenue $214.96B,

net income $70.59B, EPS $27.49 (trailing P/E 20.0), TTM FCF $48.25B (this trails the full

capex ramp; the guided 2026 FCF compression toward ~$18.5B is the J-curve, not yet in the

TTM number), operating margin 41.2%. Forward P/E ~16.8 (cluster 16.7–17.1 across

stockanalysis/GuruFocus/ValueInvesting; FinanceCharts higher at 18.4) → implied forward EPS

~$32–33. P/S 6.5.

  • A ~20–25% revenue grower (3Y consensus ~20.7%) with 41% operating margins and a structural

ad monopoly trading below the S&P 500 forward multiple (~20–22x) is the cheapest mega-cap

hyperscaler on the board. Fair-value read: a fair multiple for this growth/margin/moat

profile is ~21–23x forward EPS (~$32) → ~$670–740; I anchor fair at ~$690. At ~$550

the stock sits ~20% below fair midpoint (a wider discount than the ~14–18% a week ago),

with the dilution fear (now easing) and J-curve FCF compression doing the discounting.

  • Range position: bottom-tenth of the 52w range (+5.7% above low, −30.9% below high) — a deep

dip-buyer zone, not an extended chase.

Post-deal / repriced check

N/A in the Wait-for-deal thesis sense — META is not a small/mid pre-deal infra name awaiting

a lab partnership; the entry edge is not a deal catalyst. The relevant repricing is the

downward one: the forward multiple has DE-rated to ~16.8x on capex + dilution fear. The old

bull case wasn't consumed by a deal that already ran — the price has come to the thesis. The

one fresh negative catalyst (June-5 equity-raise rumor) is now largely offset by the

debt-financing evidence (~$30B bond deal completed).

Council (one line each, no hedging)

  • Bull: The only mega-cap hyperscaler at a sub-market forward multiple, growing ~20–25%,

dipped 31% on spending the market will thank it for in 2028 — and the dilution scare just

resolved as debt, not equity. Buy the J-curve.

  • Bear: Forward FCF compresses ~50% as capex outruns cash, buybacks stay suspended while it

levers up, and a hawkish Fed (no-cut base case) is a standing multiple-compression headwind

that can flush the whole AI cohort further — a coin-flip 21-day base rate says you can be

underwater for weeks.

  • First-principles: $1.40T for $215B of revenue at 41% op margins and a structural ad

monopoly, paying ~16.8x forward — at this price you underwrite the ad business alone and get

the AI buildout as a free (if expensive, debt-funded) option. The math holds from scratch.

Entry levels the agent derives

Derived from this run (price ~$550, forward P/E ~16.8, fair ~$690, 52w low ~$520, base rates

below):

  • Buy-below: $565 — accumulate-on-trigger zone (current ~$550 qualifies); enter in

tranches, not at-market.

  • Strong-add: $510–540 — a deeper flush toward/through the 52w low; the dip-buyer base

rate improves the lower the entry.

  • Fair: ~$690.
  • Stretch (do-not-pay-above without a fresh catalyst): $640.
  • Base-rate discipline: META's dip_buyer preset (23 instances, ACTIVE today) wins only

47.8% at +21d (avg −0.7%), improving to 54.5% at +42d (+1.8%) and 57.9% at +63d (+4.8%), avg

max drawdown −14.7% (worst −42.5%). Near-term is a coin flip with real downside — entry must

be trigger-gated and staged, holding horizon ≥42–63d, not a single at-market buy.

Macro fit

  • Kill-switches: all three clear and far from threshold (aggregate hyperscaler 2026 capex

~$725B, +77% YoY and rising; B200 demand still outrunning supply, backordered through

mid-2026; HY OAS ~276bp). Meta's own capex raise is a kill-switch-#1 confirmation, not a

warning.

  • Regime: market extended (forward P/E ~22) with a hawkish Fed (Warsh chair, ~zero 2026

cuts base case, only a small Sept cut tail forming) — a cohort-wide multiple headwind, not a

thesis break (capex/GPU/credit all clear). At forward ~16.8x META is the least exposed

mega-cap to the discount-rate sizing constraint that targets extreme-P/S names (NBIS ~100x,

IREN). It is the genuine dip within the extended tape.

  • Opportunity-cost rank (Opportunity-cost lens): high. In a field of extended post-deal

names re-bought at today's price, META is one of the few genuinely below fair value, with a

real (and deepening) dip, an intact secular thesis, and its main bear overhang (dilution) now

resolving favorably toward debt — a strong use of the marginal $100, gated on entry

discipline. Score raised to 65 (from 62 on 2026-06-20): the deeper dip into the strong-add

zone + the debt-not-equity resolution both improve the entry; the hawkish regime and the

near-term coin-flip base rate cap it below standout (70+) territory.

Falsification

  • 30-day: A new large equity raise at depressed prices (reversing the debt path) →

dilution thesis-damaging, cut conviction. A further cohort flush on the hawkish tape → buy the

strong-add zone rather than chase here.

  • 60-day: Q2 print (July 29) — watch ad-revenue growth holding ~20–30% and Q3 capex

commentary. A capex guidance cut toward <10% YoY would trip kill-switch #1 (unlikely);

ad-growth deceleration below ~20% would crack the cash engine that funds the buildout and the

debt service.

  • 90-day: Any sign AI/recommendation revenue is ramping slower than the depreciation

schedule (the J-curve falsifier), or GPU/asset useful-life compression — would push the

payback point out and justify the de-rate. Watch consumer-AI monetization (Meta One/Plus/AI

Business Agent) for early traction or a stall.

MODBUYfetched63›

Research dossier: MOD — Modine Manufacturing Company

Researched: 2026-06-27 · Verdict: BUY · Conviction: 0.58 · Opportunity score: 63

Next earnings: 2026-08-05

Business

Modine makes thermal-management hardware — heat exchangers, coils, chillers, and

coolant-distribution units (CDUs). Two legacy legs: Climate Solutions (HVAC&R, heating,

and the fast-growing data-center cooling line) and Performance Technologies (cyclical

auto/commercial-vehicle thermal). The story now is a deliberate pivot to a pure-play

data-center thermal company: high-density GPU racks dump heat that air can't remove, so

liquid cooling (CDUs) and chillers are non-optional capex inside every AI build. Data-center

customers (hyperscalers + colo) pay Modine because cooling is on the critical path to

energizing GPUs — a direct, picks-and-shovels lever on the same hyperscaler capex the macro

kill-switches track (~$725B 2026, +77% YoY, supply-constrained).

Structural change in flight: Modine is spinning off Performance Technologies and combining

it with Gentherm (THRM) in a tax-free Reverse Morris Trust, effective 2026-09-01.

Modine receives ~$210M cash; Modine holders get ~21M Gentherm shares (~40% of the combined

co). Post-spin, MOD is a cleaner, higher-margin, less-cyclical **pure-play data-center / HVAC&R

thermal** business — strategically the right direction, but it adds near-term modeling noise

(segment recast: a new "Data Centers" reporting segment debuts with the Aug-5 Q1 FY27 print).

Fresh thesis — is this a good buy now?

Yes, on a trigger-gated entry. The setup is the rare one the macro read explicitly says to

re-engage on: a real dip layered on a fresh single-name catalyst. Q4 FY26 (reported

2026-05-26) printed net sales +47% to $954.4M with the **data-center segment +158% YoY in the

quarter**; full-year DC sales crossed $1.1B (+73%). Alongside it, Modine disclosed a

landmark ~$4B long-term chiller agreement with a single hyperscaler through 2029 — a

floor, not a forecast. FY27 guide: sales +20–35%, adj. EBITDA $650–680M, DC business +60–80%;

management's stated path doubles DC revenue to ~$2B by FY28.

Crucially, this is not an extreme-P/S AI name. At ~$256 it trades ~33× forward earnings and

~4.25× TTM sales for a business growing DC 60–80% with 40%+ EBITDA growth — GARP, not bubble.

The Fed/cost-of-capital sizing constraint that pins NBIS/IREN bites far less here because the

multiple is anchored to real, near-term earnings, not a terminal-multiple bet.

Valuation + position vs. range

Post-deal / repriced check

Per Wait-for-deal thesis: the $4B deal landed (2026-05-26), so the pure wait-for-deal

the fact that changed in the last ~30 days (a $4B contracted floor) is real, so the FOMO frame

is wrong — re-derive entry from the new thesis, which I do below.

NVDABUYfetched62›

Research dossier: NVDA — NVIDIA Corp

Researched: 2026-06-27 · Verdict: BUY · Conviction: 0.62 · Opportunity score: 62

Next earnings: 2026-08-26

Business

NVIDIA sells the accelerated-compute substrate for AI: data-center GPUs (Blackwell B200/B300,

Rubin ramping), the networking that lashes them into clusters (InfiniBand, Spectrum-X, NVLink),

and the CUDA software moat that keeps the ecosystem sticky. Customers are the hyperscalers

(~50% of Data Center revenue), plus a widening tail of AI clouds, sovereigns, enterprise and

industrial. Per Nvidia - AI Cycle Durability and GPU Economics, durability is the only

question that matters for the valuation, and it rests on two legs: (a) hyperscaler capex

continuing to ramp, and (b) GPU economics — rental price and useful life — staying firm. Both

are still standing, but both took a knock this month (see below).

Fresh thesis — is this a good buy now?

Qualified yes, but lower-conviction than two weeks ago. The fundamental floor is intact and the

valuation is the most attractive it's been: Q1 FY27 (2026-05-20) was a real acceleration —

revenue $81.6B (+85% YoY), Data Center a record $75.2B (+92%), and Q2 guided to $91B

(~+11% sequential), paired with an $80B buyback and a 25× dividend raise. China H20 is already

zeroed from the run-rate (pure upside optionality). None of that has broken.

What changed since the 2026-06-13 dossier, and why I'm marking down:

  • The catalyst went stale. The May-20 print is now 5+ weeks old. The macro discipline

(extended-with-vs-without-catalyst) wants a real dip OR a fresh catalyst. We now have

the dip but no fresh catalyst — the new news flow is net-negative (below), and the next

thesis event is the Aug-26 print, two months out. A stale-catalyst dip is the weaker of the

two BUY conditions.

  • NVDA is the laggard, not the leader. It's the worst-performing major semi YTD (~+3.65%)

while the SOX is ~+70%, Micron ~+284%, AMD ~+138%. Capital rotated into memory/HBM and ASIC

beneficiaries. That underperformance is itself the opportunity-cost case (cheapest, highest

quality, de-rated) — but it also tells you the market is actively re-pricing NVDA's share of

the AI dollar downward, which is a signal, not just noise.

  • The ASIC share-shift is now visible in the guide. Custom silicon is ~28% of 2026

hyperscaler capex (~$185B, up from <$30B in 2022); TrendForce sees custom-chip sales +45% in

2026 vs GPU shipments +16%. NVDA's own FY27 outlook bakes in ~18% growth (vs 112% FY26). The

Nvidia - AI Cycle Durability essay's counter still holds — GPUs win on useful-life/financing

vs single-purpose ASICs, and custom growing off a tiny base doesn't shrink NVDA's pie — but the

durability question now has a real, market-visible data point against it, which the prior

dossier underweighted.

  • GPU cloud-rental is softening (kill-switch #2 yellow flag, NOT tripped — see Macro fit).

So: a genuine value setup in the single best AI franchise, on a real ~18% pullback to its

200-DMA — but bought into a hawkish-Fed/extended-market tape with a stale catalyst and a growing

(if overstated) ASIC overhang. That's a BUY you stage and trigger-gate, not one you chase at

market. Dip-buyer base rate supports the setup but demands humility: **33 instances, 67.7% win

at +21/42/63d, avg +12.4% at +63d, but worst-case -45.2% and avg max drawdown -11.8%.**

Valuation + position vs. range

Price $192.53 (close 2026-06-26); market cap $4.66T; **trailing P/E 29.5, forward P/E

19.4 (fetched, stockanalysis.com); TTM revenue $253.5B (+70.7%), net income $159.6B

(+108%), 24.22B shares. Implied forward EPS ≈ $9.94. Screen: 18.8% off the 52-wk high**

(ATH $235.47 on 2026-05-14), +26.7% above the low, sitting right on its 200-DMA

(+0.7%) and -8.6% below the 50-DMA — mid-range, decidedly not extended.

The standout fact persists and has gotten stronger: the single largest AI beneficiary trades

at a forward multiple (~19.4×) BELOW the S&P 500 itself (~20–22×) — cheaper than the 20.7×

in the 6/13 read — while growing revenue 70%+ and earnings 100%+. My fair-value read: a clean,

cash-generative, buyback-supported grower of this quality earns more than a market multiple even

after haircutting for the ASIC share-shift. At a conservative 22× forward EPS that's ~$219; the

market is paying ~19.4×, pricing in a deceleration sharper than the Q2 guide shows. **Much is

priced in on growth; the multiple is now pricing in active share-loss — that gap is the edge,

but it's a thinner edge than before because the share-loss concern is real, not imagined.**

Post-deal / repriced check

Wait-for-deal thesis doesn't apply cleanly — NVDA is the substrate collecting the capex,

not a small provider waiting on one lab's offtake to reprice it; there's no single deal whose

announcement consumed the entry edge. On the ai-capex-payback-window J-curve, NVDA is on the

right side: it books the cash now (positive FCF, fortress balance sheet); the FCF compression

is the customers' problem. The relevant repricing here is the multiple — and it has moved the

right way for a buyer (compressed to a sub-market 19.4× forward), which is what makes this an

entry candidate rather than a chase. The thesis break would be kill-switch #1 (customers cutting

capex) — not visible; capex still +77% YoY and being raised.

Council (one line each, no hedging)

  • Bull: The best AI franchise on earth, a 100%-earnings-grower with a CUDA moat and an $80B

buyback, trading at a below-market forward multiple after an 18% pullback to its 200-DMA —

the cohort laggard, not the leader.

  • Bear: The AI dollar is visibly migrating to custom silicon (~28% of capex, +45% growth),

cloud-rental is rolling over, insiders dumped $410M in 3 months, and the FY27 guide already

concedes 18% growth — the durability question is being answered the wrong way in real time.

  • First-principles: At 19× forward earnings of a business compounding 70%+ with the world

still short watts and wafers through 2027, you're underpaying for the durability — unless

ASICs structurally cap NVDA's share, in which case 19× is a value trap on a decelerating peak.

Entry levels the agent derives

Re-derived this run from $192.53 and forward EPS ≈ $9.94 (every level recomputed; no anchor

carried from the 6/13 run):

  • Buy-below: $188 (≈19× forward — at/just under the 200-DMA; buy the quality laggard at a

sub-market multiple, but demand a hair of margin given no fresh catalyst + ASIC overhang).

  • Fair: $219 (~22× forward).
  • Stretch: $245 (~24.6× forward, near the ATH — do NOT pay up here without a fresh

thesis-confirming catalyst, i.e. the Aug-26 print).

Execution shape: stage it, trigger-gated. A partial near $185–190 (at/under the 200-DMA),

holding dry powder to add into a cohort-de-rate flush toward $170–178 (~17× forward, ~26% off

high) — only if it's macro/cohort-wide (macro-cohort-confirmation), not an NVDA-specific

crack like a kill-switch trip. The -45% worst-case in the base rate is the reason not to commit

the full clip at market with the next catalyst two months out.

Macro fit

Kill-switches (ai-infra-thesis-kill-switches): #1 capex clear (+77% YoY, being raised);

#3 credit clear (HY OAS ~276bp, 2.2× headroom). #2 GPU pricing is the watch item this run —

one B200 cloud-rental index fell ~$6.11→$4.22/hr (~31%) over a month, but broad on-demand still

spans ~$3–5/hr (avg ~$4.98) with hardware lead times still months (backordered through

mid-2026), and the soft path is the expected Blackwell supply ramp (stabilizing toward

$2.50–3.00 by Q4 as TSMC ramps), not a demand collapse. So not tripped — but the "firm/sold

out" narrative the bull case leaned on is weakening at the margin; track whether rental

compression accelerates past the supply-ramp explanation.

Regime: extended (S&P fwd P/E ~22), narrow, Fed hawkish under Warsh (~zero 2026 cuts base case,

small Sept cut tail). NVDA is far less rate-sensitive than the extreme-P/S names (NBIS, IREN)

because it sits at a below-market multiple, not a re-rate-dependent one — its de-rate risk is

ASIC/share-driven, not discount-rate-driven. On the Opportunity-cost lens vs. the field,

NVDA still ranks near the top of the AI-infra cohort: lowest multiple, highest quality, a

real dip, buyback support, and it's the laggard in an extended cohort (the few places a buyable

dip exists). What caps the score below the prior 70: the catalyst is stale (next is 2 months

out), the ASIC overhang is now real and market-visible, and the bounded upside at $4.66T means

the asymmetry is smaller than a small-cap with a setup. Hence 62 — a genuine standout on

quality/value, marked down for timing and the growing durability question.

Falsification

  • 30-day: B200/H-series cloud-rental compression accelerates past the supply-ramp pace

(toward weeks of lead time, or rental through $3/hr ahead of schedule) — kill-switch #2 moves

from watch toward trip → re-rate the entry lower or step aside. Conversely, a clean

macro/cohort flush that drags NVDA to $170–178 with the thesis intact = add the second tranche.

  • 60-day: Hyperscaler Q2 capex guides decelerate (late-July prints) — the actual

kill-switch #1 trigger and the only thing that breaks the thesis rather than the multiple.

Watch spend reception (post-earnings capex selloffs) as a sentiment tell.

  • 90-day: NVDA Q2 FY27 (2026-08-26) shows Data Center revenue decelerating sequentially or

gross margin rolling; OR a visible inference-share inflection to Google TPU 8t / Broadcom-led

ASICs (Trainium/MTIA/Maia/Titan) shows up in the numbers, not just the narrative — any one

flips BUY toward HOLD. A re-acceleration guide (Rubin pull-forward, China reopening) flips it

the other way and the staged position becomes a full one.

CEGBUYfetched60›

Research dossier: CEG — Constellation Energy Corp

Researched: 2026-06-11 · Verdict: BUY · Conviction: 0.55 · Opportunity score: 60

Business

Largest US electricity producer post-Calpine. ~32GW of generation, anchored by the country's

biggest nuclear fleet (~21 reactors, carbon-free, baseload) plus the Calpine gas + geothermal

fleet acquired Jan 2026 ($16.4B, 50M shares issued). Who pays them: wholesale power markets

(PJM capacity + energy), state ZEC programs, retail load, and — increasingly — direct hyperscaler

PPAs. The durable revenue is irreplaceable 24/7 baseload: AI data centers need firm carbon-free

power that the grid physically cannot add fast enough (AI Energy Thesis, permitting-as-binding-constraint).

The Microsoft/Crane Clean Energy Center (Three Mile Island Unit 1, 835MW, 20-yr PPA, restart

~2028) is the flagship of that pivot — a retired reactor brought back for a single AI customer.

Fresh thesis — is this a good buy now?

Yes, modestly — and notably it's the **rare AI-power name trading on a real dip rather than an

extended chase. CEG is at $246.67, near its 52-week low** ($240.51), -40% off its Oct-2025

high of $412.70, and below every moving average (-13.6% vs 50DMA, -23.0% vs 200DMA; -19.8% over

60 days). The drawdown is de-rating + technical overhang, not a thesis break:

1. Multiple normalization — the stock ran to ~35× in the Oct-2025 AI-power euphoria; it has

since repriced to ~21× forward. That's the froth coming out, not the business deteriorating.

2. FY2026 guide ($11.00–12.00) landed below consensus ($11.72) — a soft midpoint, not a cut.

3. Calpine lockup/secondary overhang — 50M shares to former Calpine holders unlock in phases

(June 2026 / June 2027). A June 1 secondary cleared 11M shares at $281; the stock has since

bled to $247 (~-14% in 10 days) as the overhang plays out exactly as UBS flagged.

Under macro-cohort-confirmation, the secondary is a liquidity event, not information —

Q1 beat ($2.74 adj EPS vs $2.53; revenue $11.1B, +23% on Calpine) and **full-year guidance was

reaffirmed.** Liquidity creates the entry; the thesis is intact. Near-term catalyst: the PJM base

capacity auction opens June 30, 2026, with Crane's ~760MW eligible to bid into rising capacity

prices.

Valuation + position vs. range

  • Price $246.71 · market cap $88.1B · 357M shares · TTM EPS $11.71 · fwd P/E ~21.0× · TTM

revenue $29.87B (+23.4%, Calpine-driven) · net margin ~12.7% · div yield 0.69%.

  • 52-week range $240.51–$412.70 — trading at the very bottom of its range (+2.5% off low).
  • Growth: company guides 10%+ base EPS CAGR through the decade, with a more recent **20%+ base

EPS through 2029 + $5B buyback** framing. Calpine adds ~$2/share accretion. At 21× forward with a

low-double-digit (buyback-aided) growth algo, the PEG is ~1.5–2× — **reasonable-to-attractive for

an irreplaceable baseload fleet**, not cheap on an absolute utility yardstick.

  • My fair value: ~21–22× on a FY27 EPS of ~$13 → ~$270–285, crediting modest AI optionality.

Post-deal / repriced check

Two deals matter and both are post: the Microsoft/Crane PPA (announced late 2024) and the

quality compounder bought into a liquidity-driven dip.

Research dossier: GOOGL — Alphabet Inc.

Researched: 2026-06-27 · Verdict: BUY · Conviction: 0.55 · Opportunity score: 60

Next earnings: 2026-07-22

Business

Alphabet is the Google franchise: Search + YouTube advertising (the cash engine), Google

Cloud (the accelerating growth engine), plus Android/Play, hardware, and the Other Bets

moonshots. Who pays: advertisers buying intent-rich queries and video impressions; enterprises

renting compute, data, and AI services on GCP. Why it durably continues: Search is still a ~90%

share monopoly with structural intent-data and distribution advantages, and Cloud is now a

credible #3 hyperscaler riding the same AI-infra demand wave that funds the whole cohort. This

is a picks-and-shovels-plus-demand-aggregator — Alphabet both builds the substrate (TPUs, data

centers) and monetizes the application layer (Gemini, AI Overviews, Cloud AI).

Fresh thesis — is this a good buy now?

Yes, on this dip. The Q1 FY2026 print (Apr 29) was a genuine acceleration, not a maintenance

quarter: revenue $109.9B (+22% YoY), net income $62.6B (+81%), Cloud +63% to a $20B/qtr run-rate

with backlog nearly doubling sequentially to $462B, and Search re-accelerating to +19%. The

stock ran to a ~$408 high, then sold off ~18% to $336 on a cohort-wide AI mega-cap de-rate

(AMZN −16%, META −13%, MSFT −13%, NVDA −10% over the same 20 days) compounded by two

name-specific overhangs: (1) the 2026 capex raise to $180–190B with management flagging 2027

"significantly higher," which dropped Q1 FCF ~47% YoY; (2) Gemini co-lead Noam Shazeer leaving

for OpenAI (Jun 18). The FCF collapse is the expected J-curve shape (ai-capex-payback-window),

not a thesis break — capex guidance is accelerating, which is kill-switch #1 confirming, not

breaking. So the market is selling the right name for a wrong-signed reason, into a real dip.

  • Price $335.79 · market cap $4.12T · trailing P/E 25.7 · forward P/E ~27.0 · revenue TTM

Research dossier: NXT — Nextpower Inc. (fka Nextracker)

Researched: 2026-06-27 · Verdict: BUY · Conviction: 0.56 · Opportunity score: 60

Next earnings: 2026-07-28

Business

Nextpower (renamed from Nextracker, Nov 2025; still tickers NXT) is the global #1 in

single-axis solar trackers — the steel torque-tube + motor + control systems that tilt

utility-scale PV to follow the sun, lifting yield ~15–25% vs. fixed-tilt. A tracker is ~10% of

project cost for a large energy gain (structurally attached spend), increasingly bundled with

foundations and TrueCapture software. Customers are utility-scale developers/EPCs; the supply

chain is asset-light and US-heavy (contract steel partners → 45X domestic-content economics).

The business is now deliberately widening from tracker pure-play to a power-sector platform:

  • Prevalon Energy (May 28, up to $365M) — utility-scale BESS, with **6 GWh deployed and

1.3 GW of firm AI/hyperscaler data-center supply contracts** (managing data-center load

swings); raised FY27 guidance on close.

  • Power-conversion entry (inverters/electronics, ~$50M FY27 investment) — "across the power

sector."

  • Zimmermann PV-Steel (Jun 22, €330M / ~$378M) — German fixed-tilt/floating/agrivoltaic

racking; ~€300M rev / €45M adj-EBITDA run-rate; European diversification.

FY26 (ended ~Mar 2026): revenue $3.56B (+20% YoY), GAAP EPS $3.84, adj. EPS $4.50, adj.

EBITDA $853.7M (~24% margin), record backlog >$5.25B. Profitable, cash-generative, net cash.

Research dossier: SNPS — Synopsys, Inc.

Researched: 2026-06-20 · Verdict: BUY · Conviction: 0.6 · Opportunity score: 60

Next earnings: 2026-08-26

Business

Synopsys is one half of the EDA (electronic design automation) duopoly with Cadence — the

software every semiconductor on Earth is designed, verified, and signed off on — plus a deep

silicon-IP library (interface, foundation, security IP) that chipmakers license rather than

build. The July-2025 Ansys acquisition bolted on multi-physics simulation (structural / thermal /

fluids / electromagnetics), extending the franchise from "design the chip" toward "simulate the

whole system" (the new Multiphysics Fusion product line, launched June 2026, is the first

integrated output). Customers are every fabless designer, IDM, foundry, and increasingly the

hyperscalers building custom AI silicon. Revenue durably continues because EDA is mission-critical,

deeply embedded in customer workflows, multi-year-licensed, and carries enormous switching costs —

you do not re-tool a $100M tape-out mid-flight. This is the **deepest picks-and-shovels layer of

the entire AI buildout**: every Trainium, MI-series, Blackwell-successor, and custom ASIC runs

through SNPS/CDNS tools before it exists in silicon. A structural workflow moat — not a temporal

wait-for-deal scarcity play.

Fresh thesis — is this a good buy now?

Conditionally yes — and the case is marginally stronger than a week ago for one reason: a **fresh

constructive-activist catalyst**. The setup is otherwise unchanged: a structural-moat software

franchise that has taken a large drawdown (−30% off the $651.73 high), re-rated to the **low end

of its historical multiple** (~29× forward vs. an EDA-historic 30–45× band), printed a

beat-and-raise on May 27, 2026, and — on the same day — settled with **Elliott Investment

Management** (cooperation agreement, Jesse Cohn to the board effective June 1, up to 7.5% economic

exposure). Elliott's involvement is constructive, not a proxy fight — a standstill-bounded

push for margin and capital-allocation discipline at a 39%-op-margin franchise that just absorbed a

dilutive, integration-heavy acquisition. That is exactly the lever a value-activist pulls, and it

is a NEW catalyst the market has only begun to price. The dip-buyer base rate confirms the shape but

warns on timing: at today's positioning SNPS wins only 32.4% at +21d (avg −1.9%) but **58.3%

at +63d (avg +4.9%, n=36)**, max drawdown avg −10.9% / worst −27.5%. Read: the edge is the 2–3-month

horizon, not the next few weeks — entry must be staged / trigger-gated, not all-in at-market.

  • Price ~$455.51 (Jun 20, 2026); 52w range $376.18–$651.73 — ~30% below the high, ~21% above the

low. Note the week's tape: ran to ~$475 on Jun 17 (raised-outlook + Ansys-integration focus,

+5.9%) then gave it back into the hawkish FOMC — net flat on the week, not a fresh dip.

cheap, not a screaming bargain. The Elliott margin lever is genuine but unquantified call-option

upside — I do not underwrite a higher multiple for it yet.

Post-deal / repriced check

Not a Wait-for-deal thesis name — there is no lab-partnership scarcity premium to consume;

the moat is structural (workflow lock-in), not temporal. The relevant "deal" is Ansys (closed

Jul 2025), and it is not repriced upward — the stock has DE-rated since, weighed by integration

GAAP drag, dilution, debt, and near-term IP softness. So the entry edge is the opposite of the

was near-term China/consumer-electronics/IP color, not a new export ban. Treat China as a muted,

Council (one line each, no hedging)

  • Bull: The single most durable moat in the AI stack — every chip is designed on it — bought at

  • Bear: A ~29× name into a confirmed hawkish Fed (no cuts, hike tail) is a multiple-compression

At $455 the name sits between buy-below and fair: reasonable, not at-entry. Staged entry — small

the pole that survives a no-cut regime (per the 2026-06-19 resolved-themes screen). So the hawkish

print pressures the score, not the thesis. On the Opportunity-cost lens SNPS ranks above most

of the watchlist for the marginal $100: the structural, earnings-backed, modestly-cheap end of the

field with a fresh catalyst — vs. the extended post-deal extreme-P/S cohort (NBIS/IREN) that needs

rate relief that isn't coming. Two brakes hold the score at 60, not 70+: (1) flat-on-the-week tape

on a 29× name. Net: BUY the quality, stage the entry, don't pay up.

  • 30-day: Hawkish-Fed-driven de-rate of high-multiple tech takes SNPS back toward / through $400;

Research dossier: UBER — Uber Technologies, Inc.

Researched: 2026-06-27 · Verdict: BUY · Conviction: 0.55 · Opportunity score: 58

Next earnings: 2026-08-05

Business

A two-sided demand-aggregation marketplace across Mobility (ride-hail), Delivery (Eats/grocery),

and Freight. Uber takes a cut of gross bookings routed through the network; riders/eaters and

drivers/couriers are the two sides, and Uber One (~50M members, >50% of bookings) is the retention

flywheel. Durability rests on the marketplace moat: two-sided liquidity (199M MAPCs, +17% YoY)

compounds with scale and monetizes whoever supplies the ride — including third-party AV fleets.

The franchise is now structurally FCF-generative ($2.3B FCF in Q1 alone), not a cash-burner.

Fresh thesis — is this a good buy now?

Yes, but with a thinner entry edge than a week ago. This is the rare quality compounder that

de-rated 25–30% on a fear while fundamentals accelerated — the inverse of the extended,

post-deal AI-infra field the macro read tells me to keep small. Q1 2026 (May 6) was an

acceleration print: gross bookings $53.7B (+25% YoY, +21% cc), revenue $13.2B (+14%), trips 3.64B

(+20%), non-GAAP EPS $0.72 (+44%), $3B single-quarter buyback, FCF $2.3B, Uber One 50M. Q2 guide:

bookings $56.25–57.75B (+18–22% cc). The sell-off is a narrative (AV disintermediation), not a

numbers break. The buy case: a roughly market multiple for clearly above-market growth, plus a free

call option on Uber being the demand-aggregation layer over a commoditizing AV supply

(orchestration-vs-substrate) — management cites ~30% higher trips/vehicle/day for AVs run on

Uber vs. stand-alone apps, the empirical core of "demand, not the car, is the scarce asset."

vs. the agent's own 2026-06-20 run (BUY / 0.55 / 60 at $71.64): same direction, re-derived. The

material change is price — UBER has bounced from $71.64 (06-18) to $76, roughly +6%, so the

dip-buyer entry edge is slightly more eroded than the prior run already flagged. No fundamental

news has landed since (May-6 print stale ~7 weeks; June Stellantis/Wayve L4 partnership is

incremental). I trim opportunity 60→58 for the further bounce off the lows. I keep the prior run's

normalized-EPS nuance (below) — it's the cleaner valuation lens.

Valuation + position vs. range

  • Price $76.03 (feed) / $76.20 close Jun 26; market cap ~$155B; ~2.04B shares.
  • Trailing P/E 18.84, but the trailing EPS $4.04 is distorted — 2025 net income ($8.54B TTM)

carried one-time equity-stake/tax gains (NI reads −30.5% YoY while revenue grew). The cleaner

read is forward P/E ~20.6 on normalized fwd EPS ~$3.70, with non-GAAP EPS compounding +30–40%.

  • Revenue TTM $53.69B. FCF run-rate ~$9B+/yr → FCF yield ~6% on a 20–25% bookings grower buying

back $3B/qtr. Forward PEG well under 1 — cheap unless AVs structurally impair the take rate.

  • Position: off 52w high 25.5%, above 52w low 13.1%, below 200DMA (−6.8%), above 50DMA

(+3.5%), ret_20d +7.3%. Still firmly dip-buyer zone — but it has rallied ~9% off the $69.67

(Jun 23) low, so the entry is less pristine than the $68–72 prints of the last two weeks.

  • My fair-value read (AV-haircut, ~24× normalized fwd EPS ~$3.70): ~$90 — below the ~$104

sell-side mean on purpose. At $76 there is ~18% to my haircut fair value, AV as the real downside.

Post-deal / repriced check

Inverts the Wait-for-deal thesis cleanly: not a post-deal chase, no consumed entry edge —

the multiple compressed into accelerating results. Per extended-with-vs-without-catalyst,

UBER is "extended" in neither direction; it's de-rated without a fundamentals break. The caveat:

the AV debate is a slow-burn terminal-value repricing, not a one-day overreaction — it can cap the

multiple for years even if it never breaks the business, and there's no live fresh catalyst at $76.

Council (one line each, no hedging)

  • Bull: An ~18–22% bookings grower at ~20× forward earnings, buying back stock near the lows,

where AVs route through Uber's demand graph at ~30% higher utilization — the moat is demand,

and Uber is the default aggregator for a sprawling roster of AV stacks.

  • Bear: Tesla (Cybercab volume production since April, own ride-hail expanding across TX) and

Waymo (now direct via its own app in most cities, partnership cooling) scale direct-to-consumer,

bypass the app, and structurally compress Uber's take rate — today's "cheap" multiple is a value

trap on a declining terminal business.

  • First-principles: A profitable, FCF-positive demand aggregator at <21× forward earnings is a

sound business today; the AV disruption question is a 2028–2030 debate, and at this price you're

paid to wait while the aggregator-vs-disintermediation optionality resolves.

tail, so scale in; don't go all-in at-market.

HPEBUYfetched57›

Research dossier: HPE — Hewlett Packard Enterprise Co.

Researched: 2026-06-27 · Verdict: BUY · Conviction: 0.56 · Opportunity score: 57

Next earnings: 2026-09-01

Business

HPE is a ~$39B-TTM-revenue enterprise IT infrastructure company, structured post-Juniper around three pillars:

1. Server / AI compute — ProLiant Gen12, Cray-class AI systems, NVIDIA Vera-CPU systems for agentic-AI workloads. Sells to hyperscalers, sovereign clouds, neoclouds, enterprise. The revenue engine, but the lowest-margin pillar — record AI-server backlog drives the top line, not the blend. This pillar is where the new memory-cost headwind bites (below).

2. Networking (Juniper, $14B deal) — Aruba campus + Mist AI + data-center switching/optical interconnect; the AI training-fabric play (switching inside GPU clusters). Higher-margin, the synergy story; networking revenue nearly tripled YoY in Q2 and cost synergies are running ahead of plan.

3. Hybrid cloud / GreenLake — consumption-model ARR on top of hardware; the recurring, margin-accretive layer over time.

Blended gross margin sits structurally above pure ODMs (HPE captures networking + software alongside boxes), but net margin is thin (~3.7% TTM) because the mix is currently tilted to low-margin AI servers. The EPS-leverage thesis rests on mix-shift toward Juniper networking + GreenLake as the AI-server wave runs through. Revenue durability = the multi-year AI buildout (capex kill-switch far from tripping) plus a $5.9B AI-systems backlog management says skews to enterprise and sovereign customers — a longer, less hyperscaler-concentrated runway than a handful of cloud whales.

Fresh thesis — is this a good buy now?

A week ago (2026-06-19) my dossier landed BUY at $47.41, conviction 0.55, opp 52 — but flagged that the entry edge was valuation, not a fresh dip, because the give-back had "stopped giving back." The explicit falsification was: a break below $46 reopens the strong dip_buyer base rate toward $42–44. That's exactly what happened. HPE closed ~$43.63 on June 26, down ~6.5% on the day, and the screen has it at $43.66 — straight into the zone I said to size up in. So the question is whether this is the cohort flush the prior run wanted, or a name-specific thesis crack. The evidence says mostly the former, with one genuine new bear weight:

  • The drop is cohort-wide, not HPE-specific. June 26 was a broad AI-hardware flush: SMCI −11%, DELL −9% the same day (Dell fell despite AI-server revenue surging), explicitly a positioning/high-beta rotation, not a fundamentals break. Per macro-cohort-confirmation this is the buyable shape — confirm the dip is cohort, not name, before buying, and it is. The macro stance demands "a real dip OR a fresh catalyst" before deploying cash; a cohort-confirmed −8% give-back over a week into the strong-base-rate zone is a real dip.
  • The base-rate read flipped bullish. A week ago BOTH presets were active and today's tape scored the weak extended_run. Today, with the lower price, only dip_buyer is active — the strong preset: +63d win 87.1%, avg +12.5% (worst −18.4%, 34 instances). The entry is no longer "trigger-gated and small pending a deeper fill"; the deeper fill arrived and the live base rate is now the favorable one.
  • The valuation got cheaper. Forward P/E 11.35× now (vs 12.31× a week ago) on the $3.35–3.45 FY26 EPS guide and 29–33% guided revenue growth, FCF >$3.5B. Still the cheapest real-cash-flow name in the AI-infra cohort, and — confirmed again through this cohort flush — the one a hawkish Fed barely touches (it doesn't need a rate cut to defend a 11× multiple).

The one genuine new bear weight: structural memory-cost margin pressure. DRAM contract prices rose ~90–95% QoQ in Q1 2026 and TrendForce projects another ~58–63% in Q2; HBM is eating DRAM wafer capacity and the reallocation is structural — meaningful relief isn't expected before late 2027 / 2028–29. Server OEMs (Dell, HP, HPE) have pushed through 15%+ price increases but are still guiding gross-margin declines on component costs. This bites HPE's lowest-margin, highest-revenue pillar directly and works against the margin-expansion leg of the bull case (the Juniper/GreenLake mix-shift now has to out-run a rising server-COGS headwind, not just a flat one). This is the real update vs. June 19, and it's why conviction stays ~0.55 rather than rising despite the better entry. (Note: a Morgan Stanley downgrade citing exactly this memory/Juniper-margin risk circulated — but that downgrade is dated Nov 2025, stale; the memory dynamic itself is live and worsening, which is what matters.)

Net: the entry geometry improved (cohort dip + strong base rate + cheaper multiple) roughly as much as the margin outlook worsened (memory COGS). Verdict holds BUY; the improvement in entry shape edges the opportunity score up to 57.

Valuation + position vs. range

  • Price ~$43.66; market cap ~$57.9B; shares ~1.32B; TTM revenue $38.79B; net income $1.44B (~3.7% margin); P/S ~1.5×; trailing P/E 40.8× (depressed by pre-turn earnings), forward P/E 11.35× (stockanalysis).
  • 52-week range $18.27–$64.25: ~32% off the high (was 26% a week ago), ~139% above the low — the post-turn re-rate has given back a chunk.
  • My fair value: I trim slightly to ~$52 (~15.3× FY26 EPS $3.40, a hair below my prior 16× to reflect the now-active memory-cost margin drag on the EPS-leverage path), optionality toward the $64 consensus (22 analysts, avg $64.13) if Juniper synergies + GreenLake mix demonstrably out-run server COGS. At $43.66 the market pays ~11–13× forward for ~30% guided growth — I read roughly 15–18% undervalued vs. my base case, but the discount is partly earned by the margin uncertainty, not a free lunch.

Post-deal / repriced check

Per Wait-for-deal thesis, both the Juniper deal and the Q2 print are consumed — the entry edge is not "wait for the catalyst." This is the sell-the-news → digestion shape: the print spiked it to $64 (overshoot), it bled to ~$47–48, held through the June-17 FOMC, then gave back again with the cohort to ~$43.66. The follow-on entry the framework describes — post-overshoot price + undemanding multiple — is now more live than a week ago: the price is lower, the multiple lower, and the give-back is cohort-driven rather than deal-term parsing. The deal isn't the catalyst here; the cohort dip into a cheap real-FCF name is the setup.

Council (one line each, no hedging)

  • Bull: ~30% guided revenue growth, $5.9B enterprise/sovereign-skewed backlog, Juniper synergies ahead of plan, FCF >$3.5B — at 11× forward, the only cheap real-FCF name in the cohort, and the one a hawkish Fed and this cohort flush leave structurally cheapest.
  • Bear: 3.7% net margin on a box-shifter whose lowest-margin pillar now faces a structural memory-cost squeeze (DRAM +90%+ QoQ, relief years away) — the margin-expansion leg the whole "EPS leverage" case depends on is fighting a rising-COGS tide, and the stock is still +62% over its 200DMA.
  • First-principles: a $58B enterprise-infra franchise throwing off $3.5B FCF, growing ~30%, at ~11–13× forward is a coherent buy on its own merits even if AI-server margins stay pinched — the narrative is upside, thin-but-real cash earnings are the floor.

Entry levels the agent derives

  • Buy-below: $45 — current $43.66 zone is buyable on a cohort-confirmed dip with dip_buyer live; re-derived this run: fair $52 ÷ ~1.15 margin-of-safety ≈ $45. This is the first run where price sits below buy-below, not at it.

The weak extended_run preset is no longer the live read. The strong base rate supports entry at this zone rather than waiting — but the worst-case −18% (and −39% worst drawdown) argue for sizing in, not a full clip at once.

Macro fit

The macro "prefer names still below fair value, demand a real dip" stance describes this setup well.

APPBUYmissing56›

Research dossier: APP — AppLovin Corporation

Researched: 2026-06-26 · Verdict: BUY · Conviction: 0.55 · Opportunity score: 56

Next earnings: 2026-08-12

Business

AppLovin runs a two-sided mobile advertising marketplace. Its AXON AI engine ingests real-time

bid signals from app publishers and matches them to advertiser demand at very low latency,

producing far higher install-to-ad-spend ROI than competing networks. Revenue is now almost

entirely the Software Platform (AXON + MAX mediation) after the legacy Apps/games portfolio was

sold down. Who pays: mobile app marketers — historically gaming studios, now expanding into

e-commerce/consumer brands. The flywheel is data-compounding: every impression feeds AXON,

improving match quality, which raises advertiser ROI, which attracts more spend, which feeds

more data. For 14 years AXON was closed/invite-only; that gate comes off in June 2026.

Fresh thesis — is this a good buy now?

**This run flips the prior HOLD to a BUY, because the two things the June-5 dossier was waiting

for both happened: the price came to fair value, and the catalyst went live.**

What changed since the last dive (HOLD at ~$571):

  • Price: ~$477 (June 26, +7% on the day), down ~36% off the 52w high (~$745) and -20.6%

over 20 days — squarely in the dip-buyer zone (-4% vs 50DMA, -11.9% vs 200DMA). Market cap

fell ~$190B → ~$160B.

  • The selloff is sentiment, not fundamentals. Drivers: SEC-probe overhang (unresolved),

insider selling (CEO Adam Foroughi ~$25.8M across June 10/12; director Vivas June 16 — read

as profit-taking after a huge run, still holding millions, not a wholesale exit), and broad

SaaS-sector weakness. The business prints kept beating.

  • AXON open self-serve is now LIVE (late June 2026), not just announced. Referral-gated

self-serve has run since Oct 1 2025; June removes the gate so any advertiser worldwide can

sign up. This is the genuine business-model inflection — gaming → e-commerce, a multiples-larger

TAM at ~zero incremental infra cost. Per extended-with-vs-without-catalyst, a fact about

the business changed; this is a fresh catalyst, not FOMO.

Fundamentals (Q1 CY2026, reported May 6) are still excellent:

  • Revenue $1.84B, +59% YoY; adjusted EBITDA $1.56B (85% margin, +400bp YoY); EPS $3.56

beat $3.42. TTM revenue $6.16B (+66%); TTM net income $3.96B.

  • Q2 guide: $1.93B midpoint (+52–55% YoY), EBITDA $1.63B — above consensus. No material

deceleration.

  • Consumer-vertical momentum: March advertiser spend +25% vs January; April a record high. The

named bottleneck is a 57% go-live conversion rate among qualified leads, which GenAI creative

tools (piloted with 100+ advertisers) aim to lift.

The disqualifier the prior dossier flagged is still standing: the SEC investigation into

AXON's data-collection practices (open since Oct 2025; rooted in the Muddy Waters / Fuzzy Panda

allegations that AXON pulls cross-app identifiers from Meta/Google). That is a real binary on the

flywheel's core. So this is a BUY sized for the tail, not a high-conviction pound-the-table.

Valuation + position vs. range

The decisive re-rating vs. the prior dossier: forward P/E is now 25.6x (the prior dive

modeled ~37x on a lower ~$15.50 EPS estimate). Forward estimates rose and the price fell, so

the multiple compressed hard. For a business growing 50%+ with 85% EBITDA margins

(Rule-of-40 ~145), a sub-26x forward multiple is a PEG well under 1 — growth-adjusted, this is

not expensive. That is the heart of the fresh buy case.

My own fair-value anchor (re-derived this run, not carried):

  • Base: ~28x fwd EPS ~$18.6 → ~$520, then haircut ~3–5% for the SEC binary → fair ~$500–510.
  • Bull (AXON-open e-commerce ROAS confirms, SEC clears): 32–35x → $595–650 (street avg

target ~$650).

  • Bear (SEC forces a data-practice change that breaks cross-app targeting; margins compress):

~18–20x on impaired EPS → $280–320.

  • Probabilistic midpoint ~$500. At $477 you're ~5% below fair with a live catalyst — a modest

but real margin of safety, the first clean-ish entry since the prior dive.

Post-deal / repriced check

Wait-for-deal thesis maps loosely (ad-tech, not AI infra). The equivalent question: *has the

AXON-open catalyst been consumed by price?* No — the opposite of the prior dive. Last time the

announcement had run the stock to $571; today the stock is 17% lower and the catalyst is now

actually live, so first-cohort open-platform data (e-commerce ROAS, onboarding volume, system

stability) is ahead, not priced. This is a live catalyst with remaining optionality, bought

below fair value — not a consumed post-deal name.

Council (one line each, no hedging)

  • Bull: AXON going closed/gaming → open/e-commerce unlocks a 10x+ TAM at 85% margins with no

incremental infra — a royalty on mobile commerce — and you're buying it at <26x forward for

50%+ growth, ~36% off the high.

  • Bear: The SEC probe is about the cross-app data that is the flywheel; a ruling that

forces AXON to stop ingesting Meta/Google identifiers breaks the model, and insiders are

selling into the open-platform launch.

  • First-principles: $160B cap, ~$4B net income, 50%+ growth, sub-26x forward — cheap on a

growth-adjusted basis if the SEC tail resolves benign; you cannot buy "if," so you size small.

Entry levels the agent derives

Research dossier: AMZN — Amazon.com, Inc.

Researched: 2026-07-01 · Verdict: BUY · Conviction: 0.55 · Opportunity score: 55

Next earnings: 2026-07-30

Business

Two businesses bolted together. Retail logistics (~83% of revenue, low-margin) is the funding

mechanism; AWS (~18% of revenue but the majority of operating income at ~38% segment margins)

is the compounding engine and the only part that matters for the marginal investor. AWS is a

vertically integrated AI-compute utility — it owns the land, power contracts, data centers, custom

silicon (Trainium / Graviton / Nitro), the compiler stack, and the frontier-lab workloads training

on it. Revenue durably continues because the demand is contracted: a $364B AWS backlog (Q1 2026,

+~50% QoQ) plus Anthropic's $100B / 10-yr commitment and a new $50B OpenAI distribution deal lock in

multi-year demand on chips Amazon designs itself, with switching costs denominated in petabytes of

egress.

Fresh thesis — is this a good buy now?

Yes, but a weaker buy than a week ago — the clean dip-buyer entry has partly closed while a

fresh catalyst has landed to compensate. The prior agent run (2026-06-24, BUY 0.62/62) bought the

$234 dip squarely in the Dip Buyer zone, below both fair-value frames. Since then AMZN has bounced

+3.2% to $241.70, which is now 13.2% off its 52-week high — back out of the ≥15% Dip Buyer

band, into the "no active preset" geometry the user's own 2026-05-30 HOLD dive flagged as poor for

adds. That erodes the entry edge the June-24 run caught.

What replaces it is a genuinely fresh, thesis-confirming catalyst: on July 1 AWS pushed through

a 20% price hike on reserved GPU capacity — its third consecutive quarterly increase. That is

pricing power, not price-taking, and it directly rebuts the standing bear case (that the AWS reaccel

is "bought, not earned" via margin compression). Per extended-with-vs-without-catalyst, a fresh

thesis-confirming datapoint legitimizes buying a name that isn't at its lows — and this one attacks

the exact soft spot in the thesis. The user's 2026-05-30 dive landed HOLD at $270.64 waiting for a

$258/$230 ladder; I agree with their framework but diverge to BUY because (a) price is now

~$29 below their HOLD level and still under both their fair-value bands, and (b) a fresh pricing

catalyst has arrived that they didn't have. I mark down from the June-24 run's 0.62/62 because

the pristine dip has bounced and a new structural negative (EU DMA gatekeeper designation of AWS)

has appeared.

Valuation + position vs. range

EPS TTM $8.36 (stockanalysis.com, fetched). FCF is −$2.47B — negative, exactly the J-curve shape

ai-capex-payback-window predicts when a hyperscaler front-loads $200B of capex ahead of revenue.

Treat it as expected, not a break: capex guidance is still rising (kill-switch #1 far from

threshold), so FCF compression is the consequence of acceleration. Third-party estimates have 2026

FCF swinging to roughly −$19B before recovering to ~+$48B in 2027 — the payback curve, not a leak.

Headline EPS also overstates operating power (~$16B of Q1 GAAP profit was an unrealized Anthropic

fair-value mark) — weight AWS revenue/margin over net income.

Research dossier: AVGO — Broadcom Inc.

Researched: 2026-06-30 · Verdict: BUY · Conviction: 0.58 · Opportunity score: 55

Next earnings: 2026-09-03

Business

Broadcom is two businesses fused: (1) Semiconductors (~$15B/qtr, the growth engine) — it

co-designs custom AI accelerators (XPUs) with individual hyperscalers and sells the **AI

networking silicon** (Tomahawk/Jericho switches, optics) that stitches thousands of those chips

into a cluster. Six core custom-silicon customers, publicly including Google, Meta, OpenAI and

Anthropic. (2) Infrastructure software (~$7B/qtr, the cash ballast) — VMware plus the legacy

CA/Symantec stack, sold as sticky enterprise subscriptions. The XPU + switch-fabric position is a

genuine orchestration-vs-substrate substrate play at the silicon layer: whoever builds the

cluster pays Broadcom regardless of which lab wins the model race. That's why the revenue durably

continues — picks-and-shovels — and unlike the hyperscalers it is FCF-positive right now, not

underwater in the ai-capex-payback-window J-curve.

Fresh thesis — is this a good buy now?

Yes, on a staged basis — the entry gate the Jun-23 run said was closed has re-opened. That run

landed HOLD/47 explicitly because the Jun-3 earnings catalyst had aged out and the price (~$380)

was only ~fair. Its own 60-day falsification named the exact event that would flip it back to BUY:

"a fresh catalyst — a new XPU customer, an FY26 AI raise, or a named multi-year offtake."

That event landed June 24: Broadcom and OpenAI unveiled Jalapeño, OpenAI's first custom

LLM-inference ASIC, as the first piece of a plan to deploy **~10 GW of OpenAI-designed accelerators

between 2H-2026 and end-2029** — taped out in ~9 months (claimed fastest high-performance ASIC

cycle ever). This is a named, multi-year offtake with a deployment schedule, not a reaffirm. It

converts OpenAI from "one of six logos on a slide" into a productized, scheduled revenue stream and

hard-confirms the durability of the XPU pipeline through the back half of the decade.

The reason this is a buy and not a chase (extended-with-vs-without-catalyst): the catalyst is

6 days old (well inside the catalyst window) and the stock did not re-rate on it — it popped

~2% intraday and sits at $377.75, still ~24% off its $495 high and in the dip-buyer zone

(−8.1% vs 50DMA). So I am buying a de-rated quality name into a fresh positive catalyst, which

is the opposite of paying up for one already priced. The honest counterweight: Jalapeño's revenue is

"modest in FY26, meaningful in FY27" — back-half-loaded — and OpenAI was already a known XPU

customer, so this concretizes rather than expands the logo count. It earns the upgrade, but a

measured one.

Valuation + position vs. range

  • Price $377.75 (Jun 30 close); market cap $1.80T; 4.76B shares; dividend $2.60 (0.69%).
  • Forward P/E ~23.7× vs trailing 62.0×; TTM revenue $75.47B (+32.3%), TTM net income

$29.32B (+127%); P/S ≈ 24× TTM ($1.80T / $75.47B).

Research dossier: TLN — Talen Energy Corporation

Researched: 2026-06-13 · Verdict: HOLD · Conviction: 0.55 · Opportunity score: 55

Next earnings: 2026-08-06

Business

Independent power producer (IPP) in PJM. Crown jewel is the ~2.2 GW Susquehanna nuclear plant

in PA; the rest is a merchant gas/coal fleet, recently enlarged by the Cornerstone

acquisition of two CCGT gas plants (financed April 2026). Who pays them: wholesale power

markets (spark spreads + PJM capacity payments) and, increasingly, a hyperscaler under a

long-dated retail PPA. The durability case is the AI Energy Thesis nuclear lane — Talen

sells dispatchable, carbon-free baseload into a grid where AI data-center load is the marginal

buyer, and the Susquehanna footprint is already-permitted, already-interconnected (long the

permitting-as-binding-constraint). The June-2025 Amazon deal: 1.9 GW, 17-year, ~$18B

revenue, restructured from co-located behind-the-meter into a grid-connected front-of-meter

retail PPA (Talen as AWS's licensed PA retail provider), plus optionality on SMRs and

Susquehanna uprates.

Fresh thesis — is this a good buy now?

Q1 2026 (reported May 5): Adjusted EBITDA $473M (>2× YoY), Adjusted FCF $350M (~4× YoY), driven

by the acquisitions + tighter PJM spark spreads. 2026 guidance reaffirmed: Adj EBITDA

$1.75–2.05B, Adj FCF $0.98–1.18B (ex-Cornerstone); pro-forma EBITDA tracks toward ~$2B with

Cornerstone. $1.9B buyback authorization runs through 2028 (~12% of the cap; $100M bought in

Q1). So the operating story is intact and improving. The catch is timing, not quality: unlike

the rest of the AI-infra cohort, TLN is not extended — ret_60d +6.5%, ret_20d +2.2%, sitting

right on its 50-DMA (+0.7%) and below its 200-DMA (−2.9%), ~20% off the 52w high. That is a

consolidating, mildly-corrective post-deal name, not a parabola. The fresh-buy question is

name at a fair-to-slightly-cheap price, but the entry-defining catalyst is a year old and the

near-term macro (binary FOMC in 3 days, beta 1.60) argues for a trigger-gated entry rather than

at-market.

  • Price ~$360.58. Market cap ~$16.4B on ~45.4M shares. 52w range $255.50–$451.28 (20% off high,

~41% above low).

2027–28. Revenue TTM $3.24B (+55% YoY). GAAP net income −$21M / EPS −$0.46 — this is

hedge/mark-to-market noise; the cash story (Adj FCF) is the real signal, but the GAAP–adjusted

gap is a thing to watch, not ignore.

  • FCF yield ~6.6% ($1.08B guidance midpoint / $16.4B cap). With ~$3–4B net debt (post-$4B

refi + Cornerstone financing), EV ~$20B → EV/EBITDA ~10× on ~$2B PF EBITDA. For an IPP

Post-deal / repriced check

extended-with-vs-without-catalyst — none has landed yet.

Research dossier: MSFT — Microsoft Corporation

Researched: 2026-06-27 · Verdict: HOLD · Conviction: 0.6 · Opportunity score: 54

Next earnings: 2026-07-29

Business

Three segments: Productivity & Business Processes (M365/Office, Copilot add-on, LinkedIn,

Dynamics), Intelligent Cloud (Azure, server products, enterprise services), and More Personal

Computing (Windows, Xbox, search). Who pays: enterprises on multi-year commercial contracts (the

$627B contracted backlog is the durability signal), consumers on subscriptions, and — increasingly

— AI workloads on Azure. Revenue durably continues because of switching costs (M365 is embedded in

enterprise workflow), the Azure enterprise distribution moat, and the OpenAI compute relationship

that routes a structurally large AI workload through Azure. TTM revenue $318.27B, TTM EPS $16.79,

FCF $72.9B (TTM), GM 68.31%, op margin 46.80% — still the strongest cash base of any hyperscaler,

even as quarterly FCF compresses.

Fresh thesis — is this a good buy now?

Last week (my 06-20 dossier) MSFT was a HOLD at $379.40, 31.7% off high. It kept falling.

Today it's **$371.67 (last close; $372.97 at the 06-26 close), 33.1% off the 52-week high, ~6.4%

above its 52-week low (~$349), -24% on the trailing year, ret_20d -13.0%**, ret_60d

+0.4%, -9.6% vs 50DMA, -17.0% vs 200DMA. It hit a fresh ~3-year low in late June (down

~21.6% in the month alone — among its worst months ever). This is now the **deepest, most genuine

dip in the megacap-AI complex** — the opposite of a chase.

But the reason it keeps falling has intensified, not resolved, since last week. The OpenAI

overhang that I flagged on 06-20 is now quantified and worse:

1. OpenAI concentration, now sized: ~45% of the $627B backlog (~$281B) is tied to OpenAI —

a single customer that is actively diversifying to AWS and Oracle, faces projected losses,

and reportedly missed 2026 sales targets. This is the ouroboros-economy-circular-financing

risk made concrete: MSFT's $37B AI run-rate and ~$250B Azure commitment lean on a counterparty

whose demand is visibly questioned and whose cloud spend is now flowing partly elsewhere.

2. NEW since last week — OpenAI IPO push-out: an NYT report flagged OpenAI's expected IPO may

slip into 2027. MSFT holds a 27% stake (~$135B); pushing out the monetization/validation

event hit sentiment directly. This is a fresh negative catalyst, not the J-curve symptom the

payback frame tells me to expect.

3. A pile of smaller fresh negatives: a 20-year Chevron power deal (more capex commitment for

AI data centers); ~1,000 Xbox layoffs + console price hikes up to $150 (hardware rev -33% YoY,

gaming -7%, memory-cost squeeze); a class-action securities-fraud suit alleging overstated

Copilot/Azure capability; and Nature (06-24) skepticism of MSFT's quantum claims. None is

thesis-breaking; together they explain why the de-rate has no buyer cushion yet.

4. First stabilization tell: 06-25 -3.8%, then 06-26 +4.82% on cooler PCE + stabilizing

yields. A sharp one-day bounce off the low is a dead-cat until the print confirms it — do not

chase it.

Per ai-capex-payback-window, FCF/margin compression while capex guidance still rises ($190B

CY2026, +84% YoY capex; quarterly FCF $15.8B, -22% YoY) is the expected shape, and kill-switch

#1 is nowhere near tripped — that part holds. The OpenAI demand/concentration question is a

different, real worry: not "payback is far away" but "is the anchor AI customer solvent and

committed enough to take the capacity MSFT is building?" That fresh negative is why I won't upgrade

to BUY at-market into a deepening slide.

Valuation + position vs. range

  • Forward P/E 20.14× (stockanalysis.com, fetched 06-26), trailing 22.21×, P/S 8.71×, mkt cap

$2.77T. That is now at or below the S&P's ~20–22× forward (macro.md) — for the highest-quality

The restructured OpenAI partnership (~$250B Azure commitment + IP through 2032) **already landed and
