(signed May 5, 2026) to build a 40 MW campus in Columbiana, AL — Phase 1 (15 MW) targeted

Dec 15, 2026, full 40 MW by Q1 2027, with a $1.4B expansion option and ~$2.5B total value

including renewals; and (2) NeoCloudz GPU-as-a-Service, live since May 15, 2026 with first

AI revenue booked, backed by a fresh $35M NVIDIA Vera Rubin (next-gen GPU) purchase commitment

(June 3, 2026, initial deployment Q1 2027). Why would this revenue durably continue? Cerebras

is a credible, well-funded counterparty (IPO'd, ~$6.38B raised, $24.6B RPO, GAAP-profitable

FY2025 per the prior audit trail) — a 10-year MSA with renewal options is a real anchor, not a

pilot. The structural risk is that DGXX's entire forward AI-colocation book rides on one

customer not renegotiating or slow-walking delivery.

Fresh thesis — is this a good buy now?

Price closed $4.62 on 2026-07-02, down 9.6% on the day (stockanalysis.com prints $4.60;

treating $4.60–4.62 as the same reading). That's a −32% slide from $6.75 on 2026-06-18, and

well below the user's own $5.40 buy-below trigger set in a 2026-05-19 vault deep-dive (WATCH

at $7.72 then) — a trigger the user's 2026-06-09 follow-up dive (WATCH at $5.90, 29.3%

Monte-Carlo break-even) implicitly reconfirmed. Mechanically, spot is now ~15% through that

level. Two questions decide whether that's a green light: (1) is the drop cohort beta or

name-specific bad news, and (2) has anything material changed since the trigger was set.

(1) Cohort, confirmed. July 2's tape: RIOT −7.5%, HUT −8.5%, IREN −4.7%, CIFR −8.2%, with

CORZ/WULF/HUT/RIOT/CIFR all down ~10-14% into the session — driven by the worst-ever month of

spot Bitcoin ETF outflows (−$4.5B in June), miner economics pushed toward shutdown-level

profitability per Antpool data, and a Meta "Compute" business-unit headline pressuring the

whole GPU-supply-glut narrative. DGXX's −9.6% that day sits squarely inside that range — this

passes macro-cohort-confirmation: the cohort is also red, so this reads as liquidity, not

new information about Cerebras or Columbiana specifically. But DGXX is an imperfect fit for

that lens: it's still ticker-classified and beta-correlated as a bitcoin miner (residual legacy

segment, ATM-funded capex model shared with the cohort) even though the thesis it's actually

being bought for (Cerebras/Columbiana) has nothing to do with BTC price. That cuts both ways —

it's a real argument the market is mispricing DGXX on the wrong cohort tag, but it also means

DGXX inherits the cohort's dilution-funding and volatility DNA (beta ~6.1) whether or not the

AI thesis performs.

**(2) Something did change since the trigger was set — and it's a yellow flag, not a green

one.** Digi Power X signed a non-binding LOI with Omnis Pleasants LLC (owner of the 1.3 GW

Pleasants Power Station, WV) on January 7, 2026, targeting a definitive agreement within

~90 days (missed) and a load study within 30 days. On March 2, 2026 the company announced

due diligence was extended 120 days — landing the self-set deadline at ~June 30, 2026.

As of today (July 3, 2026), extensive search of SEC filings, StockTitan's DGXX news feed, and

general web search finds no announcement since June 3 (the NVIDIA Vera Rubin PR) touching

Pleasants at all — no definitive agreement, no further extension, no termination. The deadline

has passed silently. Companies that are closing a deal usually say so; going dark past a

self-set deadline is more often a sign the deal is stalling or being quietly renegotiated than

a sign it's fine. This doesn't touch the core Cerebras/Columbiana thesis (Pleasants was always

optionality — a possible second Tier-1 signing / equity stake in the plant, not the base case),

but it removes one leg of the bull case that both the agent's and the user's earlier reads

leaned on ("355 MW unbooked pipeline is a free 2nd-signing option"), and it's new information

neither the 5/19 nor the 6/09 vault dives had visibility into.

Net: the slide itself is beta, not thesis-breaking. But the trigger that says "buy at $5.40"

was calibrated before the Pleasants deadline came and went unexplained. Re-running the

judgment (not just the arithmetic) on today's data says the setup is weaker, not stronger,

than it looked on 5/19 or 6/09 — even though the price is lower.

Valuation + position vs. range

  • Price: $4.62 (7/2 close) · 52-week range: $1.86 – $9.20 → ~50% off the 52-week high,

well outside (deeper than) the 15–30%-off-high band the dip-buyer backtest below is built on.

  • Market cap: ~$419–421M (91.09M shares out — essentially flat since May, i.e. the ATM has not

been drawn aggressively in the last ~6 weeks).

  • Revenue TTM: $31.70M, down 4.9% YoY (legacy wind-down; Q1 print alone was −26.8% YoY).

Net loss TTM −$31.38M, EPS −$0.59. Pre-profit; Q1 gross profit was the fourth straight

negative-gross-profit quarter per the prior audit trail (unverified this run, flagged as

carried-forward, not re-confirmed).

  • Cash: ~$150M (June 3 disclosure) vs. ~$65M YTD capex deployed at Columbiana — a real cushion

relative to near-term Phase 1 spend, though the ~$440M full-buildout capex estimate from the

6/15 audit still implies a larger financing need over the next 12-18 months (the live $175M

ATM, upsized 5/8/2026, is the standing overhang; it has not been meaningfully drawn since,

which is mildly reassuring but not resolved).

  • Price-to-backlog: $419M cap vs. $1.1B Cerebras 10-yr MSA = 0.38× — cheaper than the

0.49–0.58× cited at $7.02 on 6/15, and well below CoreWeave-style operational comps (~2–2.5×).

This is the honest core of the bull case: even haircut heavily for execution risk, the

contracted backlog alone is a large multiple of the current cap.

  • Forward P/S: at the FY2027 guide midpoint ($275M) against a modestly diluted ~95-100M share

count, fair value in the 2.0–2.5× sales range lands at $5.50–$6.90/share — i.e. today's

$4.62 is trading meaningfully below even a conservative forward multiple, if the guide

holds and Phase 1 delivers on time.

  • Short interest: ~4.2% of float (3.4M shares), down 54.8% from the prior reporting period —

moderate and shrinking, not a crowded-short setup and not a squeeze catalyst either way.

Post-deal / repriced check

Per Wait-for-deal thesis, the Cerebras deal (5/5/26) is the wait-for-deal catalyst and it

already fired — the pre-deal entry edge is consumed. The stock ran $3→$9.20 on the deal, then

gave back the entire move and then some: $9.20 → $6.75 (6/18) → $4.62 (7/2), now trading

below both the agent's own prior post-deal buy-below ($6.50, set 6/9) and the user's own

post-deal trigger ($5.40). This isn't "extended, don't chase" — it's the opposite failure mode:

a post-deal name that has round-tripped through its own entry zone and kept falling on cohort

beta rather than deal-specific news. That's the exact setup macro-cohort-confirmation is

built for. The complication is that **the fresh catalyst since the last trigger (NVIDIA Vera

Rubin, 6/3) is a small positive, while the fresh non-catalyst (Pleasants silence) is a

larger, unresolved negative** — so the net update since 6/9 tilts slightly bearish on

information even as the price tilts bullish on value.

Council (one line each, no hedging)

  • Bull: 0.38× price-to-backlog against a credible, funded counterparty (Cerebras) and a

cohort-driven (not thesis-driven) −32% slide is exactly the liquidity-event entry

macro-cohort-confirmation is designed to catch — and the NVIDIA commitment is a real,

dated confirmation the NeoCloudz leg is more than a slide deck.

  • Bear: Revenue is still shrinking, four straight negative-gross-profit quarters (carried

from prior audit, unverified this run), single-customer concentration, a live $175M dilution

overhang, and now a second-leg catalyst (Pleasants) that has gone dark past its own deadline —

silence after a self-set deadline is evidence, not neutrality, and it should update you

bearish on the optionality leg specifically.

  • First-principles: A company with a $1.1B signed contract, ~$150M cash, and a cheaper

price-to-backlog than every operational comp is worth more than $419M if it executes — but

"if it executes" is now carrying more open questions (Pleasants status, Phase 1 on-track

confirmation not refreshed since 6/3, Q2 print not yet in) than it was three weeks ago, not

fewer.

Entry levels the agent derives

Re-derived from today's price-to-backlog and forward-P/S math, discounted for the Pleasants

silence (removes the "free second-signing option" upside leg without changing the base

Cerebras/Columbiana case):

  • Buy-below (small starter only, not full size): $4.50 — inside the current price, i.e. a

tranche-sized nibble is defensible on the cohort-beta + cheap-backlog logic, but this is

explicitly gated per catalyst-payoff-shape-sizing: the payoff here is symmetric/left-skewed

with real concentration risk (single customer, unresolved second catalyst), not a clean

asymmetric-upside-with-floor — so it caps adds, it doesn't earn a full-size position.

  • Fair value: ~$6.20 (midpoint of the 2.0–2.5× FY2027-sales-guide band on a modestly

diluted share count) — assumes Phase 1 delivers on schedule and NeoCloudz keeps ramping;

no credit given for Pleasants.

  • Stretch: ~$11.00 — requires Phase 2 financing to close, the 40 MW build to fully ramp,

AND Pleasants to convert to a definitive agreement (i.e., the optionality leg has to come

back from silence, not just avoid getting worse).

  • Invalidation: below ~$3.50 — the level at which the market is pricing in either Phase 1

slippage past the Dec 15 RFS date or a Cerebras counterparty problem; reassess the whole

thesis there, not just the entry price.

  • Full-size add gate (event, not price): either a Pleasants resolution in either direction

(a definitive agreement is bullish; a clean termination is actually clarifying and lets you

re-price without the optionality noise) or Phase 1 on-track confirmation in the Q2 print

(2026-08-13) — not a lower price alone.

Macro fit

Kill-switches: all three remain clear as of the 2026-06-05 macro read on file (capex

accelerating, GPU pricing/lead-times not collapsed, credit spreads tight) — this read is

~4 weeks stale relative to today and should be refreshed, but nothing in this dive's search

surfaced a kill-switch trip. Regime: still extended by valuation, rate-cut timeline still

pushed out — a sizing constraint on high-P/S names generally, though DGXX at 2× forward sales

is not in the extreme-P/S bucket that constraint is aimed at.

Entry-shape base rate (research/patterns.py --ticker DGXX, 1129 bars): the dip_buyer

preset (15–30% below 52w high, 39 instances) shows +63d win 22.2%, avg −13.6%, average max

drawdown −33.6% (worst −71.3%). DGXX today is **~50% off its 52w high — deeper than the band

this backtest covers** — so if anything these already-weak numbers are optimistic for how

extreme today's drawdown actually is. This is a real headwind the price-to-backlog bull case

has to overcome, and it's the strongest quantitative argument against mechanically buying the

dip here. (For contrast, the extended_run preset — not the live setup — shows a much healthier

+63d win 45.0%, avg +8.1%: this name performs far better bought on strength than bought on

weakness, historically.)

Opportunity-cost rank: mid-pack. DGXX is genuinely cheap on backlog and has a real, dated

positive catalyst (NVIDIA) since the last trigger — but it's competing for the marginal dollar

against names without a live, self-inflicted information gap (Pleasants) and without a

dip-buying base rate this weak. Small-starter-appropriate, not a top-of-book allocation.

Falsification

30 days (by Aug 3, 2026): Any Pleasants news at all — definitive agreement, further

extension, or termination — resolves the single biggest open question in this dive.

Continued silence past 30 more days of nothing should itself be read as increasingly bearish.

60 days (by Sep 3, 2026): Q2 print (2026-08-13) — first look at NeoCloudz revenue

recognition, Phase 1 construction-milestone commentary, cash burn/runway update, and whether

management addresses Pleasants on the call even without an SEC filing.

90 days (by Oct 3, 2026): Phase 1 Dec-15 RFS trajectory should be unambiguous one way or

the other by this point (on-track vs. slipping) via the Q2 call and any interim construction

updates.

Flips to AVOID: Cerebras terminates/materially renegotiates the MSA; Phase 1 confirmed

slipping past Q1 2027; a dilutive raise that swamps the forward-revenue-per-share case; or

Pleasants explicitly terminates combined with Phase 1 slippage (either alone is a leg lost,

not a broken thesis — both together would be).

Flips toward higher conviction BUY: Pleasants converts to a definitive agreement (removes

the current silence-risk entirely and restores the "free second signing" optionality), or Q2

print confirms Phase 1 firmly on track with NeoCloudz revenue materializing at scale.

Not investment advice. Sandbox account only.

Research dossier: MDB — MongoDB, Inc.

Researched: 2026-06-30 · Verdict: HOLD · Conviction: 0.57 · Opportunity score: 50

Next earnings: 2026-08-27

Business

MongoDB sells a document-oriented database platform. Two revenue buckets:

  • Atlas (cloud-managed, consumption-based) — the growth engine, ~75% of total revenue,

grew 29.4% YoY in Q1 FY2027 and added a record $117M of dollar net-new ARR in one quarter

(four straight quarters of >29% growth). Multi-cloud, serverless, with vector search,

text/time-series search, and stream processing folded in natively — the bet is collapsing a

separate vector store + operational DB + feature store into one managed surface, and becoming

the persistent-state layer for AI agents going to production. (orchestration-vs-substrate:

MDB is an orchestration-side data layer, not GPU substrate — its AI exposure is workload

demand, not the capex J-curve.)

  • Enterprise Advanced + Other (self-managed license + support) — mature, ~+13% YoY,

high-margin, declining share of mix.

Who pays: dev-centric enterprises building modern apps — fintech, e-commerce, gaming, real-time

personalization, AI-agent backends. The document model's schema flexibility fits when data shape

evolves fast (i.e. every AI product in production). Atlas is sticky once pipelines are built on

it. Durability: high.

Last print — Q1 FY2027 (quarter ended 2026-04-30): revenue $687.6M (+25% YoY), an

acceleration from the ~22% trough; non-GAAP op margin 18% (up from 16%); non-GAAP EPS

$1.32 (vs $1.18 est.); a small GAAP net income of +$4.4M (TTM GAAP still −$29M). TTM

revenue $2.60B (+23.6%). No new print since — the next data point is the Aug 27 Q2 report,

so fundamentals are unchanged from the May 28 print.

Fresh thesis — is this a good buy now?

**Support held. Catalyst didn't change. So the verdict shouldn't either — it's a hold-of-support

name to accumulate on a real dip, not a buy at $336 with nothing new since May.**

Ten days ago (06-20) MDB sat exactly on its 200-DMA at $332.75, right on the $335 trigger, with a

just-cleared hawkish FOMC as the new fact. The open question I left was whether the 200-DMA

shelf (~$320–325) would hold or the dip-buyer base rate would reassert toward $300. It held:

price is $335.90, now ~+1.1% above the 200-DMA, and there was no further leg down. That is

mild confirmation the multiple rests on real 25% growth rather than pure duration — but a held

200-DMA is not a fresh catalyst, and the price is now ~$11 above my $325 buy-below trigger

rather than on it. Per the lens (extended-with-vs-without-catalyst, principles §"Price

levels: inputs, not laws"), buying above your own entry level without a genuinely new catalyst is

exactly the IREN-at-$61 mistake. Nothing has landed since the print: the only June-20-to-30 items

are routine (investor-conference presentations, an Assenagon 13F-style purchase on 06-27) — not

thesis-moving.

Two cautions still cap the verdict at HOLD:

1. The dip base rate remains poor and still active. patterns.py dip_buyer preset (active

today): +63d win 33.3%, avg −13.5%, avg max DD −25.4% across 33 instances; +21d win

40.0%. This name's dips historically keep dipping. The extended_run preset is the better cohort

(+63d win 45.5%) but isn't the one that fires here. Buying at-market into a 33%-win setup

demands a trigger, not a tick — and at $336 we are above the trigger, not at it.

2. Hostile regime, unchanged. Macro (2026-06-30) is a quiet continuation of the hawkish June

FOMC: no 2026 cuts in the base case, a hike tail in the dots, hold-cash / keep-high-P/S-small

in force. A 53× forward-P/E name gets no rate-relief tailwind this year; it has to earn the

multiple on growth/FCF alone.

Bottom line: The thesis is intact, support held, and the price is fair-to-slightly-cheap —

but no new fact arrived to make $336 a better entry than the $325-on-confirmation level I'd buy.

Still on the doorstep, still not through the door — and now standing a step back from the

trigger.

Valuation + position vs. range

My fair-value read (re-derived this run):

  • Base: FY2027 $2.94B → FY2028 ~$3.58B at ~22% (Atlas-led) → 9.5× = ~$34B → ~$390/sh

(lands near the 40-analyst consensus ~$394).

  • Bull: AI workloads push growth to 26–28% and margin to ~22% (Rule-of-40 ~50) → 11× FY28

→ ~$39.4B → ~$455/sh.

  • Bear: growth fades to ~17%, hyperscaler-DB pricing compresses Atlas → 8× FY27 → ~$23.5B →

~$273/sh.

At $335.90 you sit ~14% below base-case fair value — a real but shallow discount, and slightly

less margin of safety than the ~15% at the 06-20 $332.75 read. Reward/risk is essentially

unchanged-to-marginally-worse vs ten days ago: price up a touch, fair value the same, regime the

same.

Post-deal / repriced check

Not a "wait-for-deal" name (product company, not infra-for-hire). The relevant question: has the

AI-thesis catalyst landed and been priced out? **Landed yes, priced out no — the price gave it

back and is holding the give-back.** MDB ran to its $444.72 high the day after the 2026-05-28

print, then fully retraced to ~$333 and has stabilized just above the 200-DMA. So you're no longer

paying the post-gap premium for evidence already in hand. The residual catalysts still ahead — a

quantified AI-revenue line, NDR back above 120%, an OEM/platform-embedding deal naming Atlas as

the agent data layer — are unbought. The old bull case is, unusually, still available near the old

price; the constraint is the regime and the dip base rate, not a consumed edge.

Council (one line each, no hedging)

  • Bull: Atlas at a $2B+ ARR run-rate growing 29% with vector search built in is the default

data layer for every AI agent reaching production — a compounding wedge toward $5B+ ARR,

available ~24% off its high at fair value after a pure round-trip that just held its 200-DMA.

  • Bear: Hyperscalers' Mongo-compatible APIs (DocumentDB, Cosmos, AlloyDB) commoditize the

moat at their economics; a 53× forward P/E leaves no cushion if growth slips toward the

bear-case 17%, and the no-cut/hike-tail regime compresses exactly this kind of multiple —

while MDB's own dips win only ~33% of the time at +63d.

  • First-principles: A ~20%-growth, ~18–20%-op-margin database at ~9× forward sales is fair,

not cheap; the edge is timing (pre-catalyst price, post-catalyst thesis), and a timing edge is

worth less when the entry is above your trigger and the macro clock (rates) ticks against the

multiple.

Entry levels the agent derives

Derived from this run's data. The 200-DMA shelf held, so I keep the buy-below just under it —

demanding a genuine retest/hold rather than chasing at $336, which is above the trigger with no

fresh catalyst.

Do not chase above ~$395 without a new catalyst (a disclosed AI revenue segment, NDR back

above 120%, or a major platform-embedding/OEM deal). Any entry should be small (high-P/S sizing

constraint in a no-rate-relief regime) and gated on a confirmed retest-hold of the ~$322–332

shelf — not at $336 at-market.

Macro fit

Kill-switches: All clear (capex +77% YoY, GPU pricing/lead-times firm/sold-out, HY OAS

~278bp). MDB is not a direct AI-infra pick-and-shovel — no GPU/capex J-curve exposure — so

kill-switch #1 is a demand tailwind check for Atlas, not a direct exposure. Hyperscalers keep

building supply-constrained → the agent/AI workloads that land on Atlas keep growing.

Discount-rate constraint (the binding factor, still against MDB): The June FOMC resolved

hawkish — no 2026 cuts in the base case, median dot 3.8%, a hike eyed for later in the year — and

the 06-30 macro read is a quiet continuation, if anything marginally more hawkish. The

cost-of-capital relief a 53× forward-P/E name would want to defend its multiple is not coming

in 2026. A sizing constraint and a persistent multiple-compression headwind, not a thesis break.

MDB is moderate-to-high P/S, less exposed than NBIS/IREN (its multiple rests more on real 25%

growth than duration, per the 2026-06-19 resolved theme), but not immune.

Regime: Market extended (forward P/E ~20–22×), breadth narrow, "demand a real dip OR fresh

catalyst; hold cash; keep high-P/S small" in force. MDB satisfies a partial dip leg and has a

confirmed (already-banked) catalyst — but the dip is shallow relative to fair value, the price is

now back above my trigger, and the macro clock cuts the wrong way for the multiple. The rule

lets you act on "real dip + confirmed catalyst," but the stance says do it small and on a

confirmed retest, not at $336 at-market.

Opportunity-cost rank vs. field: MDB remains clearly better-positioned than the extended

post-deal AI-infra names (IREN/NBIS/CRWV) that score LOW with no fresh catalyst — it has a

confirmed catalyst, a retraced fair price, and a multiple defensible on growth. Roughly in-line

with software peers (ESTC), richer than most. It does not clear the 70+ standout bar because (a)

the dip base rate is poor (33% win at +63d), (b) it's only ~14% below fair value (shallow, not a

deep dislocation), and (c) the entry now sits above the trigger with no fresh catalyst.

Score: 50 — top-of-the-watchlist accumulate-on-confirmation, a notch below last week's 52

because the actionable trigger ($325) is no longer in hand (price drifted up to $336) while

nothing new landed to justify paying up.

Falsification

30-day: Does the ~$315–322 200-DMA shelf hold on a retest, or does the dip-buyer base rate

reassert toward $300? A retest that holds near $322 (hawkish FOMC already absorbed) flips this

to a small BUY at the trigger. A break below $315 resets the entry materially lower and keeps it

HOLD.

60-day: Q2 FY2027 (2026-08-27) — does Atlas growth hold ≥27% YoY and op margin stay ≥18%?

Any quantification of AI-specific workload contribution, or NDR back above 120%, is the re-rate

catalyst → re-run with a BUY lean. Atlas growth below 25% re-rates lower.

90-day: Any hyperscaler/lab platform-embedding or OEM deal naming Atlas as the native

AI-agent data layer — the "wait-for-deal"-style event for this name. That moves the verdict to

BUY regardless of where the price sits.

Flip to AVOID if: Atlas net-dollar-retention falls below 115% for two consecutive quarters

(hyperscaler substitution accelerating) or management cuts/withdraws FY2027 guidance citing macro

Researched: 2026-06-22 · Verdict: HOLD · Conviction: 0.6 · Opportunity score: 50

Next earnings: 2026-08-03

Business

Two-platform enterprise software: Gotham (government/defense/intelligence — the data

ontology + operational layer behind mission-critical decisions) and Foundry (commercial),

with AIP (Artificial Intelligence Platform) now the growth engine, landing enterprises via

bootcamps and expanding via land-and-grab. Revenue is durable because it sits at the

orchestration layer (orchestration-vs-substrate) — the ontology/decision layer that

gets stickier the deeper it's embedded: multi-year sticky government contracts on one side,

high-NRR AIP expansion on the other. This is the intelligence layer that captures margin, not

the physical substrate that risks compression. Q1 FY26 (qtr end Mar): revenue **$1.633B, +85%

YoY** — the fastest growth in company history since the 2020 listing — US revenue +104%, US

commercial +133%, GAAP EPS $0.34 (beat $0.28).

Fresh thesis — is this a good buy now?

The setup is genuinely unusual for this field: **a pristine, accelerating compounder that is

DOWN, not extended.** PLTR sits at $119.5, ~42% off its high and 0.2% above its 52-week low,

with negative momentum across every window (−13% 20d, −23% 60d, −25% vs 200dma). Critically,

the drawdown is not a fundamental break — Q1 was the best growth quarter in company history

and management raised FY26 guide to +71%. The de-rate is driven by (1) **multiple

compression off an extreme starting valuation, (2) the SaaS-cohort fear** that AI agents

erode seat-based software economics (CRM, NOW, ZS, PANW all weak on the same screen), (3) a

name-specific headline — France's domestic intelligence service announced it will drop

Inverts the usual Wait-for-deal thesis frame: PLTR is not a deal name whose entry edge was

Business

Global REIT owning ~150k+ communications towers (macro sites leased to wireless carriers on long,

Towers are the cash engine: multi-tenant, mid-single-digit organic escalators, extremely sticky

tenants (carriers can't cheaply re-home radios). CoreSite is the growth kicker — its meet-me-room

APLDHOLDfetched48›

Research dossier: APLD — Applied Digital Corporation

Researched: 2026-07-03 · Verdict: BUY · Conviction: 0.55 · Opportunity score: 62

Next earnings: 2026-07-29

Business

Applied Digital builds and leases hyperscale AI/HPC data center campuses — a compute-infrastructure

landlord, not a cloud/GPU-rental operator (that business, Applied Digital's old bitcoin-hosting +

cloud-services lines, is now a rounding error next to the AI Factory leasing segment). The model:

acquire land + power interconnect in North Dakota/Texas-belt sites, build shell-and-core + power/

cooling infrastructure, sign long-term (15-year) triple-net-style take-or-pay leases with

investment-grade hyperscaler tenants for the critical IT load, and collect rent once each building

reaches Ready-for-Service (RFS). Revenue is contracted years before it's recognized — the backlog

is the business. As of early July 2026: 5 campuses (Polaris Forge 1 & 3, Delta Forge 1 & 2,

plus the original Ellendale/Harwood ND footprint), **1.4GW of contracted critical IT load / 2.15GW

of grid power, ~$36B base-term contracted revenue / ~$86B if all renewal options exercise**,

~70% of contracted revenue backed by US investment-grade hyperscalers. TTM revenue is only

$319M (+66% YoY) — the gap between $319M booked and $36B backed is the entire investment case: this

is early-innings monetization of an already-signed, already-investment-grade backlog, not a

speculative pipeline.

Who pays: undisclosed-by-name but described as "US-based, high investment-grade hyperscaler(s)" for

Delta Forge 2 ($5.2B/15-yr, 210MW, RFS ~1Q28) and Polaris Forge 3 ($7.5B base/$18.2B full, 300MW,

RFS ~Aug 2027) — plus CoreWeave at Polaris Forge 1 (150MW lease, now being restructured through a

CoreWeave special-purpose-vehicle subsidiary carrying an A3/Moody's-rated project facility, backed

by a springing parent guarantee and a $50M letter of credit — converting CoreWeave's non-investment-

grade (BB) parent credit into IG-rated project financing for APLD's own construction debt). Why the

revenue durably continues: 15-year take-or-pay leases with early-termination economics that make

walking away far more expensive than paying rent, IG counterparties, and — per

permitting-as-binding-constraint — APLD sits on the already-permitted side of the ledger

(Delta Forge 1 named explicitly in that framework), which is a scarcity premium every new local

moratorium elsewhere makes more valuable, not less.

Fresh thesis — is this a good buy now?

The stock is down ~22% over six sessions (52-wk high $50.73 → $33.07 at the 7/2 close, -34.8% off

high) while the fundamental tape over the same six weeks has been the best stretch in the

company's history: two new hyperscaler leases signed (Delta Forge 2 6/8, Polaris Forge 3 6/8-9,

lifting base backlog from ~$23.5B/3 campuses in May to ~$36B/5 campuses now), the CoreWeave lease

restructured onto an IG-rated SPV (lowers APLD's own project-debt cost), and Polaris Forge 1

Building 2 Phase-1 delivered RFS on schedule on 7/1 (75MW, 175MW now live at that campus alone).

No analyst has downgraded the name or cut a target through this drawdown — Northland ($82),

Lake Street ($90) and Craig-Hallum ($79) all raised targets in the first half of June, before

the slide started, and consensus (~11 analysts, "Strong Buy") sits at ~$73-84 against a $33 tape,

implying >100% upside if the sell-side view is even roughly right.

What actually happened: two distinct events compressed into one six-day candle. (1) **6/26,

idiosyncratic:** APLD amended its revolver to $430M (expandable to $550M, matures 2029) and lifted

its Series G convertible preferred commitment from $1.59B to $2.0B — read by the market as a

dilution/leverage headline days ahead of the print, not as the "funding secured for the buildout"

story management would tell. (2) 7/1-7/2, cohort/macro: a broad AI-infra/semiconductor selloff

(BofA's Bubble Risk Indicator flashing on tech/semis, a Broadcom AI-revenue-guidance miss erasing

~$1.3-1.4T of sector market cap in two sessions, SOX -7-10%) took CRWV -14% and NBIS -17% the same

day per the 2026-07-02 in-house decision log — APLD's move that day was beta, not alpha. The

in-house decision log already ran macro-cohort-confirmation on the 7/2 leg and confirmed it:

cohort down hard alongside APLD, no APLD-specific bad news that day, last name-specific news

(the PF1 Bldg2 RFS) was positive. That satisfies the dip-buy gate for the second leg of the drop.

The first leg (6/26-6/30) is real information, not pure liquidity — it's a legitimate dilution-risk

repricing, and the entry framework below prices it in rather than dismissing it.

Net: this reads as a genuine dip in a name whose fundamentals just got materially better, not

a broken thesis re-rating down to match a real deterioration. The market hasn't yet given credit

for two consecutive quarters of on-schedule execution and $12.5B of net-new backlog signed in the

last month; it's instead pricing near-term dilution optics and importing sector-wide bubble fear.

Valuation + position vs. range

Market cap ~$9.4-9.5B at $33.07 (share count 285.8M, +28% YoY — heavy issuance, flagged below)

against $36B base-term / $86B fully-exercised backlog — roughly 0.26x base backlog, or

~0.11x on the full-exercise figure. TTM revenue $319M (+66% YoY); Q3 FY26 alone was $126.6M

(+139% YoY) with GAAP net loss -$100.9M but adjusted EBITDA +$44.1M — the GAAP loss is

depreciation/interest-heavy construction accounting, not an operating problem (adjusted EBITDA

is solidly positive and scaling). No meaningful P/S read is useful here given revenue is a tiny

fraction of contracted backlog; the ai-capex-payback-window J-curve frame applies in reverse-form:

capex is heavy and FCF is deeply negative (-$1.8B, per public trackers) because the company is

building out already-leased capacity, not speculatively. Balance sheet: ~$2.1B cash, ~$2.7B debt,

no significant maturities in the next two years, plus ~$4.1B of additional lined-up financing

capacity (the $2.0B Series G ceiling + $430-550M revolver + project-level debt facilities) —

liquidity is not the near-term risk the -22% price action implies.

Position vs. range: $33.07 sits 34.8% below the 52-week high and >250% above the 52-week low

($9.02) — nowhere near "extended," and roughly at or just above the last-computed 200DMA (~$30.6

as of 6/12; the window has likely drifted up toward the low-$30s since, given how much higher

2026 prices have been than the year-ago base it's rolling off). This is a name testing a real

technical support level with fundamentals that improved into the test, not a name chasing new

highs.

Post-deal / repriced check

Per Wait-for-deal thesis, APLD's original wait-for-deal edge (mystery hyperscaler at Delta

Forge 1) was already consumed by mid-2026 (W18) — this is unambiguously a post-deal name, and in

fact a serially post-deal one: Delta Forge 2 and Polaris Forge 3 landed in the last month on top

of that. The framework's three post-deal patterns (immediate compression / sustained repricing /

sell-the-news) don't cleanly fit here — this is a fourth shape: **deal-flow kept compounding while

the multiple de-rated.** Backlog is up ~53% (~$23.5B → ~$36B) since the last name-specific dip-buy

read in May, while the stock is down from its 52-week high. That's the opposite of "the entry edge

is consumed" — if anything a fresh entry edge has opened up because price and backlog have

diverged. The risk to that read: the de-rating isn't pure sentiment. The 6/26 financing amendment

is a real fact pattern (heavier preferred/revolver capacity, active issuance) that a rational market

partially prices as dilution risk, and the C13 - Asset Growth and Capex Caution /

C14 - Share Issuance and Dilution Drag empirical priors say aggressive-asset-growth,

heavy-issuance names underperform on average — 28% share growth YoY and a rising preferred stack

are exactly the profile those anomalies flag. APLD's exception case, per C14's own "is this

issuance funding demand, or funding dilution?" test: the backlog is contracted, 70% IG-hyperscaler-

backed, take-or-pay — this reads as demand-matched capex, the exception condition the anomaly

allows for, not empire-building. That's a judgment call, not a certainty, and it's the single

biggest thing that could be wrong about this verdict.

Council (one line each, no hedging)

NFLXHOLDfetched48›

Research dossier: NFLX — Netflix, Inc.

Researched: 2026-06-22 · Verdict: HOLD · Conviction: 0.45 · Opportunity score: 48

Next earnings: 2026-07-16

Business

Subscription streaming at global scale — ~325M paid memberships (the company no longer

reports the quarterly number) paying monthly for a content library + originals. Revenue is

recurring and sticky; the durable engine now has three legs: (1) core subscription pricing

power, (2) the paid-sharing/extra-member crackdown that converted freeloaders into payers, and

(3) a fast-ramping ad tier guided to ~$3B in 2026 (a doubling YoY). TTM revenue $46.89B

(+16.7%), net income $13.37B (~28.5% net margin), operating margin ~32%. This is a

cash-generative, high-margin compounder — not a capex J-curve name and not AI-infra.

Fresh thesis — is this a good buy now?

At $72.88 the stock is down ~42% off its highs and sitting ~1.5% above its 52-week low — a

real dip, not an extended chase. The fall is a cluster of name-specific sentiment shocks,

not a fundamental break: (1) Netflix lost the Roku bidding war to Fox — its second failed

high-profile M&A pursuit, raising capital-allocation questions; (2) co-founder/board chair Reed

Hastings declined re-election at the June 4 AGM, which LightShed's Greenfield said is "spooking

investors"; (3) Q2 guidance came in light ($12.57B rev vs $12.63B consensus; $0.78 EPS vs

$0.84); (4) Meta expanding Instagram-for-TV to Samsung sets in the US adds a competitive

overhang. None of these breaks the earnings engine — revenue still +16.7%, margins still

expanding, ad business still doubling. So the business is fine; the narrative is wounded. The

buy case is "quality compounder on sale into a management transition." The reason to wait: the

catalysts are recent and unresolved (leadership, M&A discipline, competition), the print is 24

days out and just guided down, and the dip-buy base rate for this name is poor.

Valuation + position vs. range

  • Price $72.88 · market cap $306.88B · 4.21B shares · P/E 24.99 · forward P/E 23.28 ·

TTM rev $46.89B. (All fetched from stockanalysis.com — the feed price and reported

fundamentals reconcile cleanly: 4.21B × $72.88 = $306.9B.)

  • Position vs range: −45.7% off the 52-week high, +1.5% above the 52-week low, −17.8% vs

50DMA, −25.7% vs 200DMA, −21% over 60 trading days. Implied 200DMA ≈ $98, 52w high ≈ $134.

It is essentially at the bottom of its range and still falling (−5.8% on the research day).

  • Fair-value read: a 16% grower with expanding ~32% operating margin and a doubling ad leg

trading at forward P/E ~23 — i.e. roughly the market's multiple (S&P fwd P/E ~22.5) for a

faster, higher-margin grower — is fair-to-slightly-cheap. Sell-side mean target $114

(+56%) is aspirational but directionally says the de-rate overshot the fundamentals. Much of

the good news (ad ramp) is known; the bad news (mgmt transition, M&A whiffs, competition)

is fresh and not yet resolved, which is why the multiple compressed rather than the estimates.

Post-deal / repriced check

Inverse of the usual AI-infra problem: this isn't a post-deal name whose edge was consumed by a

landed partnership — it's a name repriced DOWN by a string of negatives, two of them

failed deals (Roku to Fox). The entry edge here would come from the de-rate being an

overreaction, not from a catalyst already in the price. The risk is the mirror image: the

negative catalysts (leadership uncertainty, competitive overhang) may not be fully discounted

yet, and a light-guided print into July 16 carries downside PEAD risk.

Council (one line each, no hedging)

  • Bull: A 16%-growth, 32%-margin global monopoly-ish compounder with a doubling ad business

just got marked down 42% on a CEO-succession scare and two failed acquisitions — the cash

machine is untouched and you're paying a market multiple for a better-than-market business.

  • Bear: Two failed M&A pursuits plus the founder walking off the board signal a company

unsure of its next act while Meta/YouTube attack the ad-supported tier — a falling knife at

its 52-week low that just guided down, and the dip historically keeps bleeding.

  • First-principles: At ~$307B for ~$13B of growing net income (P/E ~25) you're buying a

durable subscription annuity at a fair price; the business makes sense from scratch — the open

question is entry timing, not whether to own it.

still open, the dip thesis is gone and you're just chasing.

Macro fit

  • Kill-switches (AI-infra) — N/A: NFLX is not a compute/power/land name, so the three

kill-switches don't bind. The relevant macro overlay is the rate/regime read: market

extended (S&P fwd P/E ~22.5), Fed hawkish (no cuts in 2026, hike tail). A profitable,

Research dossier: VST — Vistra Corp.

Researched: 2026-06-27 · Verdict: HOLD · Conviction: 0.58 · Opportunity score: 48

Next earnings: 2026-08-06

Business

Largest US competitive (merchant) power producer + retail electricity. ~41 GW of generation —

nuclear (Comanche Peak TX; PJM fleet incl. Energy Harbor), natural gas, coal, solar/storage —

plus the TXU/Dynegy retail book serving ~5M+ customers. Two revenue engines: (1) merchant energy

+ capacity margins on the fleet, (2) the retail spread. The durable case is the structural

shortage of dispatchable, already-interconnected baseload against AI/data-center + electrification

demand. VST owns existing carbon-free nuclear hyperscalers want on 20-year contracts and cannot

build themselves — the AI Energy Thesis "owns the scarce, already-permitted generation" lane

(long permitting-as-binding-constraint, not short it).

Fresh thesis — is this a good buy now?

A genuinely fresh catalyst landed since my last dive — and the stock did not chase it. On

June 22 Vistra was named the **preferred power provider and a $1B founding investor in Helix

Digital Infrastructure**, a ~$10B AI data-center venture backed by KKR, NVIDIA, and the Kuwait

Investment Authority. That is exactly the kind of new, discrete item my 2026-06-20 dossier said

would flip the thesis — yet VST sits at $163.49, essentially flat vs the $163.75 I logged a

week ago (and slightly down on the day). So per extended-with-vs-without-catalyst this is the

cleaner half of the rule: new positive information with no repricing chase. That nudges

conviction up.

But I won't over-reward it. Helix is a $1B equity outlay + "preferred provider" status, not a

signed PPA with locked MW / price / term — strategic optionality and near-term FCF drag, not

contracted cash flow yet. The market's non-reaction reads as "interesting, not yet accretive,"

which is the right read. And the stock is still only ~4% below my fair value with a live PJM

capacity-collar overhang. So: improving setup, but not a buy at $163 with no real dip and the

hard accretive catalysts (Cogentrix close, Perry, Q2 guide) still ahead. HOLD, modestly more

constructive than June.

Research dossier: CCJ — Cameco Corporation

Researched: 2026-06-27 · Verdict: HOLD · Conviction: 0.62 · Opportunity score: 47

Next earnings: 2026-07-30

Business

Cameco is the largest publicly traded Western uranium producer, mining the highest-grade

deposits on Earth (Athabasca Basin: McArthur River/Key Lake, Cigar Lake). Three legs:

(1) uranium — production sold mostly under multi-year, market-related utility contracts;

(2) fuel services — UF6/UO2 conversion and CANDU fuel bundles; (3) Westinghouse — its

49% stake (Brookfield 51%) in the reactor-technology business: AP1000 new-build, fuel

fabrication, and reactor servicing. Revenue durability is high: nuclear is must-run baseload,

fuel demand is inelastic once a plant is committed, and utilities contract years forward.

FY2026 guidance reaffirmed at Q1: 29–32M lbs uranium delivered, with the contracted book and

the now-financed AP1000 deployment pipeline giving multi-year forward visibility. (stockanalysis.com

shows TTM revenue $2.53B / net income $466M; lower than the CAD operating run-rate because of

CAD/USD translation and equity-method Westinghouse treatment.)

Fresh thesis — is this a good buy now?

The AI→nuclear→uranium chain is real, confirmed, and still strengthening, and this run a **fresh,

material catalyst landed that the June-20 dossier explicitly said was missing: on 2026-06-23 the

US DOE conditionally committed up to $17.5B** in government-backed loans to finance Westinghouse's

purchase of long-lead items for up to 10 AP1000 reactors (five two-unit projects), each jointly

owned by Westinghouse + a utility partner with $500M equity each, committed upfront. Energy

Secretary Wright framed it as cutting construction cost and **accelerating deployment by up to three

years**. This de-risks the single most cost-of-capital-sensitive piece of CCJ's optionality — the

AP1000 financing — and Cameco owns 49% of the vendor. Per extended-with-vs-without-catalyst this

is the favorable shape: a thesis-confirming fact appeared in the last 7–14 days, **and the stock did

not run on it — CCJ was $108.89 on the June-23 announcement day and is back to $104.49 (June-26)**,

below the June-20 level, sitting at the 200DMA. So you'd get the catalyst without paying up.

The discipline check keeps this a HOLD, not a BUY. The loan is **debt financing for Westinghouse

projects, not free money to Cameco**, and it requires Westinghouse to commit equity. Westinghouse

still posted a net loss ($46M Cameco share) in Q1 — the AP1000 platform is optionality, not yet

an earnings engine. Layered on a 71x forward / 97x trailing multiple for a commodity-linked

business, into a no-cut/hike-tail regime that compresses exactly those long-duration multiples, the

catalyst improves the quality and durability of the case more than it creates near-term value. The

pullback to the 200DMA is real and the entry-shape base rate is genuinely good — but at $104.49 the

stock is around fair, not at a margin-of-safety price.

Research dossier: FSLR — First Solar

Researched: 2026-06-25 · Verdict: HOLD · Conviction: 0.63 · Opportunity score: 47

Next earnings: 2026-07-23

Business

First Solar is the largest US-domiciled solar module maker, building thin-film cadmium-telluride (CdTe) panels rather than the polysilicon modules that dominate globally — a technology that structurally sidesteps the silicon trade war. Revenue is ~96% US, from domestic fabs in Ohio, Alabama, and Louisiana plus older Malaysia/Vietnam capacity. Customers are utility-scale developers and corporate clean-energy buyers on multi-year, fixed-price, take-or-pay contracts — unusual forward visibility for a manufacturer. The P&L rides two US policy levers: (1) Section 45X manufacturing credits (~$0.17/W thin-film; FY26 guide >$2.1B, ~78% of the ~$2.8B EBITDA guide), booked directly and sold for cash (>$2.0B of 45X credits transferred to date, incl. a ~$775M deal); (2) tariffs (Section 201 — now expired Feb 2026 — plus 301, AD/CVD, and a pending Section 232 polysilicon case) that wall off Chinese/SE-Asian supply. Demand durability is real (contracted backlog + AI-driven electricity growth); the risk is concentrated in policy, not end-demand.

Fresh thesis — is this a good buy now?

Same headline as the 2026-06-17 dossier — HOLD that is close to a trigger-gated BUY — but the setup has marginally improved on three fronts, none yet sufficient to buy at-market:

1. The 45X tail is now essentially closed, not just shrinking. The OBBBA (signed July 2025) leaves the solar 45X manufacturing-credit phase-out unchanged while letting the project-side 48E/45Y credits expire quickly. The 6/8 dossier's "45X repealed → $120–160" bear case is no longer a live tail — the central legislative risk is resolved in FSLR's favor. The residual medium-term risk is unchanged: project-credit phaseout throttles 2027+ US solar deployment demand, cushioned near-term by the contracted backlog.

2. Sell-side moved above spot. The broad consensus (MarketBeat 47-analyst set) is now ~$277 with 30 Buy / 5 Hold / 1 Sell, after UBS→$330 (6/11) and Mizuho→$300 (6/15), both citing Section 232. On 6/17 consensus sat below spot; it now sits ~11% above it. (The narrower stockanalysis 35-analyst set is still ~$247 — right at spot — so read this as a sell-side that's split, with the catalyst-believers pulling the broad number up.)

3. A deeper pullback into the trigger zone. FSLR is $248.54, −9.2% over 20d and 22.4% off the 52w high — a cohort-wide, macro-driven move (hawkish FOMC multiple compression) compounded by an insider/policy-uncertainty headline, not a thesis break (macro-cohort-confirmation). The 50dma (~$241) and 200dma (~$234) now sit just below — a pullback to ~$236 reaches the DMA cluster and my buy-below.

Against that: macro stays hawkish (no 2026 cut, hiking bias) — a multiple-compression headwind for any high-duration name; at $248.54 the stock is still above my $236 buy-below; and the Section 232 catalyst remains unfired (extended-with-vs-without-catalyst: no thesis-confirming fact has landed in the last 7–14 days — the ruling is pending, not made). So the gate ("real dip OR fresh catalyst") is almost met, not met. HOLD, with two clean BUY triggers below.

Agreement/disagreement with the 6/17 dossier: I agree with its core read and carry the same HOLD. I push slightly higher on conviction (0.62→0.63) and opportunity score (44→47) because the 45X tail is now closed (vs. "shrinking but pending") and sell-side has crossed above spot. I keep HOLD because at-market is still above my derived buy-below into a hawkish tape with the catalyst unfired.

Valuation + position vs. range

  • Price (2026-06-25): $248.54 · Market cap: ~$26.7B

  • TTM: revenue $5.42B (+27%), net income $1.67B, EPS $15.48 · P/E 16.1× · forward P/E 12.9× · P/S ~4.9×
  • Normalized (ex-45X): still rich (~$5–7 EPS → ~40×+), BUT with the solar 45X phase-out now legislatively unchanged, the credit is closer to a structural feature than a one-year transfer at risk — the normalization haircut applies less than the 6/8 run assumed.

GAAP-cheap (~13× fwd), normalized-expensive — and the policy clarity tilts the read further toward "the GAAP number is the more real one."

Post-deal / repriced check

Not a wait-for-deal name. The relevant question: is the catalyst consumed? The Q1 beat (record $1.04B rev, 47% GM) is fully absorbed, and the 45X legislative outcome is now largely settled (priced as durable). But the Section 232 polysilicon decision is a fresh, unpriced binary still live in its window (Commerce report was due ~late March 2026; the President has up to 90 days → a ruling can land any day, plausibly late June). Per extended-with-vs-without-catalyst, no thesis-confirming fact has landed in the last 7–14 days, so at-market is not a repricing entry. A favorable Section 232 ruling would be a fresh catalyst that flips this to BUY at the prevailing price.

Council (one line each, no hedging)

  • Bear: Project-side ITC/PTC phaseout (0% by 2029) throttles the US solar-deployment demand FSLR's backlog ultimately depends on, and a hawkish Fed compresses the multiple on an ex-credit business still worth ~40×.

Revenue durability rests on (1) AI data-center networking spend persisting — kill-switch #1 is

from a normal dip, not the deal" pattern. This is that dip. The remaining un-priced catalysts

are the Aug-6 Q2 ramp confirmation and an eventual Oracle qualification — neither in hand.

Council (one line each, no hedging)

  • Bull: A pure-play 800G/1.6T supplier with a $324M+ order book and 1.6T shipments starting

Research dossier: CRM — Salesforce, Inc.

Researched: 2026-06-12 · Verdict: HOLD · Conviction: 0.42 · Opportunity score: 45

Next earnings: 2026-08-26

Business

Enterprise SaaS incumbent: Sales/Service/Marketing clouds, Data Cloud, Tableau, MuleSoft,

Slack, now Agentforce (agentic AI). Customers pay recurring per-seat subscriptions; revenue is

sticky (multi-year enterprise contracts, switching costs, embedded workflow). TTM revenue

$42.8B, ~90% subscription. This is application/orchestration layer, not AI-infra

picks-and-shovels — the AI-infra kill-switches (capex, GPU, credit) don't gate it. Its swing

factor is whether agentic AI is a tailwind (new monetization) or a solvent (seat erosion).

Fresh thesis — is this a good buy now?

The setup is a genuine value dislocation, rare in this field: a quality compounder trading at

~12× forward earnings / ~10% FCF yield, near its 52-week low, after a ~33–38% YTD drawdown.

Q1 FY27 (reported 2026-05-27) was a clean beat — revenue $11.1B (+13% YoY), non-GAAP EPS $3.88

(+50%), FY27 guide raised to $45.9–46.2B, and a $25B accelerated buyback that puts a real

floor under the share count. Agentforce ARR hit $1.2B (+205% YoY), and 7 of the 10 largest Q1

deals added seats — directly contradicting the disruption narrative. The market is pricing

terminal-value impairment into a business still growing double digits and gushing cash.

The catch: the de-rate is a thesis-narrative dip ("SaaSpocalypse"), not a liquidity dip.

Per macro-cohort-confirmation, cohort-wide weakness driven by information (will AI agents

collapse seat-based SaaS?) changes the thesis rather than simply creating a clean entry.

Organic growth has decelerated to ~9% (Tableau/marketing/commerce soft), Agentforce is still

<3% of revenue, and the resolving catalyst — proof of H2 reacceleration or Agentforce reaching

material scale — has not landed. So: cheap and well-defended, but the overhang is real and

unresolved. Not a high-conviction at-market buy here.

Valuation + position vs. range

Not applicable in the Wait-for-deal thesis sense — there's no lab-partnership deal to

Research dossier: QCOM — Qualcomm Incorporated

Researched: 2026-06-27 · Verdict: HOLD · Conviction: 0.62 · Opportunity score: 45

Business

Qualcomm sells (a) QCT — Snapdragon SoCs/modems for handsets, plus fast-growing

automotive (digital cockpit/ADAS) and IoT/edge silicon; and (b) QTL — a high-margin

patent-licensing business that taxes essentially every 3G/4G/5G device. Handsets still pay most

of the bills (~$6.0B of $10.6B Q2 rev) but the franchise is structurally shrinking: **Apple

in-houses its modem fully by 2027** (~$5.7–5.9B/yr, ~13% of the top line, gone) and China Android

is soft. The forward story is a pivot into automotive, IoT, and a brand-new AI data-center line

(custom CPU + inference accelerators, HBM-free "High Bandwidth Compute") to replace the handset

decline. As of the June 24 Investor Day that pivot now has **named hyperscaler anchors and dated

targets** — the key change since the prior dossier.

Fresh thesis — is this a good buy now?

Closer than it was, but not yet. The prior dossier (2026-06-20, $226, HOLD) called the setup

exactly: it predicted that if the June 24 Investor Day delivered targets not landed revenue, a

sell-the-news de-rate toward $180–190 would follow and that dip would be the buy. The event

landed June 24 (initial pop to ~$215.50, then an ~8% single-day reversal and −5.12% on June 26),

arrived. But it has brought the stock to my fair value, not below it — there is no margin of

Research dossier: CAMT — Camtek Ltd

Researched: 2026-06-27 · Verdict: HOLD · Conviction: 0.68 · Opportunity score: 44

Next earnings: 2026-08-11 (estimated — Q1 reported 2026-05-12; Camtek reports ~mid-August; AGM set July 29)

Business

Camtek is an Israeli semiconductor-equipment company selling inspection and metrology systems

for advanced packaging. Two platforms carry the story: Hawk (3D metrology — bump height,

warpage, co-planarity for HBM and chiplet stacks) and Eagle / Eagle G5 (2D optical

inspection for packaging lines). Customers are OSATs (ASE-, Amkor-type assembly/test houses)

across Asia and the leading HBM makers (Micron, SK Hynix, Samsung-adjacent). Revenue is durable

as long as the HBM/advanced-packaging cycle holds: AI accelerators require 3D inspection at every

stack and there is no substitute for that step. Management positions Camtek as "the tool of

reference for 3D metrology," with the Hawk + Eagle-G5 mix tracking toward ≥50% of 2026 revenue

(vs ~30% in 2025). The small Visual Layer tuck-in (AI vision/data-curation, closed ~Q1) is

optionality toward a future inspection-software layer, not yet a revenue line.

FY2025 revenue ~$496M (+16%). Q1 2026 (reported May 12): revenue $121.7M, non-GAAP EPS $0.70,

non-GAAP gross margin 51.0%, GAAP net income $31.6M. Q2 2026 guide $129–131M; H2 2026 guided

25% above H1 → implied full-year run-rate ~$565–570M. On the Q1 call management flagged **>$260M

of 2026–27 expected revenue already in hand** as orders/forecasts from two HBM makers — the order

book is the durable part of the story. June 2 disclosed a $105M+ order ($55M tier-1 OSAT +

$50M+ Hawk from a leading HBM maker, 2027 delivery).

Fresh thesis — is this a good buy now?

Closer than a week ago, but not yet. The prior dossier (2026-06-20, $195, HOLD/27) passed CAMT

for one reason: it was defending highs above the Street with no fresh catalyst, and the

macro give-back the lens wanted hadn't arrived. The give-back has now arrived. CAMT is

~$153.7, down ~21% from $195. The cause is a cohort-wide late-June semiconductor selloff

(Nasdaq -4% in a session, ~$1.3T of semi cap erased; trigger = Broadcom's soft Q3 AI-chip guide

plus a commodity-memory/smartphone-demand crisis). Per macro-cohort-confirmation this is a

liquidity event, not CAMT-specific information — exactly the kind of dip that creates an

entry rather than denies one.

Two things genuinely improved: (1) at $153.7 the stock now trades ~18% below the $187

consensus (it was ~4% above it at $195), and (2) the forward multiple compressed from ~50x to

~39x. The HBM shortage the thesis rests on is intact — the "memory crisis" in the headlines is

commodity DRAM/NAND + smartphones, not HBM; HBM stays sold out through 2027 (Micron 2026

sold out, SK Hynix tight through 2027), and CAMT's $260M+ booked HBM demand sits inside that.

So why still HOLD, not BUY? Three things hold the verdict back:

1. It's at fair value, not below it. My fair band is $155–165; $153.7 is the low edge of

fair, ~10–15% above my buy-below ($130–140). The high-conviction add zone hasn't printed.

2. A fresh fundamental yellow flag specific to CAMT's 2027 thesis. SK Hynix is reportedly

decelerating HBM4 mass-production expansion — redirecting capacity to higher-margin general

DRAM after a downward revision to Nvidia "Rubin" build forecasts. That is new information

(not liquidity), and it points squarely at the pace of the 2027 HBM4 tool demand CAMT's order

book is levered to. Not a thesis break (shortages persist through 2027, orders are in hand),

but it nicks the conviction that made the prior dossier comfortable.

3. This name punishes knife-catching. The dip_buyer base rate is the active preset today and

it's weak (below).

Net: the entry is approaching, and a cohort dip into a below-consensus price is the right

direction — but at fair value, mid-fall, with the active base rate against me and a genuine

HBM4-pace question newly on the table, the disciplined move is to gate the buy at $130–140 (or

on a clean Q2 order-book confirmation) rather than chase the knife here.

Valuation + position vs. range

NEEHOLDfetched44›

Research dossier: NEE — NextEra Energy, Inc.

Researched: 2026-06-13 · Verdict: HOLD · Conviction: 0.55 · Opportunity score: 44

Next earnings: 2026-07-24

Business

Two engines under one holdco. FPL (Florida Power & Light) — the largest US regulated

electric utility by retail sales, a low-cost, rate-base-growth monopoly serving ~12M Floridians;

the steady, credit-quality cash flow. NextEra Energy Resources (NEER) — the largest developer

of wind/solar/storage in the world, ~33 GW renewables+storage backlog, increasingly the

unregulated growth engine selling power into the AI/data-center load surge. Newer legs: a DoC

award to build 9.5 GW of gas for large loads in TX/PA, plus a 30-GW data-center "hub"

ambition by 2035. Revenue durably continues because both legs are long the single biggest

secular demand story in US power — data-center load growth that the grid physically can't keep up

with (AI Energy Thesis).

Fresh thesis — is this a good buy now?

The whole question right now is the Dominion acquisition (announced 2026-05-18). NEE is buying

Dominion in a $66.8B all-stock deal — 0.8138 NEE shares per D share plus a pro-rata slice of a

$360M cash payment, ~23% premium to D's pre-announcement close, largest utility merger on record,

expected to close mid-to-late 2027. NEE has slid ~10% over 20 days on the announcement.

The strategic logic is genuinely strong, not an empire grab: Dominion owns Data Center Alley

(Northern Virginia, the world's largest data-center market) — data centers were ~28% of its

Virginia sales last year and it carries a ~51 GW contracted data-center pipeline. Combined

large-load pipeline ~130 GW. And per Bloomberg the deal is credit-accretive: NEE had been

leaning on unregulated NEER earnings, drifting toward downgrade thresholds; bolting on Dominion's

regulated VA rate base shores up the credit profile. So the sell-off is the mechanical all-stock

dilution trade-down that acquirers reliably take on signing — not the market judging the deal

value-destructive.

But that does not make it a buy here for the marginal $100. The drop is name-specific (this

deal), not a cohort-wide dip — it fails the macro-cohort-confirmation test for dip-buying.

The deal injects ~18 months of regulatory binary (FERC + Virginia SCC + DOJ on the

largest-ever utility merger; VA SCC + data-center-rate politics is a live concession/break risk),

which caps re-rating until clarity lands. And the timing is poor: the **FOMC dot plot is 3 days

out (Jun 16-17)** and NEE is a rate-sensitive bond-proxy (2.9% yield) — exactly the kind of name a

hawkish "no cuts until 2027" dot pressures. Quality name, real pullback, wrong moment to commit at

market.

Valuation + position vs. range

  • Price $85.99; mkt cap ~$179B; trailing P/E 21.8×, forward P/E ~21.2×; div yield 2.90%

($2.49); EPS $3.94; revenue $27.9B TTM; ~2.09B shares. 52-wk range $67.20–$98.75; ~13% off

the high, ~28% above the low, sitting right on its 200DMA (-5.7% vs 50DMA after the deal slide).

  • Guidance: 2026 adj EPS $3.92–$4.02 (targeting high end); reaffirmed **≥8% adj-EPS CAGR

through 2032 and 2035** off the 2025 base of $3.71. Q1'26 adj EPS $1.09 beat $0.97 (+10% YoY).

  • My fair-value read: an 8%+ regulated-quality grower with a real AI-power tailwind earns

~20–22× forward EPS; on ~$3.97 that's ~$79–$87 before the deal overhang. Applying a

deal-uncertainty/dilution discount, fair ≈ $84 — i.e. roughly where it trades. The ~10%

drop looks like a fair repricing for dilution + 18-month deal risk, not a gift; analyst PTs

($98.47 avg, BMO $104) price the deal as cleanly accretive and the regulatory path as smooth,

which I think is too optimistic on timing.

Post-deal / repriced check

Different flavor of "deal" than Wait-for-deal thesis (which is about small/mid infra

pre-lab-partnership). Here NEE is the acquirer and the catalyst is dilutive M&A. The entry

edge is not consumed in the usual sense — if anything the stock is cheaper post-announcement —

but the name is now a special situation: an 18-month merger-arb-flavored overhang sits on top

of the operating story. You're no longer buying a clean compounder; you're buying a compounder

plus a regulatory binary that won't resolve until 2027. Size and timing must respect that.

Trigger-gated, not at-market: the base-rate work (below) wins on dips but the entry needs a

trigger given the name-specific drop and macro overhang.

Macro fit

  • Kill-switches: none tripped — capex +77% YoY and still being raised, GPU lead times still

Research dossier: VRT — Vertiv Holdings Co.

Researched: 2026-06-16 · Verdict: HOLD · Conviction: 0.66 · Opportunity score: 44

Next earnings: 2026-07-29

Business

Vertiv makes and services the physical guts of a data center: the power layer (UPS, PDUs,

switchgear, transfer switches) and the thermal layer (air cooling and, increasingly, direct

liquid cooling / DLC), plus integrated rack systems and monitoring software. Customers are

hyperscalers, colos (Equinix, Digital Realty), and enterprise IT. Roughly 78% critical

infrastructure/solutions, 22% integrated rack.

Why the revenue durably continues: GB200/B200-class AI racks pull 60–120 kW vs 5–10 kW for

legacy racks — a 10–20× density step-up that forces a full power+cooling re-spec (higher-rated

PDUs, DLC instead of CRAC air, new switchgear). It is not substitutable with a software

workaround; every AI cluster needs this gear from someone. The $15B+ backlog (12–18 months of

forward revenue) is contracted, not aspirational. Vertiv is also vertically integrating the

thermal stack: ThermoKey (heat-exchangers/AI cooling) closed June 12, 2026, following the

Strategic Thermal Labs deal — building DLC depth ahead of the liquid-cooling transition.

Fresh thesis — is this a good buy now?

The picks-and-shovels case is intact and unusually clean: Vertiv wins regardless of which lab

or model wins, hyperscaler capex is ~$725B in 2026 (+77% YoY, kill-switch #1 nowhere near

tripping), and VRT sits directly in the spend path with positive earnings and a real backlog —

higher quality than most of the post-deal AI-infra field.

But "good business" ≠ "good entry today," and two things hold me at HOLD:

1. Price is at fair value, not below it. At $299.60 (forward P/E ~44×, ~47× on the $6.35

FY26 adj-EPS midpoint), the margin of safety is thin. With rate cuts now being removed,

not added, multiple expansion is not the path — you'd be underwriting 30%+ growth into a

44× multiple and relying on earnings to do all the work.

2. A dated, name-relevant macro headwind lands tomorrow. The June 17 FOMC dot plot is

expected to erase the last 2026 cut (CPI back up to ~4.2%); that pressures exactly the

high-P/S cohort VRT lives in. The macro stance is explicit — don't pre-position into the

print. Buying a fair-value 44× name the session before a known hawkish binary is the wrong

side of the odds.

No fresh thesis-confirming catalyst landed in the last 7–14 days — ThermoKey closing is an

incremental thermal bolt-on, not a step-change. Per extended-with-vs-without-catalyst, with

no new business fact the "wait for a real dip" frame is the correct one, not FOMO.

The constructive nuance: the dip-buyer base rate for this name is genuinely good

(below), and tomorrow's dot plot could itself trigger a cohort-wide high-P/S selloff. A

macro-driven cohort dip (not name-specific bad news) is precisely the macro-cohort-confirmation

green light to buy a dip. So this is HOLD-with-a-trigger, not HOLD-and-forget: a flush toward

$265–280 on a hawkish print, or a Q2 beat+raise July 29, flips it.

Verdict: HOLD. Own-worthy business; the price doesn't demand buying today, and tomorrow's

event could hand a better one.

LITEHOLDfetched43›

Research dossier: LITE — Lumentum Holdings, Inc.

Researched: 2026-06-27 · Verdict: HOLD · Conviction: 0.6 · Opportunity score: 43

Next earnings: 2026-08-18

Business

Lumentum makes the photonic components that move data inside and between AI datacenters with

light instead of copper: EML and DML laser chips, indium-phosphide (InP) transceivers, VCSELs,

and optical circuit switches (OCS). Who pays them: hyperscalers and networking OEMs building

400G/800G/1.6T optical interconnect, plus a mature/declining tail in telecom ROADMs and Apple

3D-sensing VCSELs. Why the revenue durably continues: as GPU clusters scale past 100K toward

1M+ accelerators, copper physically cannot carry the per-lane bandwidth — the migration to

optics is forced by physics, not preference. Lumentum holds an estimated **50–60% share of

EMLs and is the only vendor shipping 200G-per-lane EMLs at volume**, with demand running

~25–30% ahead of supply. It sits squarely in the current binding AI-infra bottleneck.

Fresh thesis — is this a good buy now?

The business case is unchanged and strong: the optical-interconnect supercycle is real, the

800G→1.6T transition is the near-term revenue driver, and Q4 FY26 is guided to a record

$960M–$1.01B with a 35–36% non-GAAP op-margin step and 1.6T transceivers ramping in the

quarter. Nothing in the fundamentals has broken since the June-19 read.

What has changed is the tape. LITE is **~$817 (June 26 close), down ~12.6% on the month and

~5.4% on the week**, caught in a broad AI-photonics/semis selloff triggered by a report that

OpenAI may delay its IPO to 2027 (weighing a sub-$1T print now vs. waiting, spooked by

SpaceX's post-listing plunge). That is a **sentiment/cohort de-rate on a financing-timeline

headline, not a thesis break** — none of the three AI-infra kill-switches moved. So the stock

has done exactly what the June-19 falsification flagged ("a real pullback into the buy zone on a

cohort de-rate flips this toward BUY") — it's started the move, but it isn't there yet. At $817

the stock is in the middle of my fair-value band, ~18–28% above the buy-below zone, and the

dip is young. My answer: HOLD, but a constructive HOLD — this is the right name moving in the

right direction for the right (non-fundamental) reason; wait for it to reach the trigger rather

than catch a falling knife mid-range into a hawkish tape with a binary print seven weeks out.

Engaging the user's 2026-04-28 dive (WATCH @ $797): its core call has aged well and I still

agree with it — the wait-for-deal trigger (NVIDIA stake, S&P inclusion) had already fired, so

chasing the gap was a catalyst-payoff-shape-sizing anti-pattern, and the post-print fade it

emphasis, same as June 19: their P/S-bear $558 band assumed a deeper retrace than the business

has since warranted — revenue/margin execution only strengthened — so my buy-below sits a notch

thesis-scare, not just the cohort wobble we're in.

Research dossier: NXPI — NXP Semiconductors N.V.

Researched: 2026-06-26 · Verdict: HOLD · Conviction: 0.45 · Opportunity score: 43

Next earnings: 2026-07-28

Business

NXP is a #1/#2 global supplier of automotive and industrial/IoT embedded processing, analog,

connectivity, and security silicon. Customers are tier-1 auto suppliers and OEMs (~56% of

revenue), industrial/IoT, mobile (secure NFC/eSIM), and comms-infra. The moat is design-in

stickiness: an S32 vehicle-processor or a radar/secure-element win is locked into a platform for

the model's 5–10-year life, with high switching costs and content-per-vehicle that compounds as

cars electrify and go software-defined. This is a cyclical business (auto/industrial

inventory cycles), not a secular hyperscaler-capex play — durability comes from content growth

and long design cycles, not AI buildout.

Fresh thesis — is this a good buy now?

NXP is mid-cyclical recovery off the 2024–25 auto/industrial inventory correction. Q1 2026

(reported Apr, calendar Q1): revenue $3.18B +12% YoY, non-GAAP EPS $3.05 +16%, beating

consensus — and the recovery is broadening, not narrow: Industrial & IoT +24%, Mobile +16%,

Comms-Infra +21%, Auto +6% (the laggard, but guided to accelerate). Q2 guide is the real tell:

$3.45B ±$100M, +18% YoY / +8% sequential, all regions and end-markets up, EPS ~$3.50 mid;

Auto guided to low-double-digit YoY. The fresh thesis is a clean re-acceleration with an edge-AI

optionality kicker (Kinara acquisition, "physical AI," S32 processors for SDVs). At 17.8×

forward earnings that growth is not richly priced — the issue is timing, not quality.

Research dossier: SWKS — Skyworks Solutions

Researched: 2026-06-27 · Verdict: HOLD · Conviction: 0.6 · Opportunity score: 43

Next earnings: 2026-08-04

Business

RF front-end semiconductors — power amplifiers, filters, switches, antenna tuners — that sit in the radio of every cellular device. ~60% of revenue is one customer (Apple); mobile is ~58% of revenue. The rest ("broad markets" — automotive, IoT, infrastructure/edge, data-center connectivity) grows ~11% YoY off a small base. Revenue durably continues because every phone needs RF content and 5G/Wi-Fi 7 lift dollar-content per device — but durability is hostage to (1) Apple's unit cycle and (2) Apple in-sourcing RF/modem. The live, quantified version of that overhang: SWKS is losing ~20–25% of its RF content in the iPhone 17 lineup (late-2025 cycle) — the structural impairment is not hypothetical, it's already in the run-rate. Nascent higher-value adjacency: power electronics for AI data centers / EVs (SelVCD/ProVCD gate drivers, PCIM Europe 2026-06-10) — not yet material.

Fresh thesis — is this a good buy now?

The case is value + a consolidation catalyst, not growth. Forward P/E ~14.0, a 4.2% dividend, TTM revenue just back to positive (+2.3% YoY vs FY25's −2.2%). The structural story is the pending Qorvo acquisition (announced 2025-10-27): SWKS buys QRVO for 0.960 SWKS shares + $32.50 cash per QRVO share, combining the #1 and #2 western RF front-end players into a ~$22B entity with >$500M annual synergies and materially less cyclicality.

So the at-market picture is modestly better than a week ago — ~7% to consensus + 4.2% yield ≈ ~11% total-return path — and the price has come into my prior buy-below zone. But it's still a no-growth, Apple-concentrated, content-losing cyclical whose re-rate rests on a merger that's a year out and FTC-gated. Business

total backlog $8.65B vs $4.88B a year ago (+77%). Commercial revenue +121% YoY; government

+4% (steadier). Revenue durably continues because the naval base is contracted and the

beat-and-raise plus the May 7 $1.4B naval propulsion contract ($1.285B long-lead material,

ESTCHOLDmissing42›

Research dossier: ESTC — Elastic N.V.

Researched: 2026-06-08 · Verdict: HOLD · Conviction: 0.55 · Opportunity score: 42

Business

Elastic is an enterprise search, observability, and security platform company built on the ELK stack (Elasticsearch, Logstash, Kibana). Three pillars: (1) Search — hybrid keyword + vector search, now marketed as "The Search AI Company" powering enterprise RAG applications; (2) Observability — log analytics, APM, infrastructure monitoring; (3) Security — SIEM/XDR. Revenue is >90% subscription, ~80% sales-led. Customers are deeply embedded: the ELK stack is load-bearing infrastructure for search and logging across thousands of enterprises; migration costs are high.

FY2026 full-year revenue: $1.739B (+17% YoY). $100k+ ACV customers: 1,660+. $1M+ ACV customers: >240. Net Expansion Rate: ~112%. AI use cases now penetrate >1/3 of the $100k ACV cohort; AI-using cohorts expand ~5% faster than baseline. AI backlog: >$1B as of Q4 FY26 (May 2026 report).

Why revenue continues: The installed base is structurally sticky (search indices, log pipelines, SIEM rules are not moved lightly). The enterprise-sales motion is converting larger multi-year deals — record $1M+ deal quarter in Q4 FY26, and customers with $5M+ ACV growing ~30% YoY. The platform bet (one vendor for search + observability + security) creates consolidation tailwinds vs. running three point solutions.

Fresh thesis — is this a good buy now?

As of June 8, 2026 — 12 days after Q4 FY26 earnings — the answer is: compelling valuation, awkward timing; wait for a cleaner entry.

The business case is genuine:

  • FY27 revenue guidance: $1.985–$2.0B (~15% YoY). Non-GAAP operating margin guided to ~19% (targeting 25% by FY29). FCF margin ~21.5% in FY27 → ~$430M free cash flow on a $6.35B market cap = ~6.8% FCF yield. For a 15% grower with margin expansion, this is dramatically cheap vs. software peers.
  • EV/forward sales: ~2.7x (peer group: Datadog ~10x, CrowdStrike ~18x, MongoDB ~10x). Even applying a steep discount for Elastic's competitive pressures, 3x the current multiple is defensible.
  • AI search positioning is real. Hybrid search (keyword + vector) is the dominant enterprise pattern for RAG applications — Elastic has been doing hybrid search longer than the pure vector DB startups. The $1B+ AI backlog gives near-term revenue visibility.

The timing tension: the Q4 FY26 beat (revenue $451M vs. ~$440M estimate, profitability turn, FY27 guidance) already landed on May 27 and the stock popped +24% from ~$50 to ~$62. That catalyst has been absorbed. The principles mandate: "demand a real dip OR a fresh catalyst." At $62 post-bounce, neither condition is cleanly met. A macro-induced pullback to $52-58 would restore the asymmetry needed to justify a new buy.

Conclusion: fine business, real value, not the right moment to add at $62. HOLD; watch for the re-entry.

Valuation + position vs. range

Price: ~$62 (June 8, 2026)

52-week range: $42.05 – $96.07

Position: 45% above the 52-week low; 36% below the 52-week high — squarely in the middle of the range, post-bounce.

Market cap: ~$6.35B

Assumed net cash: ~$700M–$1B (strong FCF generation; $340M deployed in buybacks during FY26 while balance sheet remains clean)

Implied EV: ~$5.35–5.65B

My fair-value read: 4.5x forward revenue is defensible for a 15% grower with expanding margins and an AI search optionality layer. That implies: 4.5 × $1.992B = ~$8.96B market cap → ~$86/share. At current price of $62, this is a ~38% discount to fair value.

How much is already priced in: the FY26 beat and FY27 guidance raise are in the price. The AI backlog converting to consumption revenue acceleration — if it materializes in FY27 earnings prints — is not fully in the price yet.

Post-deal / repriced check

ESTC is not a Wait-for-deal thesis name. It's an established public company with no pending hyperscaler partnership the market is waiting on. The relevant repricing event here was the Q4 FY26 earnings catalyst (May 27, 2026): revenue beat, profitability turn, FY27 guidance, AI backlog disclosure. That landed 12 days ago and the +24% jump is in the price.

This is analogous to a post-deal scenario in the sense that the most recent catalyst has been absorbed. The old low-price entry opportunity ($42-50 pre-earnings) is gone. The question from here is whether the post-catalyst multiple (3.65x P/S, ~$62) is worth owning. It is — but only marginally at today's price vs. a better entry 10% lower.

There is no pending deal or partnership announcement that changes this calculus on a binary basis.

Council (one line each, no hedging)

  • Bull: At 2.7x EV/forward revenue with a 6.8% FCF yield and $1B AI backlog, Elastic is priced like a dying business while actually compounding at 15% with expanding margins — the discount is explained by competitive narrative, not business reality.
  • Bear: AWS OpenSearch permanently impaired the open-source moat, Datadog owns observability mindshare, CrowdStrike/Splunk own enterprise SIEM, and 15% growth at decelerating trend is the ceiling, not the floor — re-rating never comes.
  • First-principles: A business generating $430M in annual FCF from $2B in enterprise revenue, growing at 15% with sticky installed base and hybrid-search AI positioning, should trade at more than $6.35B; at the current price you are buying real cash flows cheaply, not speculating on a thesis.

Entry levels the agent derives

Derived from current revenue run-rate and FY27 guidance — not inherited.

  • Buy below: $58 — brings EV/forward sales to ~2.5x; sets up ~50% upside to fair value with comfortable risk/reward in a macro drawdown scenario
  • Fair value: $86 — 4.5x FY27E forward revenue, reflecting 15% growth + margin expansion + AI optionality; ~35% discount to peer median multiples
  • Stretch: $102 — 5x+ forward; requires AI backlog conversion to produce revenue acceleration in FY27 Q1-Q2 prints above the ~15% baseline guidance

Current price at ~$62 is above my buy-below ($58). Not a buy today; re-enter on the dip.

Macro fit

Kill-switches: All three clear (see state/research/macro.md). Hyperscaler capex still accelerating at +67-77% YoY benefits Elastic's observability business (more compute = more logs, metrics, traces) and validates AI search demand. No kill-switch concern.

Rate constraint: With cuts pushed to September/October 2026 at earliest, multiple-expansion is on hold for high-P/S names. Elastic at 3.65x P/S is not in the high-P/S danger zone — the rate constraint matters far less for ESTC than for NBIS/CRWV/PLTR-type names. The FCF yield of 6.8% provides a real rate-of-return anchor.

Regime: Extended market, narrow breadth, CAPE at 42x. A broad market correction of 10-15% (possible if June FOMC or May CPI hawkish) would take ESTC to $52-55 — roughly my buy-below target, incidentally. Not a reason to panic if already held; is a reason to wait before adding at $62.

Opportunity-cost rank vs. field: ESTC is unusual — one of the few names in the software universe trading at a material discount to intrinsic value rather than a premium. Most of the AI-infra watchlist (CRWV, NBIS, IREN, BE) is post-deal and extended. ESTC has different risk/reward character: lower upside (no deal catalyst, no binary event), lower downside (real FCF vs. concept stocks), but mediocre timing. Score: 42/100 — real value, but not the screaming buy that a pullback to $50-55 would be. The better risk/reward today lives in cash or in names with a fresh catalyst, not in a post-bounce software stock.
