GEVHOLDmissing42›

Research dossier: GEV — GE Vernova

Researched: 2026-06-09 · Verdict: HOLD · Conviction: 0.60 · Opportunity score: 42

Business

GE Vernova (spun from GE, April 2024) is the energy-technology company with three segments:

1. Power (~$23B revenue) — gas turbines, aeroderivatives, nuclear steam, gas engines. The sold-out core. Gas turbine slot reservations have grown from 83 → 100 GW through Q1 2026, targeting 110 GW by year-end. New equipment pricing running 10–20% above Q4 2025 levels in a seller's market.

2. Electrification (~$13.9B projected 2026, +44% YoY) — grid transformers, switchgear, HVDC systems, software. Data-center-driven demand is the growth engine: $2.4B in data-center equipment orders in Q1 2026 alone, more than all of 2025 combined. Book-to-bill ~2.5x.

3. Wind (~$7B) — offshore and onshore wind; still margin-challenged but improving. Not the thesis driver; margin drag remains.

Who pays them: utilities, hyperscalers, independent power producers, grid operators globally. Power and Electrification revenue is long-cycle (backlog → execution over 3–5 years). Services contracts on installed base provide a durable recurring stream once gas turbines are shipped.

Why it continues: hyperscaler AI buildout is inelastic to GEV's pricing; the grid-upgrade backlog for HVDC and transformers is multi-year regardless of macro. GEV's only scaled competition in gas turbines is Siemens Energy and Mitsubishi — no new entrant can build that manufacturing capacity in years.

Fresh thesis — is this a good buy now?

The AI energy thesis is as directly applicable here as anywhere in the field. Power IS the binding constraint, and GEV is the picks-and-shovels supplier. Q1 2026 results confirmed the thesis is accelerating, not decelerating:

  • Orders +71% organic to $18.3B — not a one-quarter fluke; driven by both gas turbine reservations and electrification
  • Backlog at record $163B (+$13B sequential), now targeting $200B by 2027 (one year ahead of prior plan)
  • Gas turbine slots filling through 2030: pricing power is locked in for years, not quarters
  • Guidance raised to $44.5–45.5B revenue / 12–14% adj. EBITDA / $6.5–7.5B FCF for 2026

The question isn't whether GEV is a great business — it demonstrably is. The question is whether $939 is a good entry. The stock has already run from ~$458 a year ago to a high of $1,182, then pulled back 21% to $939. In an extended market with 3.8% CPI and no rate cuts until fall 2026 at earliest, the current pullback reflects macro multiple compression rather than fundamental deterioration. That's encouraging for the business but doesn't resolve the valuation question: at ~37x 2026 FCF, you are paying for the continued strong execution of an already-proven story.

Verdict: HOLD. The business is excellent, the pullback from peak is real (21%), but the price is at/near fair value for THIS macro environment. I'm not finding a genuine margin of safety at $939.

Valuation + position vs. range

My own fair-value derivation:

Using FCF multiple for a high-quality industrial with pricing power and backlog visibility:

  • 30x 2026 FCF ($7B) = $210B market cap → $778/share (deep-value entry, demanding a discount)
  • 35x 2026 FCF = $245B → $907/share (fair; captures pricing power + backlog premium)
  • 40x 2026 FCF = $280B → $1,037/share (stretch; requires rate cuts to justify)
  • 45x 2026 FCF = $315B → $1,167/share (analyst consensus range; priced for perfection)

At $939, the stock trades at ~37x 2026 FCF — between my fair (~$907) and stretch (~$1,037) bands. Not cheap; not obviously broken.

Using 2027 FCF (if growth trajectory continues to ~$9–10B), the picture looks better: 25–28x — more defensible for a business of this quality. But that requires continued execution through an uncertain macro.

Post-deal / repriced check

GEV is post-IPO (April 2024) and post-multiple-re-rate. The stock was in the $100s at spin; the AI-energy thesis became widely appreciated through 2025 and the stock ran accordingly. At $939, this is not a "pre-deal" entry. The 21% pullback from the $1,182 peak is meaningful, but the 52-week run of +105% makes clear the entry-edge from the pre-recognition phase has been consumed.

The extended-with-vs-without-catalyst check: the Q1 2026 blowout (April 22) is a FRESH thesis-confirming catalyst — orders +71%, backlog record, guidance raised. The stock's decline from $1,182 to $939 POST that catalyst is instructive: either the market disagrees with the valuation even after the blowout, or the macro environment (3.8% CPI, rate compression) is capping the multiple. Both suggest the repricing is not done. The old entry edge is gone; to enter here requires building a new entry case from the current data, which yields a fair-value range of $907–$1,037 — straddling current price.

Council (one line each, no hedging)

  • Bull: $163B backlog + 100 GW turbine slots locked through 2030 + pricing running 10–20% above prior levels = a multi-year compounding machine the market is temporarily discounting on macro noise.
  • Bear: At 37x 2026 FCF in a rising-rate, no-cut environment, the multiple has nowhere to go but down until the Fed actually moves, and the Wind segment is still a margin anchor.
  • First-principles: A business with locked-in demand, pricing power, and irreplaceable manufacturing capacity trading at 37x FCF is priced for continued excellence — it's not expensive enough to avoid, but not cheap enough to chase in a market with 3.8% CPI and CAPE of 42x.

These are derived from 2026 FCF guide ($7B midpoint) and share count (~270M):

Current price ~$939 is sitting just below the "fair" band entry, slightly above buy-below. The pullback from $1,182 has created a marginally more interesting setup than a month ago, but I don't want to pay "fair" in this macro — I want to pay "buy-below."

Macro fit

Kill-switch status: All three clear. Capex growth far from threshold; GPU pricing/lead-times in generation-transition only; credit spreads historically tight. The secular AI buildout is intact — no structural kill on the demand side for GEV's products.

Regime: Extended. S&P trailing P/E 25.3x, CAPE 42.3x, Buffett Indicator 237%. CPI at 3.8% with no cuts expected until fall 2026 at earliest. This is a multiple-compression environment for premium-multiple industrials, not a multiple-expansion one. GEV at 37x FCF in this regime requires the business to re-rate through earnings growth rather than multiple expansion — that's possible (2027 FCF of $9-10B would cut the multiple to 25-28x at current price), but it takes time.

Opportunity-cost rank: Compared to the field:

  • vs. cash (3.5–3.75% risk-free): GEV's FCF yield is ~2.7% on 2026 guide — actually below cash yield. You're betting on continued FCF growth, not yield.
  • vs. other AI-energy names (BTM gas, nuclear, electrification): GEV is the highest-quality but also most-priced. Smaller/earlier-stage power names may offer better risk/reward on the same thesis.
  • In the opportunity-cost cross-section: GEV is a "hold and wait for $880" rather than "buy now at $939." The May CPI print (June 11, two days away) is a near-term catalyst for the macro; a cool print could re-rate the stock toward $1,000+, a hot print could push it toward $880.

Score: 42/100. Excellent business, but at ~fair value in a compressed-multiple macro. Not the marginal best use of fresh capital today.

Falsification

30-day: May CPI (June 11) — if it reverses toward 3.0–3.2%, rate-cut timeline recovers and GEV re-rates toward $1,050+. A hot print (>3.5%) reinforces the multiple-compression thesis and pushes the entry toward $880.

60-day: Q2 2026 orders/guidance (July earnings) — the $18.3B Q1 order print set a high bar. If Q2 orders slow (say below $12–13B), the backlog narrative cracks. If they hold above $15B, the $200B backlog-by-2027 target looks credible. Watch for Electrification data center order continuation (Q1 was $2.4B vs. all of 2025 — is this a one-time pull-forward or a new run-rate?).

90-day: Wind segment margin trajectory — if Wind gross margins turn positive on a sustained basis (vs. currently sub-zero on some programs), GEV's consolidated margin guide has upside. Flip: if offshore project charges recur, the margin guide comes in at the low end.

Thesis-flip to BUY: Price drops to $850–880 (30–32x 2026 FCF) with fundamentals intact. OR: Q2 2026 orders come in above $16B and guidance is raised again — that would be a fresh catalyst justifying a buy even at $939.

Thesis-flip to AVOID: Multiple hyperscaler capex guidance reductions (kill-switch #1), a sustained pullback in Electrification data-center orders, or Wind charges large enough to impair the FCF guide materially below $6B.

RMBSHOLDfetched42›

Research dossier: RMBS — Rambus Inc.

Researched: 2026-06-27 · Verdict: HOLD · Conviction: 0.50 · Opportunity score: 42

Next earnings: 2026-07-27

Business

Rambus sells memory interface chips — the DDR5 server-DIMM chipset (Registering Clock

Drivers / RCDs, companion data-buffer chips, temperature sensors, power-management ICs) sitting

between the CPU and the DRAM modules — plus silicon IP (security IP, interface IP licensed

into SoCs) and a legacy patent-licensing stream. Customers are DIMM module makers and

server/hyperscaler OEMs; they pay because every DDR5 server module needs an RCD and Rambus holds

~mid-40% RCD share. The durable revenue is per-module silicon content: as the industry steps

DDR5 → MRDIMM (each MRDIMM-12800 needs 1 MRCD + 10 MDB chips) and adds new formats (LPDDR5X

SOCAMM2), silicon dollar content per module rises, expanding TAM even at flat units. The

AI/data-center memory-bandwidth build is the secular tailwind; full-scale MRDIMM adoption is

slated to begin H2 2026.

Fresh thesis — is this a good buy now?

Quality franchise, now at fair value but with a fresh, un-sizable legal overhang — a HOLD,

not yet a BUY. Since my 2026-06-20 HOLD dossier ($141.17), RMBS fell ~19% to ~$114.7, finally

into the ~$118–120 "fair" zone I derived last week. But the drop is **not the clean cohort-wide

de-rate that my falsification clause said would flip this to BUY**. It is bad-news-contaminated:

1. DOJ criminal antitrust subpoena (the new fact). Rambus disclosed in its Q1 (Mar-31-2026)

10-Q that it has been responding since Q1 2026 to a U.S. federal grand-jury subpoena tied

to a criminal investigation by the DOJ Antitrust Division. The market only digested it the

week of June 16–18. This is an unquantifiable left-tail on a company whose leverage is its

IP/licensing position and ~mid-40% RCD share — exactly the kind of dominance that draws

scrutiny. Worst-case 10-Q language includes fines, business-practice changes, even an

injunction; base case is a multi-year, immaterial-but-overhanging process. Either way it

compresses the multiple while unresolved.

2. Insider exodus: 25 insider sales / 0 buys over six months — CEO Seraphin ~$4.4M, COO Fan

~$5.75M, director Higashi ~$2.39M, Stang 5k/~$730k (Jun 2). A cluster this one-sided into a

selloff is a poor tell.

3. Analyst downgrade citing softer memory-module supply/demand and slower-than-expected

growth — a fundamental knock, not just multiple noise.

4. Cohort weight: the whole semi complex is down ~20% over 20d (QCOM −22.5%, ON −27%, SWKS,

AVGO −14.7%), so part of the move is genuinely macro.

Per macro-cohort-confirmation: before buying this dip, confirm it's cohort-wide, not

name-specific bad news. It is both — and the name-specific half (criminal antitrust + insider

exodus + a real downgrade) is the part that should stay my hand. Per

extended-with-vs-without-catalyst, the "what changed in 7–14 days?" test comes back with a

negative catalyst, not a thesis-confirming one. So this is a cheaper quality name with a new,

genuine cloud — wait for the print or DOJ clarity, don't reflexively buy the dip.

Research dossier: S — SentinelOne, Inc.

Researched: 2026-06-27 · Verdict: HOLD · Conviction: 0.45 · Opportunity score: 42

Business

SentinelOne sells the Singularity platform — AI-driven endpoint/XDR security, cloud security,

and SecOps/data analytics — to enterprises, displacing legacy AV and competing head-on with

CrowdStrike and Microsoft Defender. Revenue is sticky subscription ARR (net retention + new

logos); ripping out an endpoint agent is operationally painful, which is the moat. Named a

Leader in the 2026 Gartner MQ for Endpoint Protection Platforms for the 6th straight year and

won "Best Endpoint Security Solution" at the 2026 SC Awards. Current product push is **agentic

security**: Purple AI Agentic Investigation (GA), Singularity Credits (a unified AI-consumption

currency), Amazon Bedrock AgentCore integration, AWS Security Hub Extended availability, and the

Flex licensing model driving multi-product adoption.

Fresh thesis — is this a good buy now?

Same fundamental name as a week ago, but the entry has gotten slightly worse, not better. Two

things changed since the 2026-06-20 read, both marginal:

1. The stock recovered off its post-earnings low. Last week it sat just below both DMAs

(-3.4% / -3.3%), near the 52-wk-low zone — the cleaner part of the dip-buyer setup. Today

~$15.91 (bounced +6.35% on 2026-06-26) it is back to **mid-range and above both DMAs (+0.4%

50DMA, +2.7% 200DMA; 20d -12%, 60d +23%)**. The near-low cheapness that made the buy-below

case sharper a week ago has partly faded — without any new financial information.

2. The "DoD contract" is not a fresh catalyst. It is now surfacing in aggregator summaries

("large U.S. DoD contract, hundreds of thousands of endpoints"), but it traces back to the

Q1 FY27 earnings call (2026-05-28) disclosure, not a new award — and no primary source

gives a contract value, term, or date. So it is real, undimensioned, and **already in the

tape** since late May. I do not treat it as the financial catalyst the thesis is waiting on.

The Q1 FY27 print itself was solid: revenue $277M (+21% YoY), ARR $1.16B (+23%), net-new ARR

+55% YoY, RPO $1.5B (+30%), non-GAAP EPS $0.04 (2× consensus), op margin 4% (+~550bp YoY), $812M

cash, zero debt. The stock de-rated anyway because management **held — didn't raise — the

~20% FY27 guide and announced an 8% workforce cut** (read as profitability-over-growth).

Purple AI GA (2026-06-17) is a genuine, secularly-aligned product event (agentic-ai-compute-mix

/ orchestration-vs-substrate playing out in security) — **but a product/narrative event, not

a financial one.** Whether the agentic layer expands deal sizes won't be visible until Q2 FY27

(~late Aug). So today is still a healthy, debt-free, FCF-positive ~20%-grower at a cheap multiple

with **no fresh financial catalyst forcing the discount to close, and a worse entry price than a

week ago.** Default-to-HOLD holds, and the opportunity score drops a notch.

CIENHOLDfetched40›

Research dossier: CIEN — Ciena Corporation

Researched: 2026-06-30 · Verdict: HOLD · Conviction: 0.65 · Opportunity score: 40

Next earnings: 2026-09-03

Business

Ciena designs and sells optical networking hardware, software, and services. The flagship is

WaveLogic coherent optics (the DSP-driven modems that push data down long-haul and data-center

fiber — 800G shipping, 1.6T qualifying), plus packet/routing platforms and a network-management

software layer. The newest product is RLS Hyper-Rail, an intelligent line system co-created

for AI-training interconnect across hundreds of kilometers.

Who pays them: historically Tier-1 carriers (AT&T, Verizon, BT), but growth has fully rotated to

hyperscalers building data-center interconnect (DCI) — the fiber links between AI data

centers carrying petabits/second, plus the DCOM out-of-band management product scaling at Meta

and a second hyperscaler. Ciena is the incumbent leader with proprietary DSP silicon and 20+

years of deployment density. Revenue durability rests on: (a) 7–10-year optical equipment

lifecycles; (b) the operational catastrophe of switching optical vendors mid-build; (c) a

hyperscaler capex wave (~$725B for 2026, +77% YoY) still mid-cycle. This is a substrate bet —

picks-and-shovels paid every time a hyperscaler lights another DCI link

(orchestration-vs-substrate).

Fresh thesis — is this a good buy now?

No. The AI-DCI thesis is real and executing, but **nothing about the business has changed since

the June 4 Q2 print**, and the price has done the opposite of what would make it buyable: it

rose ~6.7% over the past week (≈$460 → $490.56) on no fresh fact. The only late-June items are

analyst momentum — Needham reiterating Buy with a $600 PT (June 23), a Trefis "AI story is

soaring" piece — not a business change.

Run the extended-with-vs-without-catalyst test: *what fact about the business changed in the

last 7–14 days?* Answer: nothing. The Q2 print, the $7.7B backlog, and the single Hyper-Rail

multi-rail award are all four weeks stale and digested. A continued melt-up on no fresh

thesis-confirming catalyst into a hawkish Fed is the textbook "extended without catalyst" case —

wait, don't chase. The prior dossier (2026-06-23) wanted a disciplined ≤$420 entry or a confirmed

second Hyper-Rail award; neither arrived, and the stock instead ran further from the entry.

The standing yellow flag also persists: Q3 guide $1.625B = only ~+3.5% sequential with gross

margin guided to ~45% — growth compressing from ~40% YoY toward the high-20s/low-30s. That is

exactly the profile a ~10× forward, ~63× forward-P/E multiple punishes hard if the next guide

disappoints.

CRSPHOLDfetched40›

Research dossier: CRSP — CRISPR Therapeutics AG

Researched: 2026-06-19 · Verdict: HOLD · Conviction: 0.45 · Opportunity score: 40

Next earnings: 2026-08-03

Business

A gene-editing therapeutics company — the application layer, not picks-and-shovels.

Its one commercial asset is Casgevy (exa-cel), the first approved CRISPR medicine, for

sickle-cell disease and transfusion-dependent beta-thalassemia, sold via a Vertex (VRTX)

collaboration (CRSP holds ~40% of program economics). Q1 2026 Casgevy revenue was $43M, with

500 patients dosed worldwide and Vertex now filing for a label expansion into ages 5–11. Beyond

Casgevy, the value is a wholly-owned in-vivo and cell-therapy pipeline: CTX310 (ANGPTL3) and

CTX340 (AGT) for cardiovascular disease via LNP delivery, CTX320 (Lp(a)), and zugo-cel (formerly

CTX112), an allogeneic CD19 CAR-T for autoimmune + oncology. Who pays them: today, Vertex

royalties; tomorrow, the bet is that one or more in-vivo cardiovascular edits become durable,

one-shot therapies for large chronic-disease populations.

Per Gene-editing picks-and-shovels, CRSP is exactly the layer that note tells you not to

confuse with the durable bottleneck: the editing platform that consumes the infra (Twist,

Sartorius, Lonza), and that carries binary FDA/clinical risk the tools layer doesn't.

Fresh thesis — is this a good buy now?

The de-risking story is real: a fortress balance sheet ($2.44B cash at 3/31/26, boosted by

$585M of convertible notes in March) funds the whole pipeline through multiple readouts without

dilution pressure, and Casgevy revenue is finally flowing rather than promised. Against an

EV of only ~$2.6–2.8B (market cap ~$5.2B less cash), you are paying very little above net cash

for the in-vivo optionality. That is a more grounded setup than most extended post-deal AI-infra

names. But it is not a clean entry: the value driver is a stack of two-sided binary clinical

catalysts (CTX310/CTX340 cardiovascular updates and zugo-cel autoimmune/onc data, all H2 2026),

the stock sits mid-range (not a real dip), and dip-buying this name historically wins only ~37%

at +63d. The user's method demands a real dip OR a fresh, not-yet-priced catalyst — CRSP today

offers neither. The positive CTX310 Phase 1 update (deep, durable TG/LDL reductions) has already

printed and is partly in the price.

Valuation + position vs. range

  • Price $54.09 (Jun 18), market cap ~$5.2B, ~96.5M shares. Cash $2.44B → EV ~$2.7B.
  • Casgevy is a royalty/collaboration stream, not a revenue-multiple story; CRSP's own P/S is

meaningless here (reported collaboration revenue is a thin line). This is an **option on the

in-vivo pipeline backed by net cash** — value it as cash + risk-adjusted pipeline, not a multiple.

  • Position vs. 52-week range: 31% off the 52w high (~$78), +28% above the 52w low (~$42),

sitting essentially at its 200-DMA (−2.6%) and just above the 50-DMA (+2.5%). ret_20d +11.4%,

ret_60d +16.7%. Mid-range and modestly firm — neither washed-out nor extended.

  • Sell-side consensus clusters $72–$83 (Buy), but the most recent single target (Bernstein,

May 13) was $56 — i.e. the freshest analyst sees it roughly fairly valued here. I weight the

fresh, lower mark over the stale high consensus.

Post-deal / repriced check

Not an AI-infra "wait-for-deal" name, so Wait-for-deal thesis applies only loosely. The

Casgevy approval + Vertex deal — the original re-rating catalyst — is long consumed; that is

priced. The next edge is pipeline data, which is pre-event, not post-deal. So the entry edge

isn't "already eaten" the way IREN's was; it's "not yet created" — you'd be buying ahead of binary

readouts, which is a sizing/odds question, not a chase.

Council (one line each)

  • Bull: ~$2.7B EV for a profitable-collaboration Casgevy annuity plus a free option on

one-shot in-vivo cardiovascular editing in multi-billion-patient markets, fully cash-funded.

  • Bear: A single clinical-stage application name with binary FDA risk, no own-product P&L,

Casgevy ramping slowly, and a value case that lives or dies on H2 data — exactly the customer

layer the picks-and-shovels note says to avoid sizing into.

  • First-principles: At net-cash-plus-a-little you're not overpaying, but the payoff is a

lumpy, two-sided binary — fine as a small, trigger-gated option, not as a conviction core.

Entry levels the agent derives

Derived from this run's tape (range $42–$78, 200-DMA ≈ $55):

  • Buy-below ~$47 — a real dip: back toward the lower third of the range / below the 200-DMA,

where the dip-buyer base rate and the net-cash floor give a margin of safety.

  • Fair ~$54–58 — roughly where it trades and where the freshest analyst (Bernstein $56) sits.
  • Stretch ~$66+ — only with a positive, surprising H2 catalyst (clean CTX310/CTX340 efficacy

or a strong zugo-cel autoimmune signal) that re-rates the in-vivo pipeline.

Catalyst-gated, never at-market — consistent with the weak dip-buy base rate (catalyst-payoff-shape-sizing).

Macro fit

The AI-infra kill-switches (ai-infra-thesis-kill-switches) are N/A — CRSP is biotech, not

levered to hyperscaler capex, GPU pricing, or AI credit. The one macro channel that does bite is

the rate path: the 2026-06-19 macro read has cuts off the table and a hike tail, which pressures

long-duration, cash-burning, no-near-term-earnings names — and clinical biotech is the definition of

long-duration. That's a headwind to the multiple, not the science. On the Opportunity-cost lens

vs. the rest of the field, CRSP is a diversifier with idiosyncratic (non-AI) risk — attractive

as portfolio shape, but it is not below a clear fair value and has no fresh, un-priced catalyst

today, so it doesn't out-rank the field for the marginal dollar right now. Hold-cash regime → wait

for the dip or the data.

Falsification

  • 30d: A clean, deep dip toward ~$47 on cohort-wide (not CRSP-specific bad-news) weakness →

flips toward a small trigger-gated BUY.

  • 60d: H2 in-vivo readouts (CTX310/CTX340) — durable, well-tolerated efficacy that expands the

addressable cardiovascular case would lift conviction and stretch; a safety signal or weak

durability would flip toward AVOID.

  • 90d: Casgevy quarterly trajectory (next print 2026-08-03) — accelerating patient starts

validate the annuity; a stall undercuts the base value and the "free option" framing. Watch also

for any cash burn surprise or fresh dilution beyond the March converts.

Research dossier: CRWV — CoreWeave, Inc.

Researched: 2026-06-26 · Verdict: HOLD · Conviction: 0.6 · Opportunity score: 40

Next earnings: 2026-08-11

Business

Pure-play GPU cloud — a specialized AI landlord. CoreWeave buys NVIDIA GPUs (preferential

allocation is the edge), wraps them in power, cooling, and orchestration tuned for high

utilization, and rents clusters on multi-year take-or-pay contracts to entities that need

compute faster than they can self-build: AI labs (OpenAI ~$22.4B, Anthropic), Meta ($21B),

Jane Street ($6B), and a widening enterprise/financial set. Revenue durably continues because

2026 capacity is contracted and sold out at rising prices, anchored by a $99.4B backlog

(up ~50% QoQ at Q1; >$40B of new commitments signed in the quarter; near-term-weighted —

~36% recognized inside 24 months, ~75% inside four years). The moat is **temporal, not

structural** (Wait-for-deal thesis) — being a credible-now counterparty with power + GPU

allocation in hand. Substrate layer, the deepest multi-step-loop seat in the cohort

(ouroboros-economy-circular-financing): it sells to AI labs whose own revenue still depends

on hyperscaler funding, so any macro AI-spend scare hits CRWV harder than IREN by construction.

A constructive structural shift: non-IG AI-native counterparties are now <30% of backlog as

the book tilts toward investment-grade financials/enterprise — directly attacking the loop risk.

Fresh thesis — is this a good buy now?

Better than a week ago, but still not a BUY at-market. Since the 6/19 dossier (spot ~$118) the

stock has given back to ~$96.5 — exactly the index-add "buy-the-rumor" fade that dossier's

30-day falsification flagged ("fades from $118 toward $95-100 after the 6/22 rebalance"). The

Nasdaq-100 inclusion went effective 6/22; the forced passive buying completed and the technical

bid reversed (−12.4% on the week into 6/25). So the flows catalyst is fully consumed and the

mechanical overbought condition is gone — the stock is back below both DMAs in the dip-buyer

zone. What did NOT change: no fresh thesis-confirming fundamental catalyst landed; the give-back

is the technical event unwinding plus lingering digestion of the early-June Q1 print (wider loss,

soft Q2 revenue guide). Per extended-with-vs-without-catalyst, there's no new fact to chase

here — but there's also no longer a multiple to pay up into. Net: at ~$96.5 you're paying

~fair value for an intact, compounding franchise, on the single most rate-sensitive name in the

book, in a hold-cash regime — a HOLD that is one real washout away from an initiation, not a

buy at-market.

Valuation + position vs. range

  • 52-week range $63.80–$187.00; spot ~$96.5 sits **~48% off the high, ~51% above the

entry-shape has flipped from "extended" (6/19) back to dip-buyer.

TTM revenue $6.23B (+129.9% YoY) → P/S ~8.46× TTM, compressed from ~10.3× at $118.

spot is ~4% below base FV. First-principles ground-up (replacement cost on ~3.5GW contracted

Post-deal / repriced check

Post-deal and repriced — entry edge consumed (Wait-for-deal thesis). CRWV is the

operating incl. the 650 MW Childress campus) plus a multi-GW development pipeline —

interconnection queue positions that take 5–7 years and ERCOT approval to replicate. Who pays

them: today ~90%+ of revenue is still price-taking Bitcoin mining (TTM revenue $757M), but the

thesis-bearing revenue is contracted AI cloud — Microsoft's $9.7B / 5-yr GB300 contract (200 MW

IT load across Horizon 1–4, 20% prepaid) plus an **NVIDIA $3.4B 5-yr cloud contract + $2.1B equity

option struck at $70**. Revenue durably continues if the power scarcity stays scarce

(hyperscalers pay ~$28M/MW for what IREN secures at $5–7M/MW) and if the contracted ARR converts

to recognized revenue — which has not started ($0 Microsoft RPO recognized as of the last print).

Fresh thesis — is this a good buy now?

HOLD, but the most constructive HOLD this name has shown in my coverage. At $47.21 the stock

has fallen ~21% since my 6/19 dossier ($60 → $47.21) and now trades **at/just-below my base fair

value** (P/S 22.2× vs a ~22–24× base) for the first time — versus a ~9–13% premium two weeks ago.

Crucially, the move through the user's $48 add-trigger is now a **broad tech/AI-infra selloff +

BTC washout** (BTC ~$59K, −3.5% on the day; the whole screen cohort is down ~20% over 20 sessions:

FSLR −21%, APLD −21%, QCOM −22%), not the purely idiosyncratic re-rate that defined the 6/24 tape.

That is closer to the clean cohort/BTC wash the framework wants for a dip-buy

(macro-cohort-confirmation). What keeps it HOLD rather than BUY: the **one crux that matters —

Research dossier: ISRG — Intuitive Surgical

Researched: 2026-06-20 · Verdict: HOLD · Conviction: 0.55 · Opportunity score: 40

Next earnings: 2026-07-21

Business

The dominant soft-tissue surgical-robotics franchise. Sells da Vinci systems (installed base

11,395 as of Q1 2026, +12% YoY) and the Ion lung-biopsy platform, then earns the real money on

the razor-and-blade back end: per-procedure instruments/accessories + service. ~85% of revenue

is recurring and procedure-driven; >60% global share in soft-tissue robotics. The moat is the

installed base + surgeon training + 12M+ procedures of clinical data — each generation

(Si→Xi→SP→da Vinci 5) adds capability without forcing surgeons to relearn, so switching cost

compounds. This is the AI-biotech data moats workflow lane, not an AI-infra name — the

infra kill-switches don't bind here; the binding macro is the rate/multiple-compression overlay.

Fresh thesis — is this a good buy now?

The operator is firing and the stock keeps being repriced anyway — that's the whole question.

Q1 2026 (Apr): revenue $2.77B (+23%, beat $2.62B est), non-GAAP EPS $2.50 (+38%, beat $2.11),

da Vinci procedures +16% to 847k, Ion +39%, 431 systems placed (232 of them dV5 vs 147 a year

prior), installed base 11,395 (+12%). Management raised FY2026 da Vinci procedure-growth guide

to 13.5–15.5% and lifted gross-margin guide to 67.5–68.5%. Yet the stock sits at ~$407, down 29%

YTD and ~33% off the ~$600 January peak. So this is a multiple de-rate on a healthy operator —

the setup that can be an entry. It stays HOLD, not BUY, because nothing has improved versus

last week's read and one thing got worse: **the SureForm issue has been formally classified an

FDA Class I recall (the most serious class), tied to a patient death** — that drove the June 17

-3.6% session, and it is a still-developing safety-confidence overhang, not a closed chapter. Per

extended-with-vs-without-catalyst, a dip with no fresh thesis-confirming catalyst is not a

chase; the only "catalyst" on the tape (the Citi Buy upgrade) is from March 11 at $493 — three

months and ~18% stale, not new information.

Valuation + position vs. range

  • Price $406.78 (Jun 20). Off 52-wk high -32.7%, only +2.5% off the 52-wk low; -7.5% vs 50DMA,

-17.1% vs 200DMA, -13.8% over 60d. Firmly dip-buyer zone, trend broken.

  • Trailing P/E 49.4; forward P/E 38.2; market cap $144.1B; 354.2M shares; TTM revenue $10.58B

(+21.4%) → ~13.6× sales. Consensus PT $565 (registry); a reset-aware sell-side PT cluster has

drifted toward $430–$590.

  • Fair-value read: for a ~14–20% grower with 85% recurring revenue, 67–68% gross margins and a

60%+-share moat, ~38× forward is defensible but not cheap. The froth (~55×+ forward at the

peak) is gone; 38× is a normalization, not a gift. A real margin of safety wants low-/mid-30s

forward — i.e. a further leg down. Most of the easy multiple is already out.

Post-deal / repriced check

No deal-driven entry edge (Wait-for-deal thesis is the AI-infra frame). The relevant analog:

the froth multiple has been consumed (down ~33% from peak), but the cheap multiple hasn't

arrived. The repricing is partway, not finished — which is exactly why it's HOLD, not BUY: the

edge isn't clearly there yet, and the FDA Class I recall is a fresh reason the de-rate could have

further to run before it overshoots into buyable territory.

MUHOLDfetched40›

Research dossier: MU — Micron Technology

Researched: 2026-06-23 · Verdict: HOLD · Conviction: 0.62 · Opportunity score: 40

Next earnings: 2026-06-24

Business

Micron is one of three global DRAM/NAND makers (with SK Hynix and Samsung). DRAM is ~75% of

revenue — server/data-center DRAM and, increasingly, High Bandwidth Memory (HBM); NAND ~20%.

Capital-intensive and historically the most cyclical semiconductor vertical: oligopoly pricing

swings the P&L violently. The structural change of 2025–26 is HBM as a discrete, high-ASP

sub-market driven by GPU training/inference — HBM3E (H200) and HBM4 (Blackwell/Vera Rubin) carry

3–5× standard-DRAM ASPs. Micron is #3 in HBM (~21% share vs SK Hynix ~62%, Samsung ~17%) but is

the swing supplier NVIDIA wants for HBM4 dual-sourcing. Micron is now in **volume shipment of

HBM4 36GB 12H for NVIDIA's Vera Rubin** (>2.8 TB/s, >20% better power efficiency than HBM3E),

and management says HBM4 is ramping ~2× faster than HBM3E did.

Fresh thesis — is this a good buy now?

The supercycle is intact and arguably running hotter than my last read (2026-06-13): DRAM ASPs

mid-60s% sequential, NAND high-70s%, gross margin guided toward ~68%, all of 2026 HBM sold out

under 3–5 year contracts. The business case is stronger than two weeks ago. But the entry

question is the one the framework forces, and it has gotten worse, not better:

1. The stock moved up into fair value, not down to the buy-below. On 06-13 it was $982,

below my $1,045–1,140 fair band — a "warm hold." It's now **$1,051, squarely inside that

band** and ~+40% over 20 sessions. The forward-earnings margin of safety that made it

constructive has been consumed by price.

2. **The two binaries the 06-13 dossier was explicitly waiting on have both turned against the

"buy" path.** The 06-17 FOMC dot plot flipped hawkish (hike bias, no 2026 cuts) — the

opposite of the dip-catalyst that would have flipped this to BUY. And the **Q3 print is now

T-1 (June 24, after close), with options pricing a ~17% implied move**.

3. **Buying at-market, near ATH, the night before a 17%-implied binary, in a hold-cash macro is

the textbook mistake** these frameworks forbid. A beat is largely priced (consensus already at

~$35B rev / ~$20.57 EPS, GM ~68%); the asymmetry into the print is poor.

This is extended WITH catalyst per extended-with-vs-without-catalyst — the PT-doubling /

pricing-surge repricing was real, so this is not simple FOMO. But "the new thesis is worth

owning at the new price" and "buy it at-market the night before the print" are different

claims. The first is plausible; the second is not. **Verdict: HOLD — wait for the print to

clear.** Conviction in the business is high; conviction that today is a poor spot to deploy the

marginal $100 is what the 0.62 reflects.

Valuation + position vs. range

Fair-value derivation (re-derived this run): central FY2027 EPS ~$90–100, bull ~$115–120,

bear (cycle turns) ~$50. At 11–12× central ~$95 → $1,045–1,140 fair. At $1,051 the stock sits

at the bottom of fair — no longer below it. The cheapness on forward earnings is real, but it's

**~11.6× peak-cycle earnings**, which is exactly what memory looks like just before a turn. The

value-trap risk is the permanent counterweight to the low multiple.

Post-deal / repriced check

MU is not a Wait-for-deal thesis name — no single deal to consume the edge; it's a cyclical

riding an earnings supercycle, and the earnings are still accelerating sequentially, which is why

the forward multiple stays ~11–12× even as price made new highs. The 1,000%+ run has happened.

Unlike the IREN-at-$61 mistake (paying up for a post-deal name ~30% above entry with no fresh

catalyst), here a fresh catalyst did land and the multiple is contracting, not expanding. The

discipline that still bites: the move has now carried the stock to fair value, and the next

catalyst (the print) is a coin-flip-sized binary one day out — so the residual edge is "wait,"

not "add."

Council (one line each, no hedging)

  • Bull: ~11.6× forward earnings with HBM sold out through 2026 on 3–5yr contracts, HBM4

17%-implied print, where the +63d distribution is dominated by tomorrow's gap. The dip_buyer

preset is weak here (36–44% win) — this name pays to ride confirmed momentum, not to catch falling

knives, so the buy-below trigger must be a real cohort dip, not a name-specific post-print crack.

Opportunity-cost rank: structurally MU is one of the better forward risk/rewards in the field

(~11.6× forward earnings with accelerating prints, vs. a field of names at 30–100× revenue). But

the cross-sectional question is the marginal $100 right now, and right now it's: at fair value,

at ATH, the night before a 17%-implied binary, in a hold-cash macro. That's a poor use of the

marginal dollar today even for a high-quality name — hence the score drop to 40 from the

06-13's 50. The edge is one day of patience away, not gone.

Falsification

  • June 24 Q3 print + Q4 guide is the swing factor: Q4 rev > ~$35–37B with **GM expanding past

~70%** = cycle not peaking → on a post-print pullback or orderly digestion this flips to BUY

"priced for perfection," raises odds of revisiting the ~$850 buy-below.

  • Samsung HBM4 qualification at NVIDIA + the +50% capacity ramp; UBS's ~70%-SK-Hynix-Rubin-share

call — either gaining traction threatens Micron's ~21% share and the HBM mix story.

  • Micron Q4 FY26 report — gross-margin guidance rollover is the leading cycle-turn signal,

ahead of revenue.

Flips to BUY: dip to ~$850 (real, cohort-wide) OR a Q3 print with Q4 guide $35B+ and GM

NETHOLDfetched40›

Research dossier: NET — Cloudflare, Inc.

Researched: 2026-06-20 · Verdict: HOLD · Conviction: 0.62 · Opportunity score: 40

Next earnings: 2026-07-30

Business

Cloudflare runs a global connectivity cloud — 320+ points of presence carrying CDN, DDoS

mitigation, zero-trust/SASE access, and edge developer compute (Workers). Revenue is

subscription-led with heavy usage-based upsell: ~78% gross margins, ~120% net dollar retention,

and an enterprise cohort that compounds (deals >$1M up 73% YoY in Q1, record adds at the >$5M

tier). The network is a utility for a meaningful slice of internet traffic and the zero-trust

stack creates sticky multi-year enterprise contracts — that's the durable base.

The growth bet layered on top is repositioning the network as AI-agent infrastructure:

Workers AI (edge GPU inference), AI Gateway (LLM traffic routing/caching), and the May 19 2026

Cloudflare Environments for Claude Managed Agents partnership with Anthropic (run enterprise

Claude agents on Cloudflare's network with zero-trust + global latency). Agents Week (June 2026)

ran 241B tokens through Workers AI; the June 4 VoidZero acquisition folds Vite/Vitest/Rolldown

dev tooling into one-click edge deploy. Real products with real usage — but not yet a visible

revenue line.

Fresh thesis — is this a good buy now?

The setup has improved since the 2026-06-09 dossier (HOLD at ~$248) — but not enough to flip.

The stock crashed 24% to ~$194 on the May 7 Q1 print (a clean beat — rev $639.8M +34%,

non-GAAP EPS $0.25 vs $0.23 — overshadowed by the first mass layoff in company history, 1,100

roles / 20% of staff, framed by Prince as "AI made these jobs obsolete"). It then rebounded

(+11% June 1, +3.6% June 11 to $227) and has since drifted back to ~$224. So today's price is a

~10% pullback off the early-June recovery high, sitting in my fair-value zone.

What's genuinely good here: the AI-agent positioning is product, not slideware (Anthropic

runtime + 241B tokens of real traffic), the enterprise expansion engine is intact (120% NRR,

$1M deals +73%), and the layoff — read past the alarming framing — is a margin lever: $140-150M

of one-time charges that should step run-rate FCF up from ~13%. NET's own dip-buyer base rate is

favorable (44 prior instances: 55%/59%/68% win at +21/+42/+63d, avg +16% at +63d), which says

buying this name in this kind of pullback has historically paid.

What stops me from buying at $224: (1) it's ~28× forward P/S — a high-duration SaaS multiple in

a regime explicitly hostile to duration (no 2026 cuts, hike tail); (2) there is **no fresh

catalyst** since June 9 — this is a quiet drift lower, partly cohort de-rating, not a

thesis-confirming-catalyst dip the macro stance demands; (3) the load-bearing question —

does the AI-agent platform show up in the top line? — isn't answerable until July 30; (4) the

layoff carries a real bear read (is Cloudflare hedging against AI compressing its own demand?).

The dip-buyer edge is real but doesn't override paying above fair value with the proof point six

weeks out.

Bottom line: Excellent business, improving entry, favorable base rate — but priced just above

my fair value, no new catalyst, and the monetization tell lands July 30. Hold; let a deeper dip

or the print be the trigger.

Valuation + position vs. range

  • Price: ~$224 (June 18 close $224.06; screen close $223.95 on 06-20)
  • 52-week range: $158.83 – $276.82 — ~19% off high, ~41% above low (mid-upper range)
  • Market cap: ~$79.2B · diluted shares: ~354M
  • Revenue: TTM $2.33B (+31.6%); FY2026E guide $2,805–2,813M (29–30%); Q2 guide $664–665M (30%)
  • Forward P/S (FY2026E): ~28× · TTM P/S: ~34×
  • Profitability: GAAP unprofitable (TTM net income −$86.7M; forward P/E ~173×); non-GAAP FY

EPS $1.19–1.20, FCF 13% of rev ($84.1M in Q1); cash $4.164B — balance sheet is not a risk

  • Analyst PT: consensus ~$243 (Buy, 34 analysts); Morgan Stanley high at $305 (Overweight)

At ~28× forward sales the market is paying the quality-and-NDR premium plus a down-payment on

AI-agent monetization that isn't in the numbers yet. That's defensible at fair value; it's not a

margin-of-safety entry.

Post-deal / repriced check

Not a Wait-for-deal thesis name in the GPU/energy sense — there's no single lab-partnership

that re-rates it. But the analogous edge has been partly consumed: the Anthropic deal (May 19),

VoidZero (June 4) and Agents Week are all 2–6 weeks old and already in the tape (the stock made

its run, crashed on earnings, recovered). Buying $224 today is not buying ahead of those

catalysts — it's buying after them, on a drift, before the revenue proof. Per the framework, an

old (even if good) catalyst is not an entry case.

Council (one line each, no hedging)

  • Bull: The only network with 320+ PoPs + native zero-trust + the official Claude managed

runtime — if agent traffic becomes the next usage layer, NET monetizes it from a sticky 120%-NRR

base, and the layoff makes the model lean into that ramp.

  • Bear: The CEO is telling you AI obsoletes labor — including, plausibly, demand for parts of

Cloudflare's own stack — and you're paying 28× sales for an unproven monetization story into a

no-cut, hike-tail regime that punishes exactly this duration.

  • First-principles: $79B for $2.81B of 2026 revenue needs 25%+ CAGR to 2030 and FCF margins

to 25%+ and the agent layer to become real revenue; at ~$185–205 you buy the first two with

free optionality on the third — at $224 you're pre-paying the third.

Entry levels the agent derives

FY2026E revenue $2.81B, ~354M diluted shares; macro discounts the terminal multiple, so I anchor

modestly below the prior run:

  • Buy-below: ~$178 — ~22× forward P/S; strips most of the unproven AI-platform premium and

prices growth deceleration; initiate with conviction here

  • Fair value: ~$205 — ~26× forward P/S; quality/NDR/optionality premium, defensible but not a

bargain (current $224 sits ~9% above this)

  • Stretch / catalyst-confirmed: ~$238 — ~30× forward P/S; only rational if July 30 shows AI

Gateway/Managed-Agents revenue appearing or FCF margin stepping to 18%+

  • Current ~$224 ≈ 28× — above fair, below stretch; no compelling new-money entry

Macro fit

Kill-switches all clear (capex +~77% YoY, GPU pricing firm/short, HY OAS ~285bp) — secular AI

thesis intact, and NET rides it indirectly (more AI traffic → more CDN/security/Workers usage).

But the binding constraint is the discount-rate path: no 2026 cuts, dots leaning HIKE — a

direct multiple-compression headwind on a 28× P/S, high-duration name. The macro stance is hold

cash, demand a real dip OR fresh catalyst, prefer names below fair value. NET offers a modest

dip but no fresh catalyst and trades above fair, so on the Opportunity-cost lens it's a

middle-of-field hold — better positioned than a no-catalyst decelerator (it has a real platform

story and a favorable dip base rate), but a worse use of the marginal dollar than a name genuinely

below fair value with a setup. Score 40.

Falsification

30 days — tape + cohort: A cohort-wide high-P/S de-rate (macro-confirmed, not name-specific)

that carries NET toward ~$195–205 would put it at/near fair value with the dip-buyer base rate

working — a trigger-gated BUY, not at-market. A further drift on no news without reaching fair

value stays HOLD.

60 days — Q2 print (2026-07-30): The decisive test. Watch (a) any AI Gateway / Workers AI /

Managed-Agents revenue or ARR disclosure, (b) FCF margin stepping up post-restructuring (toward

18–20% from ~13%), (c) whether FY 29–30% growth holds/accelerates, (d) large-customer NRR holding

~120%. A clean step-up on (b)+(d) with early (a) flips to BUY even near $224.

90 days — monetization attribution: If by Q3 Cloudflare names specific Managed-Agent enterprise

deals with dollar figures, the agent layer becomes fundable and the multiple is earned. Absent

that, Anthropic is a marketing win and 28× P/S gets harder to defend.

Flip to BUY: price ≤ ~$200 (≤~25× fwd) with FY guide ≥28% and any sign of AI-platform revenue;

OR July 30 FCF margin ≥18% with NRR stable.

Flip to AVOID: two quarters of NRR sliding from 120%, OR FY guide cut below 25%, OR AI Gateway

usage stalling — any one signals the agent positioning is marketing not architecture, and 28× P/S

compresses toward 15–18×.

NRGHOLDfetched40›

Research dossier: NRG — NRG Energy, Inc.

Researched: 2026-06-27 · Verdict: HOLD · Conviction: 0.50 · Opportunity score: 40

Next earnings: 2026-08-05

Business

NRG is an integrated independent power producer (IPP) + the largest competitive retail

electricity supplier in the US, weighted to Texas/ERCOT and PJM, with a generation fleet that

doubled to ~25.8 GW after closing the ~$12B LS Power acquisition (Jan 30, 2026) (+13 GW

gas/dual-fuel + the CPower virtual-power-plant / demand-response platform). Revenue comes from

(1) selling power and retail plans to ~8M+ homes/businesses (the sticky, higher-quality book)

and (2) merchant wholesale generation (a price-taker spread on ERCOT/PJM clearing prices). The

growth leg is selling firm power to AI data centers: a development agreement with **GE Vernova

+ Kiewit/TIC to build up to ~5.4 GW of new gas generation** (1.2 GW by 2029, 1.2 GW by 2030,

~3 GW 2030–32), with ~445 MW total contracted capacity (incl. a 295 MW Texas deal) and >6 GW of

additional build potential. This is the AI Energy Thesis expressed through a large,

cash-generative incumbent — but note it is the grid-connected merchant side of that thesis,

the side behind-the-meter gas (Williams–Meta, VoltaGrid, BE) is routing around. NRG captures AI

load second-hand, via tighter clearing prices — not via a direct contracted on-site moat.

Fresh thesis — is this a good buy now?

No — not at $149. The prior agent dossier (2026-06-20) rated this BUY at $135, ~11% below

its own ~$150 fair value, on the dip base rate. That entry edge has now been consumed: NRG has

rallied ~+10% in a week to $149.36 (Jun 26) and is sitting at my fair value with the margin

of safety gone. Crucially, the rally is **analyst-coverage flow, not a thesis-confirming

catalyst** — Goldman initiated Buy (Jun 18), Wells Fargo Buy (Jun 22), Wolfe upgraded to

Outperform ($190 PT, "additionality play"), and the CEO is talking up a "demand supercycle." None

of that changes the one variable the merchant thesis turns on — ERCOT/PJM forward power prices —

which softened in Q1 (adj EPS $1.49 missed $1.73 by 14%; Jefferies cut its target citing lower

Texas power prices / softer PJM capacity). Per extended-with-vs-without-catalyst: chasing an

extended name is justified only when a fresh thesis-confirming catalyst just landed. A wave of

sell-side upgrades is sentiment, not the signed large-load PPA or the power-price re-tightening

this name needs. The dip got bought before the thesis got confirmed. **Wait for the Aug 5 print

(power-price re-tightening = the real confirm) or a pullback that restores margin of safety.**

Valuation + position vs. range

  • Price ~$149.36 (Jun 26 close; screen feed $149.25). Market cap $31.5B, ~211M shares.

52w range $120.11–$189.96 — now 21.2% off the high, +24.2% off the $120.11 low.

ret_20d +8.6%, +6.1% vs 50DMA, −4.6% vs 200DMA — the broken trend has healed back

above the 50DMA; the falling-knife phase is fully over and the bounce is mature.

  • Forward P/E ~14.3× (stockanalysis.com, NTM) — up from ~12.7× when the stock was $135; the

re-rate happened on the way up. Trailing P/E (~172×) is meaningless (GAAP depressed by LS Power

purchase accounting + hedge MTM noise); use the adjusted/forward lens.

  • FY26 guide (reaffirmed): Adj EPS $7.90–9.90 (mid ~$8.90). At $149 that is **~16.8× the

2026 guide midpoint — roughly fair-to-slightly-rich for a levered IPP, with little downside

margin of safety (low-end $7.90 × ~16× ≈ $126). The "cheap" 14× only holds on the forward**

~$10.5 EPS, i.e. the 2027–28 number that already assumes the guided ~14% CAGR — the exact ramp

Q1's soft power prices put in doubt.

  • Balance sheet: LS Power was debt-financed; D/E ~479%, net-debt/EBITDA target <3.0×, ~$1B

paydown planned in 2026. Elevated, rate-sensitive leverage into a higher-for-longer tape.

C13 - Asset Growth and Capex Caution flag: a debt-funded doubling of the asset base against a

data-center pipeline that is pipeline, not signed PPAs — C13's contracted-demand exception is

not yet met.

  • My fair value: ~$150 (≈17× the ~$8.90 mid guide, haircut for leverage/rate-sensitivity and

the long-dated 2029+ growth leg). The stock is now AT fair value. Street is far more bullish

(avg PT ~$199), pricing the >6 GW pipeline largely converting; I haircut that for execution +

rates + the fact that the merchant spread is a price-taker's margin.

Post-deal / repriced check

This is NOT a post-deal consumed name in the IREN/NBIS sense — the big data-center agreement the

whole bull case hinges on still has not been signed (CEO: "expects to announce in 2026"; the

Utility Dive coverage confirming this is Nov-2025 and nothing has landed since). What has

happened is a sentiment re-rate: the ~29% drawdown bottomed at $120 on June 10 and the dip has

been bought back to fair value on sell-side upgrades. So the discipline flag here is the reverse

one from the watchlist — not "post-deal, edge gone," but **"the entry edge was in the dip, and the

dip is gone; don't chase fair value up to the analyst targets before the fundamental confirm

(signed PPA or power-price tightening) actually lands."** Buying $149 here repeats the IREN-at-$61

pattern the principles forbid: paying above the entry level on an already-priced (analyst-flow)

catalyst, not a fresh one.

ONTOHOLDfetched40›

Research dossier: ONTO — Onto Innovation Inc.

Researched: 2026-06-27 · Verdict: HOLD · Conviction: 0.60 · Opportunity score: 40

Next earnings: 2026-08-06

Business

Onto Innovation sells process-control tools to chipmakers — optical metrology, macro/inspection

(the Dragonfly line), lithography systems, and software — used to measure features and find defects

across wafer fab and, increasingly, advanced packaging. Customers are the logic foundries (TSMC),

the HBM memory makers (SK Hynix, Samsung, Micron), and OSATs. The durable revenue driver is

structural, not a one-off product cycle: AI accelerators are bottlenecked on **advanced packaging

(CoWoS / 2.5D logic) and HBM stacking**, and every additional stacked-die / hybrid-bond step adds

inspection and metrology insertions. ONTO is a picks-and-shovels play on the physical constraint of

the AI buildout, and it sells to all the memory/foundry competitors rather than betting on one. The

2026 Dragonfly G5 ramp + TSMC qualification made it a default tool supplier on the most advanced 2.5D

logic flows.

Fresh thesis — is this a good buy now?

The business thesis is strong; the entry thesis is still weak, and there's a **new wrinkle since the

2026-06-12 dive: the Rigaku stake. In mid/late June ONTO agreed to buy a 27% minority stake in

Rigaku Holdings (~$710M, 61.1M shares from a Carlyle affiliate), financed by a $500M Goldman

364-day bridge loan plus new convertibles**, closing 2H26 (HSR-gated). Strategically it's coherent —

it pairs ONTO's Ai Diffract software with Rigaku's CD-SAXS X-ray metrology for a process-control

adjacency analysts size at >$1B in 5 years, plus one board seat and dividend income. But it is a

minority investment, not an operating acquisition, and it brings **leverage (bridge loan) and

dilution (convertibles)** — exactly the asset-growth / share-issuance caution flags

(C13 - Asset Growth and Capex Caution · C14 - Share Issuance and Dilution Drag). The tell is

in the tape: on the deal + Oppenheimer's $450 PT (June 22, from $370), the stock pushed a fresh

all-time high of $353 and then round-tripped back to $324 (a -5.9% day June 26 on a chip

selloff). Per Wait-for-deal thesis's post-deal patterns, that's **immediate-compression / partial

sell-the-news**, not sustained repricing — so paying up at-market on this catalyst is not justified

(extended-with-vs-without-catalyst: a fresh fact exists, but the market already declined to

sustain-reprice on it).

Valuation + position vs. range

Spot $323.92; market cap ~$16.11B; shares 49.74M; 52-week range $89.40 – $353.18 — it

trades ~8% off the new high and +262% off the low. TTM revenue $1.03B (+0.5%) with TTM net

income $106.4M (-51.4%), so trailing P/E ~150× is a trough-earnings artifact, not the relevant

number. Forward picture: forward P/E ~40× (~36–37× on some sell-side non-GAAP estimates), ~12×

forward P/S on the reaffirmed >$1.3B FY26 (>30% growth, advanced packaging >50%) guide. That is a

premium to the semicap group (KLAC ~30× fwd, LRCX ~25×, AMAT ~22×) and roughly *in line with the

semiconductor-industry forward median (~37×)*. The premium is defensible — highest-growth, purest

advanced-packaging/HBM-levered name — but 40× forward fully prices flawless execution, and the

trough-to-recovery operating leverage cuts both ways: one guide-down compresses multiple and EPS

together. The Rigaku-funding debt/converts now also sit between revenue and per-share value. Much of

the 2026 recovery is already in the tape.

Post-deal / repriced check

Per Wait-for-deal thesis: the original entry edge (TSMC G5 qual, June shipments, bullish

initiations) was consumed at the 2026-06-12 high. The Rigaku stake is a second catalyst layered

on top — and it has also already been partially digested: the stock made its high on it and gave it

back. Classify it immediate-compression / sell-the-news, not sustained repricing. The old bull

case ("ONTO is the advanced-packaging bottleneck play, now extending into X-ray metrology") is **true

but is not an entry case at $324**. The marginal-dollar entry, if any, comes from a normal pullback to

the 50DMA zone — not from paying up post-news.

Council (one line each, no hedging)

  • Bull: The single purest listed bet on the AI buildout's actual bottleneck (HBM/2.5D packaging),

  • First-principles: A great franchise at a full price, with a sound-but-dilutive deal, bought after

Research dossier: ORCL — Oracle Corporation

Researched: 2026-06-11 · Verdict: HOLD · Conviction: 0.45 · Opportunity score: 40

Business

Oracle sells (1) the enterprise database + Fusion/NetSuite SaaS apps that run the back office of

much of the Fortune 500 — sticky, high-margin, durable — and (2) increasingly, **OCI (Oracle

Cloud Infrastructure)**: raw AI compute capacity sold to hyperscalers and AI labs. The second leg

is now the story. OCI revenue hit $5.8B in Q4 FY26, +93% YoY, anchored by a **$300B,

five-year Stargate/OpenAI** compute commitment. Oracle has flipped from a mature software cash cow

into a capital-intensive AI-infra builder competing with AWS/Azure/GCP. The legacy database

business is the cushion; OCI is the growth and the risk.

Fresh thesis — is this a good buy now?

Mixed, and the answer is "not yet." The franchise is real and the stock is down **47% off its

$345.72 high** ($184 today) — a genuine drawdown, not an extended chase. Forward P/E ~22.9x on

FY27's $8.05 guide (18% EPS growth, 47% cloud growth) is not egregious for the growth rate, and

the $638B RPO backlog (+363% YoY) is a staggering demand signal. That's the bull pull.

But the drawdown is fundamentally driven, not an overdone macro selloff: the June 10 print

paired record revenue with a guidance shock — FY26 free cash flow swung to -$23.7B (from

-$394M), net capex ~$70B for FY27 (analysts model up to $100B / ~$275B cumulative FY26-28), and

$40B of fresh financing including a $20B at-the-market equity offering. Per

macro-cohort-confirmation, this is a name-specific re-rating (Oracle's balance sheet), not a

cohort-wide dip — which argues against reflexively buying it.

Valuation + position vs. range

  • Price $184.10 · mkt cap $529B · shares 2.88B · 52w range $134.57–$345.72 (down 47% off high,

Research dossier: RKLB — Rocket Lab Corporation

Researched: 2026-06-29 · Verdict: HOLD · Conviction: 0.60 · Opportunity score: 40

Next earnings: 2026-08-06

Business

Vertically-integrated space prime — the only credible Western reusable-launch challenger to

SpaceX. Two reported segments, one spine: Launch (Electron, the 50+-flight small-lift

workhorse — 91 launches all-time, 12 in 2026; HASTE, its suborbital hypersonic-test variant; and

Neutron, the medium-lift reusable rocket targeting a Q4 2026 first flight, five commercial

contracts already signed) and Space Systems (satellites, buses, components, solar, separation

systems, flight software — the majority of revenue). Customers pay for rides to orbit (commercial,

civil, and increasingly defense — Space Force, SDA, NRO, NASA) and for the satellite hardware that

flies on them. Durable-revenue case: cheap, reliable, sovereign-capable launch + the bus under the

payload is the binding constraint in the space economy, and RKLB sells both. **$2.2B backlog

(+108% YoY)** is the forward indicator; the margin-quality leg consensus underweights is defense —

the $816M SDA Tracking Layer Tranche 3 prime award (Dec 2025) and the **$190M DoW HASTE block

order** for 20 hypersonic flights.

Fresh thesis — is this a good buy now?

**Thesis intact; $98 is the wrong price — the dip that made it a buy already round-tripped on a

sell-side upgrade, not a fundamental catalyst.** This is the decisive fact of this run. The

sequence: RKLB crashed ~−44% from $151 into ~$84 by June 26 on a textbook non-execution wash

(SpaceX's $2T IPO sucking capital out of the whole space sector + the $3B ATM dilution overhang +

Nasdaq-100 sell-the-news). That dip handed a genuine entry in the high-$80s — which the user's

pre-registered $80–95 zone). **Then today (June 29) the name ripped +15.9% to $98.01 on a KeyBanc

stacked on operational cadence wins (10th consecutive Synspective launch + 17 more booked, three

Per extended-with-vs-without-catalyst, the question is whether fresh, thesis-confirming

TSMHOLDfetched40›

Research dossier: TSM — Taiwan Semiconductor Manufacturing Co.

Researched: 2026-06-19 · Verdict: HOLD · Conviction: 0.6 · Opportunity score: 40

Next earnings: 2026-07-16

Business

The world's dominant pure-play foundry — it manufactures the leading-edge logic (and increasingly the advanced packaging) that everyone else designs. Customers are the fabless ecosystem: NVIDIA, Apple, AMD, Broadcom, Qualcomm. Revenue durably continues because TSM is a near-monopoly at the leading edge (3nm/2nm) and the binding bottleneck of the AI buildout. This is the "wafers" half of Gavin Baker's "watts and wafers" — and per Nvidia - AI Cycle Durability and GPU Economics, TSM's restraint (disciplined capacity adds run by "flinty old-school veterans") is what smooths and elongates the cycle by preventing an overbuild. Wafer intensity may be AI's savior; TSM is the toll booth.

Fresh thesis — is this a good buy now?

The business is firing on all cylinders, but the entry is the question, and at ~$462 (0.6% off the 52-week high, +34.6% over 60d, +37.9% above the 200DMA) this is an extended chart. Q1 2026 (reported Apr 16): revenue $35.9B (+6.4% QoQ USD), gross margin 66.2% (record, beat the high guide by 120bp), operating margin 58.1%, HPC 61% of revenue (+20% QoQ). They raised full-year 2026 revenue-growth guidance to above 30% in USD and guided Q2 to $39.0–40.2B (+10% QoQ) at 65.5–67.5% GM. June 4 annual meeting: CoWoS advanced-packaging is sold out through 2026, lead times to ~104 weeks, capacity meeting only ~80% of demand (NVDA ~60% of it). All genuinely bullish — but all of it is already in the price: the print is two months old and the CoWoS commentary is incremental, not a step-change. Per extended-with-vs-without-catalyst, the operative test is "what business fact changed in the last 7–14 days?" — nothing material did. So the run is continuation/momentum, not fresh repricing. Extended without a fresh catalyst → don't chase here.

Valuation + position vs. range

  • Price $462.12 (Jun 18), market cap $1.98T, TTM revenue $128.3B, net margin 46.5%.
  • Trailing P/E ~33, forward P/E ~23, P/S 15.4 (stockanalysis.com, fetched). Up +116% over the trailing 52 weeks.
  • The forward P/E of ~23 against >30% revenue growth, 66% gross margin and 46% net margin is not egregious on quality — PEG well under 1 and roughly in line with the broad market's ~22.5× forward P/E despite far better economics. The stretch is positional, not multiple-based: it sits at the very top of its range after a vertical run. P/S 15.4 is the number that bites if the market de-rates.
  • Fair-value read: I'd anchor "fair" near $430–445 (forward earnings supportive but no margin for the positioning) and treat anything above ~$470 as requiring a new catalyst, not just continued sold-out commentary.

Post-deal / repriced check

Wait-for-deal thesis doesn't apply cleanly — TSM isn't a small/mid pre-deal infra name awaiting a lab partnership; it's the structural substrate, a durable compounder, not a windowed supply/demand trade. But the spirit of the check does: the bull facts (record margins, raised guide, CoWoS sold out) are consumed by the +116% repricing. "It was a great business at $300" is true and irrelevant to whether $462 at the 52-week high is an entry. The old bull case is not an entry case here.

Council (one line each, no hedging)

  • Bull: The single most defensible toll booth in AI — wafers and CoWoS are the bottleneck, sold out through 2026 at 104-week lead times, and a forward P/E of 23 underprices >30% growth at 66% gross margin.
  • Bear: Taiwan single-point-of-failure (geopolitical tail, energy/Strait-of-Hormuz exposure) on a name +116% in a year and pinned at all-time highs into a no-cut/hike-bias regime that compresses every rich multiple.
  • First-principles: At $1.98T and 15× sales, you're paying a quality premium for a business that prints cash and gates the whole industry — sound to own, but only sound to initiate on weakness, not at the high.

Entry levels the agent derives

Re-derived from this run's tape (50DMA ≈ $406, 200DMA ≈ $335):

  • Buy-below: ~$405 (a real ~12% pullback toward the 50DMA) — a quality add on a cohort-wide dip.
  • Fair: ~$435–445.
  • Stretch / do-not-chase above: ~$470 without a genuinely new catalyst (e.g. a 2nm yield/pricing surprise or a CoWoS capacity step-change). Buying at $462 at the ATH violates macro-cohort-confirmation and the principles' "don't BUY above entry without a fresh catalyst" rule.
  • Entry-shape base rate (research/patterns.py): active preset is extended_run (14 instances) — +63d win 69.2%, avg +8.1%, but worst drawdown −30.7%. Decent forward odds for this name even when extended, which keeps HOLD (not AVOID) honest — but the fat left tail says any entry should be on a dip, not at-market at the high.

Macro fit

All three kill-switches clear (ai-infra-thesis-kill-switches): capex +~77% YoY (~$700–725B 2026), GPU pricing firm and short, credit tight — secular thesis intact, and TSM is the cleanest expression of it. But the Opportunity-cost lens verdict at today's price is unfavorable: macro (2026-06-19) says cuts are off the table with a HIKE tail and the market is extended (~22.5× fwd), so the stance is hold cash and "re-engage only on real dips or fresh single-name catalysts." TSM has neither a dip nor a 7–14-day catalyst right now. It is higher quality and lower P/S-risk than the extreme-P/S post-deal cohort (NBIS ~100×, IREN ~25× P/S), so on a pullback it ranks near the top of the field — but the marginal $100 today is better held than spent at the 52-week high.

Falsification

  • 30-day: Q2 print 2026-07-16 — does the >30% FY guide hold/raise, GM stay >65%, and does the stock sell the spend like the hyperscalers did (a sentiment tell)? A post-print dip toward $405–435 would flip this to BUY.
  • 60-day: CoWoS capacity ramp (75k→125–130k wpm target) and 2nm yield/pricing — a step-change either way moves fair value.
  • 90-day: Taiwan geopolitical / energy tape (Iran-war energy spike, Strait of Hormuz) and the rate path — a sustained de-rate of the leadership cohort is the dip to buy; a Taiwan tail event is the thesis-breaker that would flip toward AVOID.

AMBAHOLDfetched38›

Research dossier: AMBA — Ambarella, Inc.

Researched: 2026-06-23 · Verdict: HOLD · Conviction: 0.58 · Opportunity score: 38

Next earnings: 2026-08-26

Business

Fabless designer of edge-AI vision SoCs — low-power inference chips that run computer-vision/AI on the device instead of in the data center. End markets: automotive (ADAS/cameras, the record-revenue lane), physical security / video surveillance, IoT, robotics/drones, and portable/action cameras. Customers are OEMs; ~61% of Q1 revenue flowed through WT Microelectronics (Taiwan fulfillment partner shipping to Asian OEMs), so channel concentration is real. >46M edge-AI SoCs shipped lifetime. Revenue durability rests on the secular "AI moves to the edge" thesis plus multi-year design wins (automotive + the new Hanwha LTA) that lock in multi-generational sockets.

Fresh thesis — is this a good buy now?

A fresh catalyst did land since the 06-16 dossier — but it's negative. Around June 16–17 the US ITC reaffirmed GoPro's design-patent claim and issued a Limited Exclusion Order blocking imports of Insta360's infringing action cameras. Insta360 is an AMBA portable-video customer, so the ruling lit up AMBA's customer-concentration risk: the stock fell ~18% over the week and is down another ~7.3% today, to $65.51 (ret_20d now −25.1%, vs −14.9% on 06-16). Crucially, Oppenheimer flagged no expected FY27 revenue impact — the banned Insta360 SKUs are already obsolete in the US. So this is a sentiment/concentration-fear repricing, not a quantified revenue cut. Per extended-with-vs-without-catalyst: the only fresh fact is a negative one that the sell-side says doesn't dent the model — that lowers the price without improving (or breaking) the entry case. And per macro-cohort-confirmation, this is name-specific bad news, not a cohort-wide dip — exactly the kind of weakness to be cautious buying into. It's cheaper than three weeks ago, but still above my fair value and still not at a trigger.

Valuation + position vs. range

  • Price $65.51; market cap $2.87B; ~43.87M shares; TTM revenue $405.19M → P/S ≈ 7.1×.
  • GAAP TTM net loss −$69.63M; Q1 GAAP gross margin 58.4% (down from 60.0% YoY). Forward P/E ~79× implies only thin non-GAAP forward profitability — a growth-multiple, not an earnings story.
  • 52-week range $48.30–$96.69; at $65.51 it sits ~32% off the high, ~36% off the low, below both 50-DMA (−8.0%) and 200-DMA (−9.0%).

My fair-value read: ~7× sales for a name whose growth decelerated from +37% (FY26) to +17% (Q1 FY27), is still GAAP-unprofitable, and now carries a fresh customer-concentration overhang is fair-to-slightly-rich, not mispriced low. On ~$450M FY27E revenue, 6× sales ≈ $61; the edge-AI premium defends the mid-$60s but doesn't make it a bargain. Hanwha's ">$800M over >10 years" is a ceiling (~$80M/yr if fully realized, ramping, non-committed), not yet in the run-rate. Sell-side fair-value reads bracket wide (bull ~$97, average PT $93.75; a bear case assumes 12.1% growth and no profit by 2029) — the dispersion itself says the terminal value isn't settled.

Post-deal / repriced check

Per Wait-for-deal thesis: AMBA is post-catalyst on the positive deal (Hanwha LTA, May 28) — and that deal never produced a sustained re-rate; the stock is now well below its pre-announcement level. The newer move is the opposite of a deal: a legal/concentration overhang. So buying here is neither pre-deal optionality nor confirmed-repricing momentum — it's a name drifting down on a negative print the sell-side says is immaterial to FY27. The entry edge from the good deal is consumed; the bad news has created a lower price but not a clean dip (no cohort cover, no fundamental upgrade).

Council (one line each, no hedging)

  • Bull: The only pure-play public edge-AI inference vendor with 46M+ SoCs shipped, record auto revenue, a 10-year Hanwha anchor, and a $50M buyback — and the latest −25% is a non-fundamental legal scare (Oppenheimer: no FY27 hit), so you're buying a secular name ~32% off its high on noise.
  • Bear: Perennially "about to inflect" — $405M revenue and GAAP losses after a decade; growth decelerating +37%→+17%; 61% of revenue through one distributor; a fresh ITC ruling just exposed customer-concentration risk; 7× sales into a hawkish Fed.
  • First-principles: A $2.87B cap for a loss-making ~$400M-revenue niche SoC designer only pencils if you underwrite multi-year edge-AI acceleration — plausible, but at $65.51 it's not discounted enough to be a bargain, and the marginal buyer needs a real trigger.

Entry levels the agent derives

Re-derived this run, sanity-checked against the weak dip base rate:

  • Buy-below: ~$55 (≈5× sales, ~16% under spot, near the consolidation shelf above the $48 low) — and trigger-gated, not at-market: dip-buying this name wins only 31% at +21d / 45% at +63d, avg drawdown −18.9% (worst −54.3%).
  • Fair: ~$60–63.
  • Stretch / do-not-chase-above: ~$67. Above spot there is no entry — both the positive catalyst (Hanwha) faded and the negative one (ITC) is fresh.
  • Deep-value floor reference: 52-week low $48.30.

Macro fit

Kill-switches all clear (capex, GPU pricing, credit) — secular AI thesis intact, single-name weakness is noise/opportunity, not a break. BUT the regime is hostile to paying up here: the June 17 FOMC came in hawkish (held 3.50–3.75%, dot plot flipped to a hike bias, no 2026 cuts, hike eyed as early as October) — the cost-of-capital relief a 7× P/S name needs to defend its multiple is not coming. Market extended (fwd P/E ~22.4). On the Opportunity-cost lens, AMBA is a fine watch but not the best use of the marginal $100 today: the only fresh catalyst is negative, the dip is name-specific (no cohort cover), the base rate is weak, and it still trades above my fair value with a hawkish Fed on the multiple. It ranks mid-field — hold cash, wait for the $55 trigger or a clean fundamental re-acceleration.

Falsification

  • 30-day: Does it base in the low-$60s or roll toward $55/$48 as the ITC overhang and hawkish dots weigh on high-P/S names? A break under $55 opens the trigger-gated buy zone; a clean reclaim of the 50/200-DMA on volume upgrades the setup.
  • 60-day: Q2 FY2027 print (~Aug 26) — revenue beat the ~$108M guide mid, and does GAAP gross margin stop slipping below 58%?   • 90-day: Early evidence the Hanwha LTA converts to booked, dated orders (vs. "potential") and auto revenue hits the promised record — that moves the deal from ceiling to run-rate and justifies a higher multiple. A major automotive design-win loss or Nvidia Jetson displacement is the AVOID trigger.

Researched: 2026-06-20 · Verdict: HOLD · Conviction: 0.6 · Opportunity score: 38

Business

Arista designs high-performance data-center Ethernet switches (now 800G, with a 1.6T

generation launching) running a single Linux-based network OS (EOS) and managed by CloudVision.

The product is "the back-end fabric that wires GPUs together." Who pays them: the Cloud

Titans — Microsoft and Meta are each ~15–20% of revenue (combined >35%), with the rest split

across other hyperscalers, neoclouds, and enterprise/campus. Why the revenue durably continues:

AI clusters are networking-bound — every GPU node needs a switch port, and the merchant-silicon

+ open-Ethernet stack is winning back-end share from NVIDIA's InfiniBand as clusters scale to

100k+ GPUs. This is a picks-and-shovels AI-infra name at the compute-interconnect layer, not a

land/power play.

operating cash flow $1.69B. Management raised FY26 to ~27.7% growth (~$11.5B) and lifted the

2026 AI-networking target to $3.5B. On June 9 they launched the 7060XE7 1.6T portfolio (128×800G

ports, AMD-NIC ecosystem ties) — a real product cadence catalyst, but an incremental one, not a

thesis-changing event. The stock has already run +20.7% in 20 days and sits 5.6% off its 52-week

high. The May print was the repricing catalyst; six weeks later it is largely digested. Under

extended-with-vs-without-catalyst, the honest read is "extended, mostly-without a fresh

catalyst" — the 1.6T launch is continuation, not new thesis. So this is a quality compounder I

want to own on a dip, not chase at highs.
