Research dossier: AVAV — AeroVironment, Inc.

Researched: 2026-06-22 · Verdict: HOLD · Conviction: 0.55 · Opportunity score: 38

Next earnings: 2026-06-29

Business

Pure-play tactical unmanned systems: small UAS (Raven, Puma), loitering munitions (Switchblade)

and, since the 2025 all-stock acquisition of BlueHalo, space/SATCOM ground systems, directed

energy and counter-UAS. Customers are the US DoD, allied militaries and international partners;

revenue is program-of-record and firm-order driven (drones + munitions are now consumable,

attritable wartime spend, not just durable platforms). Why it durably continues: structural

shift in defense procurement toward cheap mass-producible autonomous systems (Ukraine lesson),

plus a record $3.5B in awards with ~93% of FY26 revenue guidance backed by firm orders — rare

visibility. The BlueHalo bolt-on broadened the addressable base but is lower-margin and unproven

as an integrated unit.

Fresh thesis — is this a good buy now?

A genuinely beaten-down name (-64% from its 52w high, sitting AT the low ~$151) — the opposite

setup from the extended post-deal AI-infra cohort. The dip is real and the structural demand

story is intact, so this is worth watching. But it is not a buy today for one decisive

reason: it has a binary print in 7 days (June 29) plus an Investor Day July 8, on a name that

just (a) cut guidance to a GAAP loss, (b) lost its largest program of record (SCAR), and (c) has

a securities class-action overhang. Buying at-market the week before that event is a coin-flip,

not edge. The disciplined action is to let the print + Investor Day clear and buy on

confirmation that the ex-SCAR backlog holds and BlueHalo margins stabilize — not to catch the

knife now.

Valuation + position vs. range

  • Price ~$151.33; market cap $7.62B; ~50.4M shares. 52w range $150.36–$417.86 → at the floor.
  • TTM revenue $1.61B (+117% YoY, inflated by BlueHalo consolidation, not organic). FY26 guide

$1.85–2.0B. GAAP loss: TTM net -$224M / EPS -$5.15; FY26 guided loss -$201–218M

(-$4.10 to -$4.44). Forward P/E 37.9 — the Street is modeling a return to profitability in

FY27, which is the whole bet.

  • ~3.8x forward sales (mcap / ~$2.0B). For a high-growth defense name with record backlog that

is not extreme — defense peers run ~2–5x. So it's no longer expensive; it's de-rated to

fair-ish. My fair value ~$170–185 (≈4.3–4.6x forward sales, or ~28x a normalized FY28 EPS of

~$5–6 once BlueHalo margins season). At $151 the downside is "value trap / further backlog

erosion," not "still bubble-priced."

Post-deal / repriced check

Not the classic Wait-for-deal thesis setup — AVAV's "deal" (BlueHalo) already closed and the

integration is the problem, not a pending catalyst. The entry edge here isn't a coming

partnership; it's a possible over-sell. Per macro-cohort-confirmation the dip is

name-specific (SCAR loss + BlueHalo dilution), NOT cohort-wide — drone/defense peers are

catching momentum, not selling off. A name-specific dip on lost-program + guidance-cut news is

exactly the kind the framework warns is a value-trap risk, not a clean buyable cohort dip.

Council (one line each, no hedging)

  • Bull: Essential drone/loitering-munition leader at a 52w low and ~3.8x forward sales, with

a record $3.5B backlog 93% firm — the cheapest entry in years into secular attritable-defense

demand, and the June 29 / July 8 catalysts can reset the narrative up.

  • Bear: It lost its largest program of record, swung to a GAAP loss, bought a lower-margin

dilutive business it can't yet integrate, and carries a class action — you'd be catching a

knife the week before a binary print with the whole sell-side cutting targets.

  • First-principles: A $7.6B defense company doing ~$2B of firm-backed revenue isn't broken,

but it's loss-making and just lost its flagship — fair, not a screaming buy; pay up only on

post-print proof the backlog ex-SCAR holds.

Entry levels the agent derives

  • Buy-below: ~$135 (below the 52w low; ~3.4x forward sales — margin of safety for the

binary/value-trap risk if you buy without waiting for the catalyst).

  • Fair: ~$175.
  • Stretch: ~$160 — and ONLY post-June-29/Investor-Day confirmation that ex-SCAR backlog

holds and BlueHalo margin trajectory improves. Do not pay up ahead of the print.

  • Entry is catalyst-gated, not a static at-market level. Base rates support patience: the

dip_buyer preset on AVAV's own history wins just 55% at +21/+42/+63d (avg +4.1/+7.9/+3.8%)

with avg drawdown -13.3% / worst -46.5% — a modest edge that demands a trigger, not at-market.

Macro fit

Not an AI-infra name, so the three kill-switches are tangential — the Fed multiple-compression

headwind also matters less for a name already de-rated 64% and trading ~3.8x forward sales.

Defense/attritable-systems demand is a structural tailwind (macro neutral-to-supportive). On the

Opportunity-cost lens: more interesting than a generic extended post-deal AI name (real

value emerging + a dated catalyst), but the correct action today is wait-not-buy, so the

marginal-$100 score right now is low-middling. The binary print one week out is the dominant

fact — hold cash, set an alert, re-decide on the June 29 numbers. (No user deep-dive exists for

AVAV in knowledge/user-deep-dives/, so this is a fully independent view.)

Falsification

  • 30-day (June 29 print + July 8 Investor Day): Does FY26/FY27 guidance confirm the backlog

holds ex-SCAR? Are BlueHalo gross margins stabilizing/improving? A clean print with

reaffirmed organic growth flips this toward BUY (post-confirmation entry ≤ ~$160). A second

guidance cut or more backlog erosion → AVOID.

  • 60-day: SCAR recompete outcome — does AVAV/BlueHalo retain any of the $1–1.4B, or is it a

full loss? Class-action developments.

  • 90-day: First post-print quarter — organic (ex-acquisition) revenue growth and a credible

path back to GAAP profitability in FY27. If the loss persists with no margin inflection, the

"return to profitability" bet underpinning the 37.9x forward P/E is broken → AVOID.

BTDRHOLDfetched38›

Research dossier: BTDR — Bitdeer Technologies Group

Researched: 2026-06-20 · Verdict: HOLD · Conviction: 0.5 · Opportunity score: 38

Next earnings: 2026-08-12

Business

Bitdeer Technologies Group (NASDAQ: BTDR) is a Bitcoin miner executing a funded pivot into AI/HPC data-center infrastructure. Founded by Jihan Wu (Bitmain co-founder). Three revenue legs: (1) self-mining BTC — still the dominant engine (70.2 EH/s self-mining hashrate, 83.1 EH/s under management as of May 2026); (2) mining hosting / cloud hashrate; (3) AI Cloud — early GPU colocation + cloud (ARR ~$69M at 90% utilization, 4,248 GPUs deployed). It designs its own ASICs (SEALMINER line; A4 at 9.45 J/T, plus the DL1 Hydro Scrypt rig) for internal use and sale — the in-house silicon is a genuine cost edge over miners buying third-party rigs.

The durable differentiator is power-advantaged sites inside a stated ~3GW global power portfolio: Norway (Tydal — Phase 1 50MW + Phase 2 175MW, hydro, Nvidia Vera Rubin reference design, both phases now targeted for Q4-2026 energization) and a 570MW Clarington, Ohio site (power under contract with AEP). Management has explicitly reframed the story: colocation, not BTC appreciation, is the compounding bet, and "executing colocation lease agreements" is named top priority.

Why AI-colocation revenue would durably continue: cheap Norwegian hydro + EU data-sovereignty pull create real demand for EU-based AI compute. The open question is unchanged across three dives now — the anchor customer is still unsigned, so pricing power and customer quality remain unconfirmed.

Fresh thesis — is this a good buy now?

**Still pre-customer, and the net of the last 7 days is mixed-to-slightly-negative. The Tydal tenant is "in advanced negotiations" — but that exact phrase appeared in both the May and June updates, so the headline fact hasn't actually advanced. Meanwhile Clarington just picked up a fresh negative (litigation delay). Neither the "fresh-catalyst" nor the "real-dip" gate is open.**

This is a textbook Wait-for-deal thesis structure: power-rich, Nvidia-aligned sites ahead of a signed hyperscaler/AI-lab anchor. If a named customer signs the Tydal lease this reprices hard (the BE-post-Oracle "sustained repricing" pattern is the analog). Applying extended-with-vs-without-catalyst's operational test — what fact about the business changed in the last 7–14 days? — the honest answer:

  • Tydal: still "advanced negotiations," no signature. The language is now a month stale; protracted negotiation without close is a mild yellow flag, not a catalyst.
  • Clarington (NEW, negative): the 570MW Ohio site is delayed by legal proceedings filed by a neighboring company (American Heavy Plate Solutions LLC); no energization timeline given. This narrows the near-term colocation surface to Tydal alone.
  • Production: 921 BTC mined in May (+370% YoY), record hashrate — strong operational color, but mining is the gross-margin-negative leg, so volume isn't the value driver.

So the run (+35% 20d, +118% 60d, +30% vs 200DMA) is momentum without a thesis-confirming fact — the FOMO frame is the correct one. Don't chase.

Fundamentals remain uncomfortable: Q1 gross margin was -20.7% (deteriorated from -5.7% a year ago), Q1 net loss $159.5M, TTM net income -$199M. The company upsized its February convertible to $375M of 5.00% notes due 2032 (larger than the ~$325M I carried last dive) — a real dilutive/cash-cost overhang. The Q1 revenue beat ($188.9M, +169% YoY) is top-line growth, not an earnings inflection; profitability is still negative at the gross line.

Valuation + position vs. range

Price: $17.93 · 52-week range: ~$6.92 – ~$27.7 · Position in range: ~65% of high (35% off the high; +159% off the low)

  • Market cap: $4.36B (243.31M shares — confirmed via stockanalysis.com).
  • TTM revenue: $739M (+146% YoY). P/S ≈ 5.9×. For a gross-margin-negative mining base this is the wrong lens and not cheap — you're paying a forward AI-colocation multiple on a business that doesn't yet earn it.
  • Debt: ~$375M 5.00% convertibles (2032) + prior tranches; interest cost and dilution overhang are a headwind, not noise.
  • Sell-side: consensus "Strong Buy," avg PT ~$21.52 (~+20%) — note the Street is already leaning bullish, which tempers the contrarian edge.

AI-Cloud prize (unchanged math): Tydal at full 225MW (50+175), even at conservative colocation rates, could add ~$150–220M ARR at full occupancy — multiples of the current ~$69M AI-Cloud ARR. That is the entire bull case, and it is contingent on a customer that does not yet exist.

Fair-value bands (re-derived this run):

  • Buy-below: $13–14.5 — where pre-deal optionality is genuinely underpriced vs. execution risk, the gross-margin-negative base, the Clarington delay, and the now-larger convert.
  • Fair (no deal): $16–19 — roughly where it trades; pivot narrative priced, customer not.
  • Stretch (customer signed): $26–34 — a named Tydal anchor; thesis confirmed, extended-with-catalyst logic fires.

At $17.93 the stock sits mid-"fair" band — same level as a week ago, no new good news, one new bad fact (Clarington).

Post-deal / repriced check

Structurally pre-deal. Tydal is in flight (Q4-2026 energization target, Nvidia alignment) but the customer is unsigned; the entry edge has not been consumed. The awkward zone persists: too much already priced for a clean value entry, not yet confirmed enough to buy on extended-with-catalyst logic. The pre-deal edge belonged at the lows (~$6.92); $17.93 is a mid-thesis price where you pay for optionality that hasn't crystallized. If a Tydal anchor signs, the extended-with-catalyst clause flips this to a BUY even above $20 — re-derive entry from the new thesis at that point.

Council (one line each, no hedging)

  • Bull: Power-advantaged Norway site at Q4-2026 energization, in-house SEALMINER silicon, Nvidia Vera Rubin alignment, and an explicit "advanced negotiations" tenant put Bitdeer on the short list of credible-now EU AI-colocation builders — one signed lease reprices this 50–80%.
  • Bear: A gross-margin-negative (-20.7%) miner subsidizing an expensive AI build on $375M of converts, with no signed colocation customer, a Clarington site now stuck in litigation, and 243M+ shares — if BTC rolls over or Tydal energizes empty, it burns cash and dilutes.
  • First-principles: Real assets and a real energy moat priced at ~6× sales of a loss-making base; worth owning only if you're within reach of the customer catalyst — otherwise you're financing someone else's option.

Entry levels the agent derives

At $17.93: no entry here. Wait for (a) a dip into $13–14.5 — ideally a cohort-confirmed dip per macro-cohort-confirmation, not a name-specific crack — or (b) a named colocation customer.

Macro fit

Kill-switches: All three NOT tripped (2026 hyperscaler capex tracking ~$700–750B, +77% YoY and still being raised; B200 pricing/lead-times not compressing; HY OAS ~275bp). Secular AI-infra tailwind intact — single-name moves are noise or opportunity, not thesis breaks.

Regime / rates: Per the 2026-06-20 macro read, the market is extended and the Fed is a multiple-compression headwind, not a tailwind — post-FOMC (Warsh debut, 12-0 hold at 3.50–3.75%), median 2026 dot 3.8%, next move leaning HIKE, zero cuts priced; the Iran/Hormuz inflation tail keeps the Fed boxed. Stance is hold cash, re-engage only on real dips or fresh single-name catalysts. BTDR is triple-beta here — BTC exposure + an AI multiple + high debt — exactly the profile a no-cuts/hawkish regime punishes. A strong reason not to reach for it.

Entry-shape base rates (research/patterns.py, 1053 bars): Active preset is extended_run (29 instances): +21d win 60.7% / avg +33.5%; +63d win 59.3% / avg +31.2% — momentum has persisted in this name, which tempers a reflexive "extended, don't chase." But avg max drawdown -23.3% (worst -77.8%): a C12 - Lottery Stocks payoff — high win-rate with a fat left tail. The dip_buyer preset wins only 26–35% (+63d avg -20.4%) — so buying this pullback at-market (no catalyst, no cohort dip) is the wrong side of the name's own history. The base rate doesn't veto patience; it says if a catalyst does fire, paying up has historically worked.

Opportunity-cost rank: Against a field where the named AI-infra watchlist is fully post-deal/repriced, BTDR's Tydal optionality is one of the cleaner pre-deal structures still visible. But gross-margin-negative base + $375M converts + the new Clarington legal delay + triple-beta into a no-cuts Fed make it a worse use of the marginal $100 right now than power-pure plays or confirmed-revenue names. Opportunity score 38/100 — a real, still-uncrystallized setup, nudged down from 40 last week on the Clarington litigation and the staleness of the "advanced negotiations" language; no fresh catalyst keeps it mid-pack, not a standout.

Falsification

30-day: Named Tydal colocation customer signs? → flip to BUY, re-derive entry from the new thesis. None → thesis unchanged, stay patient. Also watch for a cohort-wide high-P/S de-rate (hawkish-Fed driven) that opens the $13–14.5 buy-below as a buyable dip per macro-cohort-confirmation.

60-day: Q2 2026 earnings (~Aug 12): does AI-Cloud ARR break meaningfully above $69M, and does gross margin start recovering off -20.7%? Continued gross loss without ARR acceleration pushes toward AVOID.

90-day: Tydal Q4-2026 energization progress; Clarington litigation resolution / timeline. Tydal slippage or an unresolved Clarington suit → re-rate.

Flip-to-AVOID triggers:

  • BTC sustained below ~$60K (mining economics collapse, financing pressure escalates).
  • Tydal energizes (Q4-2026) with no customer signed.
  • Another discounted equity / convert offering (fresh dilution — C14 - Share Issuance and Dilution Drag).
  • AI-Cloud ARR stalls and gross margin stays deeply negative through Q2.

Flip-to-BUY triggers:

  • Named hyperscaler/AI-lab signs the Tydal colocation lease (the primary catalyst).
  • Gross margin inflects positive (or sharply less negative) in Q2 alongside ARR growth.
  • Cohort-confirmed pullback into $13–14.5 with thesis intact.

Research dossier: CLSK — CleanSpark, Inc.

Researched: 2026-06-23 · Verdict: HOLD · Conviction: 0.6 · Opportunity score: 38

Next earnings: 2026-08-06 (Q3 FY2026, quarter ending 2026-06-30; FY ends Sept 30, Q2 reported 2026-05-11)

Business

Vertically-integrated Bitcoin miner pivoting to AI/HPC hosting. Today's cash engine is BTC

mining: ~50 EH/s, ~671 BTC mined in May (≈21.7/day), ~13,470 BTC held plus ~$260M cash;

revenue is block subsidy + fees, post-halving margin-compressed. Operations span Georgia

(Sandersville 250 MW flagship), Mississippi, and Texas/ERCOT campuses. The re-rate leg is

AI/HPC hosting: CLSK controls ~1.8 GW of power/land (using only ~808 MW for mining), 585 MW

ERCOT-approved, a 300 MW Brazoria County (Houston) acquisition, and the 250 MW Sandersville site

being prepared for a hyperscale tenant. Average power cost ~5.2¢/kWh — among the sector's lowest,

a genuine cost moat for both mining and hosting. The durable-revenue split is unchanged: mining

revenue is BTC-price/difficulty-dependent; hosting revenue would be contracted, IG-counterparty,

long-duration — if the lease signs.

Fresh thesis — is this a good buy now?

The bull case is still a re-rate from "miner" multiple to "power/neocloud" multiple when the

power bank is leased to a hyperscaler — and unlike the named AI-infra cohort (IREN/NBIS/CRWV, all

post-deal and repriced), CLSK's defining catalyst is still ahead. Per [[Wait-for-deal

thesis]] pre-deal is the one place the entry edge can still live. This run's new fact: the

counterparty is now publicly reported to be Meta for ~250 MW at Sandersville. That sharpens

the story — Meta is a genuinely power-short, AAA-credit hyperscaler — but the deal is explicitly

still in negotiation/exploration, neither signed nor terminated. The price has already run

+84% in 60 days / +8% in 20 days pricing the anticipation. Per

extended-with-vs-without-catalyst, paying up is justified only when a fresh thesis-confirming

catalyst just landed; "talks are now reported to be with Meta" is a sharper rumor, not a signed

lease. Buying +84% at-market is still chasing anticipation, not repricing a confirmation.

Valuation + position vs. range

(~$13.1)** — extended on both. Price essentially flat vs. the 2026-06-16 dossier.

overwhelmingly non-cash BTC fair-value revaluation (ASU 2023-08 marks BTC through the

P&L); Q2 FY26 was rev $136.4M (−24.9% YoY, missed $145M consensus), GAAP loss $1.52/sh (vs

  • Fair-value frame: it traded ~$9–10 before the AI narrative ran (≈ miner-only value);

Half-repriced. On the BTC-miner identity it is fully priced (rich, given the leverage).

entry**.

NTAPHOLDfetched38›

Research dossier: NTAP — NetApp, Inc.

Researched: 2026-06-30 · Verdict: HOLD · Conviction: 0.58 · Opportunity score: 38

Next earnings: 2026-08-26

Business

Enterprise data-storage and data-management. Two engines: (1) Hybrid Cloud / on-prem — the

ONTAP OS on all-flash arrays (AFA), the durable core; AFA hit a record **$1.2B in Q4 FY26 (+18%

YoY), the real growth driver inside an otherwise ~5%-growth total business. (2) Public Cloud** —

first-party storage on AWS/Azure/Google (Azure NetApp Files, Google Cloud NetApp Volumes, FSx for

ONTAP). Who pays them: large enterprises and the hyperscalers who resell NetApp's file/storage as a

first-party service. Why it durably continues: data gravity + high ONTAP switching costs — once an

org's unstructured estate, compliance, backup and DR live on ONTAP, migrating is expensive, so the

install base renews and expands. The AI angle is real but second-order: NetApp frames storage as the

layer that activates unstructured data for AI pipelines (data prep, governance, sovereign/hybrid

namespaces) — Kurian's line is "the challenge is not compute, it's activating large volumes of

unstructured data." ~1,100 AI/data-prep wins in FY26 (up ~3× from ~400); deeper Google Cloud + a

top-five Neo Cloud expansion cited in Q4.

Fresh thesis — is this a good buy now?

No — same conclusion as the prior run, now with the pullback maturing. The re-rating catalyst was the

2026-05-28/29 Q4 FY2026 print: revenue $1.95B (+13% YoY, beat $1.87B), non-GAAP EPS $2.43 (+26%

YoY, beat $2.27), an FY27 guide above Street (rev $7.325–7.575B; **non-GAAP EPS $8.70–9.00 vs

~$8.25 consensus) and a $1B buyback. The stock gapped +35% on 2026-05-29**, broke its 26-year-old

ATH, ran to a 52-week high of $192.83, and has since faded to $154.76 (now ~19.7% off the

high). Per extended-with-vs-without-catalyst this is extended-WITH-catalyst = repricing, so

the honest question is "is the new thesis worth owning at the new price?" My answer: it was a real

quality re-rate, but the easy money was the +35% gap. At ~$155 the entry edge is consumed, and the

new wrinkle this run is that the 20-day return has flipped hard negative (-13.9%) — the post-print

froth is being worked off, but the stock is still **+49.7%/60d, +12% above the 50DMA and +33% above

the 200DMA**. No fresh (last 7–14 day) thesis-confirming fact has landed: the June items

(StorageGRID 12.1 + federated global namespace, CGI/Keystone block-storage alliance, the Cisco

defense-in-depth/AI validated designs) are all incremental product/partner news, not a second

catalyst that would justify paying up. Good name, mediocre entry timing → HOLD.

Valuation + position vs. range

  • Price $154.76 (Jun 30, fetched); 52w range $93.69–$192.83 (~19.7% off high, +65% above low).
  • Forward P/E ~17.4× on the ~$8.85 FY27 EPS midpoint; trailing 24.37×; ~4.4× sales; mkt

cap $30.32B; 195.92M shares; div $0.52/qtr (ex-date Jul 10), ~1.34% yield + ongoing $1B

buyback (share count grinding down).

  • TTM revenue $6.93B (+~5%), net income $1.28B, EPS TTM $6.35. FY27 guided ~8% rev / ~9%

EPS growth.

  • Context: NTAP traded most of 2024–25 around 13–15× forward; the AI-attach narrative re-rated it

to ~17–18×. The multiple expansion — not the (modest) growth — is what moved the stock.

  • My fair-value read: for a mid-single-digit grower accelerating to ~8–9% with real AI

optionality and 25%+ FCF margins, ~17–19× forward is fair, not cheap → fair zone $150–170. At

~$155 it's roughly fairly valued; most of the AI-attach optionality is priced. Its forward

multiple sits below the market (~20–22× fwd), so it carries less multiple-compression risk than

the extreme-P/S AI cohort — a relative-quality plus, but at 8–9% growth a sub-market multiple is

deserved, not a discount to exploit. Heavily extended on the tape (50DMA ≈ $138, 200DMA ≈ $116).

Post-deal / repriced check

Per Wait-for-deal thesis: the catalyst (earnings beat + FY27 raise + Google/Neo-Cloud deepening +

buyback) has fired and the multiple has repriced from ~14× to ~17.4× forward. The pre-catalyst

"cheap storage compounder at 13×" entry case is consumed. What remains is a fully-priced quality

name, not an entry edge. Analyst behavior confirms the repricing is digested: a wave of PT hikes after

the print (TD Cowen $200 Buy, BofA $180 Neutral, Susquehanna $185, JPM/Wedbush ~$150) but **most kept

Neutral/Hold** — they chased the price with targets, not asymmetric upside; consensus ~$171.75 is only

~11% above spot.

Refresh vs the 2026-06-23 run (HOLD / 0.58 / 38, at $158): a week later the verdict is unchanged.

Price is essentially flat (~$158 → ~$155), fwd P/E ~17.7 → ~17.4, and the stock is ~20% off the high.

The one substantive change is that 20-day momentum has rolled from +13.3% to -13.9% — the move is

now visibly digesting rather than grinding higher. That is marginally constructive for a future entry

but still leaves price ~$15 above my buy-below; I confirm the prior HOLD and hold conviction/score

flat.

Council (one line each, no hedging)

  • Bull: AI is converting NetApp from a no-growth storage utility into a data-infrastructure attach

play — AFA +18% and AI wins ~3× say the mix shift is real and the re-rate has room.

  • Bear: It's still a 5–8% grower in a competitive market (Dell, Pure, VAST, cloud-native storage);

it did a decade of multiple expansion in three weeks and now must grow into 17–18×.

  • First-principles: ~$30B for ~$1.3B net income growing high-single-digits plus a 1.3% yield and

buybacks at ~17.4× forward is fairly priced from scratch — reasonable to own, not a compelling use

of new capital at today's price.

Entry levels the agent derives

Re-derived from this run's FY27 EPS midpoint ($8.85) and the current tape (no inherited anchor):

  • Buy-below: ~$140 (≈15.8× forward — into the 50DMA ≈ $138; a real margin of safety / genuine

give-back of post-print froth, not at-market).

  • Fair: ~$168 (≈19× forward; aligns with ~$171.75 consensus — fully reflects the AI-attach

optionality).

  • Stretch: ~$185 (≈21× — pay up only on a second confirming catalyst, e.g. Q1 FY27 showing AI

wins translating into accelerating product revenue, or a named hyperscaler/Neo-Cloud offtake).

Sanity check vs base rates (research/patterns.py): NTAP's extended_run preset is historically

strong (+63d win 100%, avg +15.7%) but n=9, far too small to lean on; the dip_buyer preset is

the larger, weaker sample (+63d 48%, avg +1.8%, n=25, avg drawdown -9.6%). With 20d momentum now

negative the tape is sliding toward the dip-buyer regime — whose 48% +63d win rate says a give-back

purchase here is a coin-flip, reinforcing "wait for the real dip to ~$140, don't buy at-market."

Macro fit

Kill-switches all clear (capex accelerating ~$725B 2026 / +77% YoY, GPU pricing firm/sold out, HY OAS

278bp) per macro.md 2026-06-30 — secular AI-infra thesis intact, so NTAP's AI-data tailwind is real.

The binding constraint is the Fed (held 3.50–3.75%, hawkish dots with a hike tail, no 2026 cuts)

into an extended market (fwd P/E ~20–22), which demands a real dip OR a fresh catalyst before

adding. NTAP's catalyst is fresh-ish but already paid for, and the dip — while now underway (-13.9%/

20d) — hasn't reached buy-below. Mitigant: at ~17.4× forward / ~4.4× sales it is **not an extreme-P/S

name**, so far less exposed to dot-plot multiple compression than NBIS/IREN — a relative-quality plus.

On the Opportunity-cost lens: a fully-priced, post-repricing 8% grower is a middling use of the

marginal $100 versus names still below fair value or genuinely pre-catalyst. Hence opportunity score

38 — not lower (quality + real AI attach + a deepening give-back), not higher (no entry edge left,

ran +50%/60d, extended above both DMAs).

Falsification

  • 30-day: a clean pullback toward $140 or below on cohort-wide (not name-specific-bad-news)

weakness → flips toward BUY (entry edge re-opens; confirm cohort-wide per macro-cohort-confirmation).

A break back above $193 on volume = stretch-only, keep HOLD.

  • 60-day (Q1 FY27 print, ~2026-08-26): AI/data-prep wins translating into *accelerating product

revenue* + a guide raise → confirms the re-rate, supports paying ~20×; AFA growth decelerating or AI

wins flat → the ~17–18× multiple is unearned, flips toward AVOID.

  • 90-day: hyperscaler Q2 capex (late July) decelerating would slow downstream enterprise AI

pipelines (NTAP's AI wins); competitive share loss to Pure/Dell or cloud-native storage cannibalizing

first-party cloud → structural bear case, AVOID.

RIOTHOLDfetched38›

Research dossier: RIOT — Riot Platforms, Inc.

Researched: 2026-06-22 · Verdict: HOLD · Conviction: 0.45 · Opportunity score: 38

Next earnings: 2026-07-30

Business

Riot is a large-scale Bitcoin miner (Rockdale TX, Corsicana TX) now actively pivoting into

AI/HPC data-center hosting. The core competency is the same in both businesses: securing

large blocks of cheap, contracted power at scale. Q1 2026 was the inflection — the first

revenue-generating data-center quarter ($33.2M of $167.2M total rev, ~20%), anchored by an

AMD hosting deal that grew to 50MW contracted critical-IT (option path to 200MW). Mining still

pays the bills: 1,473 BTC produced in Q1 (−4% YoY), hashrate +23–26%, all-in power cost −21%.

The company funds the data-center buildout partly by selling mined BTC (3,778 BTC → $289.5M in

Q1) and still holds ~15,680 BTC (~$1.26B treasury, May 2026). Who pays them: the Bitcoin

network (block rewards/fees) today; AMD and — prospectively — a hyperscaler tenant at Corsicana

tomorrow.

Fresh thesis — is this a good buy now?

The bull setup is a genuine wait-for-deal window that is only partly consumed: the AMD anchor

is signed (proves Riot can lease and energize HPC capacity), but the value-defining catalyst —

a hyperscaler lease against Corsicana's 1GW of approved power — is still unsigned. That is

the rare case where a real, identifiable forward catalyst remains live. The problem is the

price: RIOT is +88–114% YTD, +89% in 60 trading days, sitting 5.5% off its 52-week high, with

the sell side (KBW $37, Clear Street $38, Jefferies $37) already openly modeling the Corsicana

lease. The classic wait-for-deal edge is buying a *credible-now counterparty before the crowd

prices the deal in*. Here the crowd has already priced much of it. Per

extended-with-vs-without-catalyst: what changed in the last 7–14 days? Analyst PT raises and

anticipation — not a freshly signed thesis-confirming deal. That makes the recent run closer

to "extended without (a fresh) catalyst" than to repricing. Buying anticipation at the high is

exactly what the lens says to wait on.

Valuation + position vs. range

  • Market cap ~$10.8B on ~$653M TTM revenue → P/S ~16.6x. Rich, though below the extreme cohort

(NBIS ~83x, IREN ~25x). But the comparison flatters RIOT: ~80% of revenue is still

BTC-price-levered mining, not contracted HPC cash flow. A still-mostly-miner shouldn't carry a

pure-AI-infra multiple.

  • Net income −$867M (TTM) — BTC-fair-value and depreciation swings dominate; this is not a

clean-FCF story, it is a capital-consuming buildout funded by BTC sales + equity.

  • Position: ~5.5% off the 52-week high; 50DMA ≈ $23, 200DMA ≈ $18. The stock has run a long way

from any normal support. A pullback to the 50DMA (~$23) would be an ordinary −20% breath, well

inside this name's historical drawdown profile.

  • Base rates (patterns.py, extended_run preset, 46 instances): +21d win 51% / +6.1% at +63d,

but avg max drawdown −25%, worst −78%. Entering at this shape is a coin flip with a fat

left tail — entry must be trigger-gated, not at-market.

Post-deal / repriced check

Per Wait-for-deal thesis: partially post-deal. The AMD deal (and the BTC-treasury/mining

narrative) is in the price; the recent run is the market front-running the hyperscaler lease,

not reacting to it. So the asymmetry has compressed: if the Corsicana lease lands, the pop is

partly pre-paid (sell-side already at $37–38, ~30% up); if it slips or disappoints, the

unwind is large given how stretched the chart is. That is the wrong side of the

wait-for-deal payoff — you want to own the unpriced anticipation, not the priced one.

Council (one line each, no hedging)

  • Bull: 1GW of approved Corsicana power + a proven AMD anchor = the credible-now counterparty

a hyperscaler signs next; the lease re-rates the whole campus into contracted cash flow.

  • Bear: It's still a leveraged BTC miner with a −$867M bottom line trading at 16.6x sales near

its 52-week high, and the deal everyone's paying for hasn't been signed — slip it and this gives

back 30–50% fast.

  • First-principles: $10.8B for ~$653M of mostly-volatile mining revenue only makes sense if you

underwrite the HPC optionality — and at the 52-week high you're paying nearly full price for

optionality that is not yet contracted.

Entry levels the agent derives

  • buy-below: ~$22 (back toward the 50DMA / pre-run base) absent a signed hyperscaler lease —

i.e. demand a real dip per the macro overlay.

  • fair: ~$20–23 (10–12x sales on the current mining-heavy mix, crediting partial HPC optionality).
  • stretch / catalyst-override: if a hyperscaler lease at Corsicana/Rockdale is actually signed,

extended-with-vs-without-catalyst fires — re-derive entry from the new thesis and paying up

(even above $30) can be rational, because the price would then reflect a fact that does not

exist today. At $28.63 today you are between fair and stretch, paying for an unsigned deal.

Macro fit

Kill-switches all clear (capex accelerating, GPU pricing firm, credit tight) — secular thesis

intact, so this is a sizing/timing question, not a thesis break. But the regime is extended

(fwd P/E ~22.5) with a hawkish Fed (no 2026 cuts, hike tail), and the high-P/S sizing constraint

is tight. A 16.6x-sales name at its 52-week high with no fresh-in-the-last-fortnight catalyst is

precisely what the overlay says to wait on. On the opportunity-cost lens, RIOT ranks mid-pack:

better optionality than fully-consumed post-deal names (it still has an unsigned catalyst), but

worse entry geometry than anything sitting below fair value. Not the best use of the marginal $100

today — it's a watch-for-the-lease name.

Falsification

  • 30-day: A signed hyperscaler lease at Corsicana/Rockdale (1GW) → flips toward BUY under the

catalyst-override, even at a higher price. Conversely, a pullback to ~$22–23 with thesis intact →

BUY the dip.

  • 60-day: Q2 print (2026-07-30) — data-center revenue trajectory, AMD 50→200MW progression, the

pace of BTC sales / equity issuance funding capex (dilution drag, C14 - Share Issuance and Dilution Drag),

and any energization timeline slip.

  • 90-day: BTC price regime — RIOT's mining engine funds the pivot; a BTC drawdown weakens the

self-funding story and pressures both the treasury mark and the multiple simultaneously.

DGXX-2026-07-02-research-verdict-hold-oppcost-gate›

Decision

HOLD · $0 · entry mark $5.08 · 2026-07-02 (signal ts 2026-07-01T22:08:04Z) · signal 2026-07-01:DGXX:research:buy · confidence 0.4

Reasoning

The research board's fresh dossier (2026-07-01) flips DGXX HOLD -> BUY for the first time in

three cycles: price closed $5.09 on a genuine cohort-red day (NBIS -15%, CRWV/IREN plunging on

Meta Compute + a bearish Bernstein note), through the disciplined $5.25/$5.40 buy-below level.

Both conditions the discipline required — price at/below level AND cohort-wide, not idiosyncratic

— are met. Opportunity score ticked 45 -> 50.

But the opportunity-cost gate is the second dominant test, and it fails here: the cross-sectional

board ranks DGXX #16/119 at opp 50 — below every single name currently held (NVDA 62, META 65,

AMZN 55, FN 65, NXT 60, APP 56). Cash on hand is $6 of $100. The dossier's own suggested size

("$100-200, ~20-40 sh") is scaled for a much larger book than this $100 sandbox and isn't

directly usable; more importantly, funding even a token starter here would require rotating out

of a strictly higher-ranked holding into a strictly lower-ranked, pre-revenue, single-customer-

concentrated micro-cap — exactly the failure the opportunity-cost lens exists to block. A filled

price trigger is necessary but not sufficient; it doesn't override the rank test.

Entry shape

Not applicable — no order this cycle. If the gate clears (rank improves or cash frees from a

trim justified on its own terms), the entry shape from the dossier still holds: 1-2% lottery

starter, market order, sized for total-loss-is-a-shrug given the dip_buyer base rates (n=39,

+63d win 22.2%, avg max drawdown -33%).

What would change my mind

  • Any held name's opp rank drops below ~50 on its own merits (funds a legitimate rotation).
  • Phase 2 project-financing 8-K or a second Tier-1/hyperscale signing (the dossier's own

asymmetric catalyst) — would likely lift DGXX's opp score above the current holdings.

  • Organic cash buildup (a trim elsewhere already justified independently) crossing the ~$1-2

needed for a token starter at this size book.

Note to future self

Mirrored via --note: buy-below trigger is filled and confirmed live, but the opportunity-cost

gate (DGXX #16/119, below every holding, $6 cash) blocked the buy. Revisit if a held name's rank

falls below DGXX's, cash frees up from an independently-justified trim, or the silent July 1

10MW GPUaaS milestone gets confirmed (credibility read). Expires 2026-07-12.
